# Second Serve: Arc 1

Oct 29, 2026 · @Ganesh Krishna

Live doc: https://claude.ai/artifact/XR47XChbme3QjgaTLAuPAL
Story bible: https://claude.ai/artifact/CzEHV4vyfey9WUYExLRVrK
Arc 2 chapters: https://claude.ai/artifact/Vb8jKuNfrZmwMYVo927Uwv

## Chapter 1: Ball to the face

Kiyose Sho had 1 rule for club recruitment week, and it was to keep walking.

It was the 1st Monday of it, and Karasuno's corridors were full of people who wanted his name. A 2nd year with a trumpet case stood at the top of the stairs saying "Brass band, brass band" to every 1st year who passed. The calligraphy club had a table by the shoe lockers with a brush and an ink stone on it and a sign that said TRY IT! in very good handwriting. A boy in full kendo armor bowed at him from the doorway of the old judo room.

Sho bowed back. He took the flyer. He put it in the bin by the vending machines, folded once, on top of the brass band.

He knew how clubs went. Soccer at 9, because his friend Daisuke did soccer, until Daisuke moved to Nagoya. Swimming at 10, until the coach started timing them. Kendo at 11, for 4 months, until a 13-year-old hit him on the wrist so hard he couldn't hold chopsticks, and he understood that the older kids were allowed to do that, and would keep doing it, and that the point of kendo was to get better at being hit. He'd stopped going. Nobody had made a fuss. By 12 he'd stopped trying new ones, and by 15 nobody asked anymore except his mother.

She'd asked that morning, holding out his bento.

"Just try 1," she'd said. "1 thing. Something you'd actually like."

"I like going home."

"That's not a club, Sho."

Mio had looked up from her cereal. She was 10 and still had her pajama top on over her skirt, which was how she got dressed when she was running late, from the bottom up. "Do something cool," she'd said. "Kaho's brother's in a band."

"Kaho's brother is 19."

"So?"

He hadn't had an answer to that. The entry form had come home in his bag on Friday, and it was in his blazer pocket now, folded in 4, blank. Every 1st year got 1. You were supposed to hand it in by the end of the month.

He went out the back of the main building, the long way round to the gate. It went past the gyms and the bike sheds and the incinerator, and nobody stood on that path with flyers, because nobody walked it. The cherry trees along the fence had buds on them, tight and dark pink, about a week from opening. The air was cold in the shade and warm in the sun, and he had his hands in his pockets and was thinking about whether the konbini by the station would still have nikuman this late in the season, or whether they'd switched the steamer over to the summer things.

Shouting came out of the 2nd gym.

"Receive it properly!"

"I AM receiving it properly!"

"Then why is it going over there?"

"BECAUSE YOU SERVED IT WEIRD!"

The big sliding door was half open. Sho slowed down, because it was the way to the gate and the voices were in the way, and because 1 of them sounded about to cry.

He looked in.

The gym was the old kind, wooden floor gone honey-colored, the paint on the lines worn through in the places people stood most. A net was up across the middle. On the far side, behind the end line, a dark-haired boy in a gym shirt was bouncing a ball with the flat of his hand, hard, the same height every time. On the near side a small kid with orange hair stood in a crouch, knees bent, arms out in front of him, his whole body pointed at the server.

"I'm going to beat you," the orange kid said. "I'm going to beat you and I'm going to be Karasuno's ace."

"You can't even receive."

"I CAN RECEIVE."

The dark-haired 1 tossed the ball up and stepped into it and hit it. It came over the net hard and dipping, with a lot of spin on it, and the orange kid got his arms under it, and it went off his arms sideways into the wall.

"See?"

"That was a practice 1!"

Sho stood in the doorway and watched them do it again. Serve, receive, wall. He'd seen volleyball in PE. He'd been on a team of 6 boys who all stood still and let it land between them. This looked about the same, louder, and he was mostly thinking that if he went round by the bike sheds he'd still make the 4:20 light at the crossing.

The dark-haired 1 served again. Harder.

The orange kid's elbows bent as the ball came in. It hit the inside of his left forearm and came off it at an angle, fast, low, straight at the door.

Sho saw it come. He saw the blue and the yellow turning over each other, and had about enough time to think *oh*.

It hit him in the face.

---

White.

Then a gym. Bigger, with a curved roof and windows high up full of gray Osaka sky, and the floor wax smell so strong it was almost a taste. A setter's voice, a man's voice, shouting "Matsumoto!" and the ball going up over the net, high and soft, hanging at the top of its climb right where he wanted it, the way it hung when the setter had looked at you and decided you were the 1. His own arm going back. His own hand.

A sound in his left knee like somebody snapping a pencil, so loud the bench heard it.

Crutches in a hospital corridor. A rehab room with a mirror. The same knee, 6 months later, 12, and a doctor saying *recreational level* in the voice doctors use for the end of something.

A narrow room with 3 monitors and the blinds down. A hitter's shoulder on the screen, frozen. A setter's hips. A league's worth of other people's rallies, rewound and played, rewound and played, at quarter speed, for 9 years. A can of coffee gone cold on the desk. His thumb on the rewind button. The clock in the corner of the middle screen: 3:14 a.m.

His heart doing something it shouldn't, all at once, hard.

Then nothing.

---

"Hey."

Sky. Pale blue, with a long flat cloud across it.

"HEY. Are you alive? Hey!"

A face came into the sky. Orange hair, wide brown eyes, mouth open.

Sho lay on the concrete outside the 2nd gym and didn't move, because he was busy.

He was Kiyose Sho. He was 15. He lived in a 2-story house 6 minutes from the school gate with his mother, who packed too much rice, and his father, who hummed in the bathroom while he shaved, and his sister, who hid his phone charger whenever she was losing an argument. He was in class 1-5. He'd quit kendo. He knew all of that the way he knew which stair at home creaked.

He was also Matsumoto Satoshi. He'd been 29. He'd been a wing spiker at university until the knee, and then he'd watched tape for a living, and then he'd been sitting at his desk at 3:14 in the morning with his thumb on a button.

And then he hadn't been.

He looked at the cloud. He knew what had happened in that room. Nobody would have been there. The light from the monitors on his face all night, and the tape running, and the cleaner opening the door at 6 with the vacuum.

He'd died. He'd died, and here was a cloud.

Both lives sat in his head, side by side. They didn't argue about it. The 15 years were his, the mother and the rice and the stair, and the 29 years were his too, the knee and the room and the clock. It felt like remembering, the plain way you remember where you left your shoes, and that was the part he couldn't get his mind around, lying there on the concrete with a cloud going over.

Something warm ran out of his nose and over his lip.

"You're bleeding! Oh no. Oh no, oh no. I'm so sorry!" The orange kid dropped into a bow so deep and so fast that his forehead nearly hit Sho's knee. "That was my receive! I mean, it was his serve. But it was my receive. But his serve was really..."

"It was your receive," said a voice from the doorway.

"I KNOW IT WAS MY RECEIVE."

Sho sat up.

The world swung once and came back. He touched his upper lip, and his fingers came away red. He looked at his hand. It was a big hand, square in the palm, the nails short. A 15-year-old's hand, soft on the inside where Satoshi's had been hard as a shoe sole from 12 years of balls. It was the right hand, and it was his, and he turned it over to look at the back of it.

"Are you okay?" said the orange kid. "Can you see? How many fingers?" He held up 3.

"3," said Sho.

The dark-haired server had come out onto the concrete. He was about Sho's height, with a face that looked like it was frowning even when it wasn't. He stood over them with the ball he'd been bouncing still under his arm.

"Is he okay," he said to the orange kid, as if Sho were somewhere else.

"I don't know! I'm asking!"

Sho opened his mouth and found that what he wanted to say was *Your elbows bent. Lock them, and move your feet 1st*, and that it was in Satoshi's voice, a grown man's voice, tired, at 3 a.m. He shut his mouth again.

"I'm okay," he said, in his own. "Sorry. I was in the way."

The server looked at him a bit longer. Then he nodded, once, and went back inside.

The ball that had hit him was by his knee. It had rolled to a stop against his leg, and sat there, blue and yellow, the panels a little gray along the seams from a gym floor.

He picked it up.

It was the most ordinary feeling in the world. A volleyball, 260 grams or so, soft, the leather slightly tacky. His fingers went to the seams by themselves, the way your feet find the stairs in the dark, and the moment they did, he wanted to play so badly that his palms hurt with it.

Satoshi had loved volleyball carefully, from the edge of the room, for 9 years. Now a 15-year-old body that had never wanted anything wanted this, all at once, with the volume turned all the way up. 10 minutes ago he'd been thinking about nikuman.

He held on to the ball.

Under the wanting, the other half of his head was doing what it had done for 9 years, all on its own. It ran the last 10 seconds back. The serve: a hard topspin serve, tossed a little in front, hit right on top of the ball so it dipped. A serious serve for a 1st year. The receive: the orange kid's elbows had bent as the ball arrived, and the ball had hit the soft inside of his left forearm, off the edge of the platform, and skidded off at whatever angle it liked. Feet set too late. Arms swinging up at it. Every beginner did it, and every tired pro did it at the end of a long 5th set, and Satoshi had written *platform* in the margin of more scouting reports than he could count.

"Oh no. Was that our ball?"

A 3rd year had come round the side of the gym at a jog, carrying 2 more balls under his arm. He had soft gray hair and a beauty mark under 1 eye, and he took 1 look at Sho's face and winced.

"It was his receive," said the orange kid, pointing at himself. "Mine. I'm sorry. Sugawara-san, I'm so sorry."

"Hinata. Okay. Breathe." The 3rd year put the balls down and crouched in front of Sho. "Hi. Don't tip your head back. Forward. Like this." He put 2 fingers on the back of Sho's head and pushed it gently down. "Pinch here. The soft part. Hinata, there's tissues in my bag. Front pocket."

Hinata ran.

"I'm Sugawara," said the 3rd year. "I'm really sorry about this."

"Kiyose," said Sho, through his pinched nose. It came out *Kiyobe*. "1st year."

Hinata came back with a whole packet of tissues and gave him all of them. Sugawara rolled 2 into plugs and Sho put them in, 1 each side, and sat on the step of the gym with the tissue sticking out of his nose and the ball on his knee.

Hinata stood in front of him. He couldn't keep still. He bounced on his toes and then made himself stop and then bounced again.

"Are you joining?" he said. "Are you a 1st year? Me too. 1-1. I came from Yukigaoka. Nobody's heard of it. We didn't have a team, really. It was just me for ages and then 3 1st years joined and I borrowed 2 more and we played 1 match. 1. In 3rd year." He pointed through the door, at the dark-haired 1, who was back at the end line, bouncing his ball. "Against his school. Kitagawa Daiichi. They won 2-0. He was the setter."

"Ah," said Sho.

"So I came here to beat him, and he came here too, which isn't FAIR, and now we're on the same team. Nearly. If we're allowed." Hinata's eyes went up, to the top of Sho's head, and stayed there. "How tall are you?"

"182."

"182!" Hinata looked up at him, and then up at the place 182 would be if Sho stood, and his face did something complicated. "You'd be such a good middle. You should be a middle. You'd block everything." He stopped bouncing. "I'm going to be the ace, though. So you know."

"Okay," said Sho.

"I mean it!"

"I know."

Sugawara was looking at Sho over Hinata's head. His eyes went to the ball on Sho's knee, and to Sho's hand on top of it, and away again.

"Hinata. Go and get the mop. Kageyama's going to want to do 50 more."

Hinata went.

Sugawara sat down on the step.

"You're a 1st year," he said. "Are you looking at clubs?"

The form was in his pocket, folded in 4. Sho could feel the corner of it through his shirt.

"I wasn't," he said.

"Oh." Sugawara smiled. "Okay."

"I think I am now."

Sugawara turned his head and looked at him properly. He had a way of looking that took its time.

"Do you have any experience?"

Sho thought about how to answer that. Yes. 12 years. A university gym in Osaka and a knee and 9 years of tape. And no, none. This body had touched a volleyball in PE, badly, and once more just now, with its face.

"Some," he said.

Sugawara waited, and Sho had nothing else he could say, so they sat there. Inside, the ball hit the floor, the same sound every time.

"We're a bit short this year," Sugawara said. "We'd be glad to have you. Practice is in the morning before school and after school till 6. Club doesn't properly start till the forms are in. Those 2 just couldn't wait." He tipped his head at the door. "Bring yours tomorrow, early, if you can. Our captain's Daichi. Sawamura. He'll want to meet you."

"Thank you," said Sho. "I will." He looked at the gym. "Can I sit here and watch? Till you finish?"

"With your nose like that?"

"I'll sit still."

Sugawara laughed. "Sure. Till 6. Then go home and put ice on it."

---

He sat on the step till 6.

The sun went down behind the clubhouse and the light in the gym went from gold to the flat white of the ceiling lamps. The cold came up out of the concrete through his trousers. Sugawara went back in, and set balls for the 2 of them, high ones, easy ones, and Hinata hit them into the net and over the end line and once, by luck, into the court, and screamed. Kageyama served, and Hinata received, and the ball went everywhere.

Sho watched it all. He watched the way Kageyama's whole body went still before he served, like a held breath. He watched Sugawara's hands. He watched Hinata's feet, which were fast and in the wrong place, every time, and his arms, which were wrong in a new way every time. His nose throbbed.

At 10 to 6, Kageyama walked to the net and tossed a ball straight up, high, for nobody in particular.

Hinata went after it.

He ran from the 3-meter line, 3 steps, and his last 2 were so fast they blurred, and he left the floor, and kept leaving it. Sho watched him go up. 163 centimeters of kid, maybe, and his hand went up past the tape, and past it, and his whole head came above the net, and at the top his hand came round and slapped the ball down into the floor on the other side with a bang that went round the roof.

Satoshi had spent 9 years watching V.League middles on a screen. He knew what a jump looked like when it was good. He sat on the step with the tissues in his nose and tried to work out where that 1 had come from, and the numbers wouldn't add up.

Hinata landed and screamed "YES!" at the ceiling. Then he turned round, to the door, and pointed at Sho.

"DID YOU SEE?"

"I saw," said Sho.

"Stop showing off and get the net," said Kageyama.

"I was showing HIM!"

They put the net down at 6. Sho stood up. The world swung again, a little, and settled. He went to the door with the ball still in his hands.

"Sugawara-san," he said. "Can I keep the ball?"

"No."

Sugawara held out his hand, still smiling, and waited. Sho looked at the ball, and at the hand. Then he gave it back. Sugawara took it with his fingers spread on the panels, the way setters hold a ball, and tucked it under his arm.

"Tomorrow," he said.

---

Home was 6 minutes. He ran it in 4.

His legs wanted to, so he let them, and he went up the hill past the dry cleaner's and the shrine with the 1 stone fox and the house with the dog that barked at everybody, and the dog barked at him, and he ran past it with the tissues still in his nose and his bag banging on his back. He wasn't out of breath at the top. He noticed that, and kept going.

At the corner by the vending machine the streetlight had just come on. He stopped under it and looked at his hands again. Normal hands. A bit big. No calluses. If this were Naruto there'd be a fox sealed in his stomach, and if he concentrated hard enough it would start talking to him. He tried it. A cat came out from under a car and looked at him and went back under the car.

He walked the rest of the way.

"Tadaima."

His mother came out of the kitchen before he'd got his 1st shoe off. She was wiping her hands on a towel, and she stopped in the hallway when she saw him.

"What happened to your FACE?"

Behind her, Mio came to the living room door in her socks. She looked at him, at the swelling across his nose, at the 2 plugs of tissue sticking out of it, red-brown at the ends.

"Did a girl hit you?"

"A boy with orange hair," said Sho. "With a ball. By accident. I was standing in the door."

"Why were you standing in the door?"

"I was watching."

Mio looked at the tissue as though it might explain better.

"Is it broken? Let me see." His mother came and took his chin in her fingers and turned his face to the light, the way she had when he was 6 and fell off the wall at the park. Her fingers were cold and smelled of onion. "It's swollen. Take those out."

He took the plugs out. His mother made a noise and took them off him with the towel, and went to the freezer. There was no ice in the tray, because there never was, so she came back with a bag of frozen edamame in a dish towel and held it out. He put it on his nose. It was cold enough to make his eyes water.

"Sit down," she said. "Your father made curry."

---

His father was at the stove in his work shirt with the sleeves rolled up, stirring the pot with a ladle. He made curry on Mondays because Monday was the day he got home 1st, and he made it the same way every time, out of the box, with extra potato, and it was always good. His newspaper was folded small on the corner of the table, the way he left it when he'd only read half. He looked round when Sho came in with the edamame on his face, and his eyebrows went up and stayed up.

"Volleyball," said Sho.

"Mm," said his father, and went back to the pot.

They sat. The table was the same table, with the same burn mark by his place from the hot pot 3 New Years ago, and the same 4 chairs, and the TV on low with the news. His mother put the curry down in front of him. The steam came up into his face, sweet and brown, with the carrots cut too big the way his father always cut them.

Satoshi had eaten alone for 9 years. Konbini bento at 1 in the morning in front of a screen, the plastic lid on the desk, the chopsticks still in their paper because he'd forgotten them. Some weeks he hadn't said a word out loud to anybody until Wednesday. He knew how quiet that room had been, because he could hear it now, here, under the news and his mother's chair scraping and Mio humming something from school.

He put his hands together.

"Itadakimasu," he said.

He said it properly. He heard himself do it: both hands, a little bow of the head, the whole word, every syllable.

Across the table, Mio stopped with her spoon in the air.

She looked at him. He picked up his own spoon and started eating. After a while she started eating too, but she kept looking.

"So," said his mother. "Who was it? With the ball?"

"A 1st year. Hinata. And another 1, Kageyama, who served it. And a 3rd year, Sugawara-san. He gave me the tissues." He ate a piece of potato. "They're the volleyball club."

"That was nice of him."

"Yeah." He put his spoon down. "I'm going to join."

The TV went on talking about rain in Kyushu.

His father had been about to say something. Sho saw it before it happened: his father's mouth started to open and his eyes came up from his plate, and then he closed his mouth again and looked at Sho.

His mother put her own spoon down.

"Since when," said Mio, "do you like volleyball?"

"Since today."

"You'll quit," Mio said. She said it as a fact, the way she said the bus came at 7:40. "By Golden Week."

"I won't."

"You did kendo for 4 months."

"I know."

"You said kendo was your thing. You said it at dinner. You said it right there." She pointed with her spoon at his chair. "And then you stopped going and you said it was stupid."

"It was," said Sho. "For me. It was stupid for me."

"And swimming."

"That was 10."

"And soccer."

"Mio," said their mother.

"I'm just saying." Mio put her spoon down too, now, so that all 3 of them had, and only their father was still eating. "I'm saying he'll quit by Golden Week."

Sho looked at her. She was sitting up very straight on her chair with her chin out, and her ears had gone pink at the tops. She looked about as sure as anybody had ever looked about anything.

He'd quit kendo in July. He remembered that now. He remembered her standing in the hallway in her summer pajamas when he came back from the last practice he went to, and him walking past her up the stairs, and her asking if he'd won, and him saying he wasn't going anymore. She'd been 6. He hadn't thought about it once since.

"Bet," he said.

Mio blinked. "What?"

"Your pudding cups." She had a row of them in the fridge, the little ones with the caramel at the bottom, 4 of them, lined up on the door shelf where everybody could see them and nobody was allowed to touch them. "If I'm still going after Golden Week. All of them."

She thought about it. She thought about it hard. He could see her doing it.

"Deal," she said. "And if you quit, I get your room."

"Okay."

"Okay?" She looked at their mother, as if their mother might stop it. Their mother was looking at Sho. "It's a bet, then. You can't take it back."

"I won't."

Mio got down from her chair. She went to the fridge, where the calendar hung on the door, the free 1 from the bank with the whole year on 1 sheet and a photo of Matsushima across the top. She took a magnet off the corner of it, round and red, with the bank's logo in white. She found May, and found Golden Week, and put the magnet on it, right in the middle, and pressed it flat with her thumb so it wouldn't slide.

Then she came back and sat down and picked up her spoon and went on eating.

Their mother watched her do it. Then she watched Sho, and Sho ate his curry.

"Volleyball," said his father, after a while. "That's the 1 with the net."

"Yes."

"Mm." His father ate a carrot. "Over the net. With your hands."

"Yes."

"Mm."

He went back to the news. He was eating slowly, the way he ate when he was thinking about work, and every so often his eyes came off the TV and went to Sho's nose.

Sho put the edamame down and looked at his rice.

His mother bought it from the same shop every time, from a man in Natori whose family grew it, and she always made too much, and Sho had eaten it every day of his life. The bowl was warm in his hand. The steam had made the rim wet.

"Is it bad?" his mother said. "Your nose?"

He looked up.

"No," he said. "It's good. Everything's good."

She looked at him a bit longer than she usually did. Then she nodded, and picked up her spoon, and asked Mio about her math test, and Mio said it was fine.

When the curry was finished and his father had got up to put the pot in the sink, Sho said, "Dad."

His father turned round with the pot in both hands.

"If it's all right," Sho said, "I'm going to need shoes. Volleyball ones. The kind with gum soles, for the gym floor. And knee pads." He stopped. "If it's all right. I'll use them. I'm going to use them."

His father looked at him over the pot.

"How much are they?"

"I don't know yet. I'll look."

"Mm." His father put the pot in the sink and ran the water into it. "Saturday," he said to the window.

"We'll see on Saturday," said his mother.

Mio was looking at the magnet on the fridge.

---

He couldn't sleep.

He lay in bed with the light off and both lives going round in his head, the clock at 3:14 and the clock on his own desk, which said 10:31, then 10:36, then 10:40. At 10:40 he gave up and got up and went downstairs in his socks.

The hall cupboard had the vacuum in it and the umbrellas and the box of things that didn't have a place. Under the box, flat and soft and gray with dust, was a ball. A volleyball, an old soft 1, from a beach trip when he was 7. He remembered his father throwing it to him in the shallow water and him missing every time and crying, and his father laughing and saying it didn't matter.

He found the bike pump by the back door and pumped it until it was hard and took it out to the side of the house, where the wall had no windows, only the drainpipe and the gas meter and the light from the kitchen coming round the corner.

Wall passes. The most boring drill there was. You stood 3 meters from a wall and passed the ball against it and passed it again when it came back, and you did it 1,000 times, and every coach in Japan made every beginner in Japan do it until they hated walls.

The 1st 1 went into the hedge.

He knew why. His elbows had bent at the last instant, like Hinata's. Satoshi's half of his head had the correction ready before the ball landed: lock your arms, thumbs together, angle the platform, move your feet 1st so your arms don't have to. Sho's arms heard all of that and did whatever they liked.

The 2nd 1 hit the gutter and came down on his head.

The 3rd came back to him clean. Off the wall and into his arms and back up, a little arc, the height he'd sent it, and he nearly shouted.

He kept going. He did 200. Maybe 40 were clean. The ball got away from him and he chased it into the hedge and out of the hedge and once under his father's car, and his forearms went red, then blotchy, then hot, the skin on the inside of his wrists stinging.

By 120 his thighs were shaking from staying low. This body hadn't done anything harder than the stairs to the 3rd floor in 3 years, and it was telling him so, in the backs of his legs and the small of his back. He sat on the back step until the shaking stopped. Then he got up and did the rest.

At 11:15 the window over the kitchen slid open.

"Kiyose Sho," said his mother, "it is a SCHOOL NIGHT."

"5 more."

"Now, Sho."

The window slid shut.

He did 5 more, and when he turned to pick up the ball from the hedge, the window above his mother's was open too. Mio's. She had her arms folded on the sill and her chin on her arms, and she was watching him.

He had no idea how long she'd been there.

"What are you doing," she said.

"Passing."

"To who?"

"The wall."

She looked at the wall. She looked at him, with his red arms and the ball under 1 of them, standing in the light from the kitchen at 11:20 on a school night.

"So weird," she said, and pulled her head in, and drew the curtain.

It stayed open a little at the side. He picked the ball out of the hedge and went in by the back door.

In the bathroom he washed his arms in the sink and looked at himself in the mirror.

A 15-year-old face. His own. Puffy across the bridge of the nose, a little purple under the left eye, a bit of dried blood still in the corner of 1 nostril. He'd seen this face every morning, bored, brushing his teeth. He was looking at it differently now, and it took him a while to see how: he was looking at it the way Satoshi looked at tape.

He dried his hands. His left hand went down, all on its own, and rested on his left knee, on the kneecap, and stayed there.

He took it away.

---

Upstairs, he sat at his desk and took the entry form out of his blazer and smoothed it flat with both hands.

*Name:* Kiyose Sho.

*Class:* 1-5.

*Club:* he wrote it carefully. Volleyball.

At the bottom there was a line that said *Position (if known)*, and he sat with his pen over it.

He thought about a setter yelling a name that belonged to somebody dead, and the ball hanging there, high and soft, waiting for him. About an orange-haired kid going up past the top of the net with nothing above him but the roof.

*I'm going to be Karasuno's ace,* the kid had said. Once to Kageyama, and once on the step, to Sho, so he'd know.

A team had 1 of those.

He wrote it anyway, small, in the box at the end of the line.

*Ace.*

Then he folded the form in 4 and put it in his bag where he'd see it in the morning, and set his alarm for 5:45, and turned off the light.

**Continuity (ch 1)**

- Revised Nov 13 (revision pass of ch 1 to 29). Status card removed Nov 22 (grounded tone). Old version's red-car bit is cut; ch 6 must drop "Red car still on?". Ch 10 must drop "I also wrote that I was 190" (the form has no height line).
- Canon: Hinata and Kageyama clash at Karasuno on the 1st days of club recruitment (Hinata will beat Kageyama and be Karasuno's ace; "You can't even receive"); Kageyama's hard serve, Hinata's bad receive; Hinata is from Yukigaoka (3 1st years joined, 2 borrowed players, 1 official match in 3rd year, lost to Kitagawa Daiichi with Kageyama setting); Hinata's jump. Heights: Hinata about 163, Kageyama about 180.
- Divergence (ours): the ball off Hinata's receive hits Sho in the face (in canon the ball comes off Hinata and knocks off the vice principal's wig; the fic moves the wig to ch 2, next morning). Only Sugawara is with them today; Daichi and Tanaka don't appear and nobody calls Kageyama "King" yet. Daichi named, not met. Suga: club starts properly once the forms are in.
- Invented: the 1st Monday of recruitment week, early April; Sho looks in at about 4:15 and sits on the gym step watching till 6; cherry buds a week from opening. Sho in class 1-5 (Yachi's canon class; ch 27, 31). Hinata 1-1. Sho quit soccer at 9 (friend Daisuke moved to Nagoya), swimming at 10, kendo at 11 for 4 months (a 13-year-old's hit to the wrist), in July, when Mio was 6. Mio's friend Kaho (brother 19, in a band). Dad hums while he shaves; Mio hides Sho's phone charger when she's losing an argument; the hot-pot burn mark on the table; Natori rice from the same shop; Dad's newspaper folded small.
- Satoshi's memory (every noun kept for later chapters): an Osaka university gym, floor wax, a setter shouting "Matsumoto!", the ball high and soft, the left knee at 20 (heard by the bench), crutches, "recreational level", 9 years as a tape analyst, 3 monitors, cold coffee, thumb on rewind, 3:14 a.m. on the middle screen, his heart. The cleaner came at 6. 12 years of playing.
- Nosebleed: Sugawara's tissues, 2 plugs, head forward; Mio sees the tissue in his nose at the door (ch 23 relies on this). Mom: "What happened to your FACE?" Frozen edamame in a dish towel as the ice pack (the family habit; ch 3, ch 26). Mio: "Did a girl hit you?" (ch 23).
- Sho says "Itadakimasu" properly for the 1st time; Mio stops with her spoon in the air (ch 23: the polite voice and *thank you for the food* started in April). Sho sees Dad about to speak before he speaks (ch 23: "the looking").
- The bet (kept wording): "Bet." / "Your pudding cups... All of them." / "Deal. And if you quit, I get your room." Sho wins her 4 caramel pudding cups if he's still going after Golden Week. Mio puts the bank's round red magnet on Golden Week on the fridge calendar: the free bank calendar, whole year on 1 sheet, Matsushima photo (ch 13: "a magnet on Golden Week. Since April."; ch 44: "the round red 1 from the bank"; ch 45 moves it below December).
- Dad: box curry on Mondays, extra potato, big carrots; "Mm."; "That's the 1 with the net." Shoes and knee pads asked for politely; "Saturday." Mom: "We'll see on Saturday."
- Night: wall passes with the old beach ball (from a trip when Sho was 7) at the windowless side wall, 200 passes, about 40 clean; thighs shaking by 120, a sit on the back step, then the rest (body behind the brain, Nov 22). Mom: "it is a SCHOOL NIGHT." Mio at her window: "What are you doing." "Passing." "To who?" "The wall." "So weird."
- Bathroom: his left hand rests on his left knee on its own (Mio's list, ch 34; ch 45). Never explained.
- Entry form: Name, Class 1-5, Volleyball, *Ace* in the "Position (if known)" line (ch 2: Daichi reads it aloud). Alarm 5:45.
- Ace line: *Ace* on the form, written after Hinata said it twice. A team has 1.
- Next: Ch 2, the next morning: the form to Daichi, Tanaka, the wig, 1st practice.

## Chapter 2: The wig

Sho got to the 2nd gym at 6:50 a.m. with his form, a red nose, and a plan.

The plan was to be polite to everyone older than him and get on the court.

It was cold. The cherry trees along the fence were still buds, and the concrete step where he'd sat yesterday was wet with dew. The big door was already open. Inside, the lights were on, and 3 people in practice clothes stood near the net, and 2 more stood in front of them, having yesterday's argument again.

"I'm the 1 who's going to beat you!"

"You can't even receive!"

Hinata and Kageyama. Hinata had his school bag still on 1 shoulder. Kageyama had a carton of milk in his hand with the straw already in.

Sugawara saw Sho in the doorway and lifted a hand. "Kiyose! You came." He turned to the boy beside him, who was broad across the shoulders and had very short hair and the calm face of somebody who'd already heard this argument several times since 6:30. "Daichi. This is the 1 from yesterday. The face."

"Good morning," said Sho, and bowed. "Kiyose Sho. 1-5. Thank you for yesterday, Sugawara-san."

"How's the nose?"

"Purple."

The broad 1 held out his hand, and Sho gave him the form. Sawamura Daichi, captain, 3rd year. He read it from the top, all the way down. At the bottom he stopped.

"'Position,'" he read out. "'Ace.'"

Hinata spun round. "ACE?"

The 3rd person by the net, a 2nd year with a shaved head and his practice shirt sleeves rolled to the shoulder, came over and read the form across Daichi's arm. Then he looked up at Sho, and came closer, and closer, until his face was about a hand's width from Sho's and tilted to 1 side, with his eyebrows pulled down and his mouth pulled up.

"Oi," he said. "1st year. You think you can walk in here and be the ace?"

Sho had seen that face before, in a lot of university locker rooms, on 2nd years who'd been waiting their turn.

"I'd like to be," Sho said.

"You'd LIKE to be?"

"Yes. Sorry. I wrote it." He looked at the shaved head, and the arms, which were a hitter's arms, ropy at the forearm and thick at the shoulder. "Are you the ace, senpai?"

The face held. Then something went out of it.

"...I'm not technically the ace."

"Oh." Sho bowed. "Sorry. You looked like him."

The 2nd year stared at him. His ears went red at the tops. He opened his mouth and nothing came out of it, and he turned round and walked 3 steps away and stood facing the wall with his arms folded.

"Technically," he said, to the wall.

"That's Tanaka," said Sugawara, into his hand. "2nd year. Wing spiker."

"I CAN HEAR YOU, SUGA-SAN."

Hinata had come all the way across the court. He stood in front of Sho, very close, and looked up at him with his chin out.

"I'm going to be the ace," he said. "I told you."

"I know," said Sho. "You said."

"So you can't."

"I'd still like to."

Hinata opened his mouth, and shut it, and looked at Daichi as if Daichi might rule on it. Daichi was folding Sho's form in half along its old crease.

"Any experience, Kiyose?"

"In a past life," said Sho.

Daichi laughed. It was a short, real laugh. He put the form in his jacket pocket.

"Morning practice is 7 to 8," he said. "Get changed. You 2." He turned. Hinata and Kageyama had drifted back toward each other and were standing side by side now, both of them looking at Daichi. "I'm going to say this once. This club is a team. If you 2 can't play on the same side of the net, you can't play here."

"He's the 1 who..." Hinata started.

"Once," said Daichi, and smiled, and Hinata closed his mouth.

Kageyama drank his milk until the carton made the noise.

---

Morning practice was stretching, then passing in pairs, then the bell.

The 2nd years who lived far out came only after school, so there were 6 of them, and Daichi paired Sho with Sugawara. Sugawara threw. Sho passed. His arms sent the ball off to the right, and off to the left, and once straight up into the lights, and Sugawara laughed and threw him another 1.

At 8 they rolled the balls back into the cart, and Hinata and Kageyama ran for the shoe lockers arguing about who was faster, and the bell went for homeroom while Sho was still tying his indoor shoes.

---

School was strange.

It was the same school as yesterday: the same 3rd-floor classroom, 1-5, the window row, at the back, with the radiator that knocked and a view of the hill behind the gyms. The same teacher, a woman in her 50s who said "Well then" before everything. The same boy in front of him, Okada, who slept through homeroom with his head on his arms. Sho knew all of it. He'd known it for a week.

But the 29-year-old in him hadn't sat in a high school classroom for 11 years, and he kept noticing things. How small the desks were. The smell of chalk. How every 1 of the 30 people in the room was so young that it was almost hard to look at, their faces round and soft and worried about the math quiz, and how he was 1 of them, and his own hands on the desk were the same.

In math, the teacher wrote a quadratic on the board, and Sho looked at it with both lives' worth of school behind him and had no idea. He wrote it down anyway.

At lunch he opened his bento. His mother had made it at 6 with the edamame bag still on the counter: rice, a whole row of tamagoyaki, karaage, a lot more rice under that, and a note on a strip of paper under the lid that said *eat all of it*. He ate all of it. He was hungry the way he hadn't been hungry in years, in either life.

Then he went down to the vending machines by the stairs, because he wanted a drink, and Kageyama was there.

He was standing in front of the machine with a 100-yen coin, looking at the milk. There were 3 kinds of milk. He was looking at them as if 1 of them had insulted him.

Sho waited behind him. Kageyama pushed a button. A carton of milk dropped. He took it out and turned round and saw Sho and stopped.

"...Kiyose," he said. He said it carefully, like a word in a test.

"Kageyama-kun."

Kageyama stood there with the milk. He didn't move out of the way of the machine, and he didn't go.

"You wrote ace," he said.

"Yes."

"You can't receive either."

"No," Sho agreed. "Not yet."

Kageyama frowned at that, as if *not yet* were a move he hadn't expected. He put the straw in the milk.

"How long did your serve take?" Sho said. "The 1 you hit at Hinata."

"Take?"

"To learn."

Kageyama thought about it. He thought about it seriously, which Sho hadn't expected, with his eyes on the floor and the straw in his mouth.

"I started the jump serve in 1st year," he said. "Junior high. It was bad for a year. Then it was okay." He drank. "The 1 yesterday was a normal 1. Standing."

"It was good."

Kageyama's ears went red. He looked at the vending machine. "It hit your face."

"His elbows bent," said Sho.

"I know that," said Kageyama. Then he looked at Sho properly, for the 1st time, as if he were checking something.

Sho bought a bottle of water. When he turned round Kageyama had gone, and the stairs were empty, and somewhere above him on the 2nd floor he could hear Hinata shouting "KAGEYAMA! WAIT!" and feet running.

---

After school there were more of them. Daichi, Sugawara, Tanaka. 3 more 2nd years: a quiet 1 with sleepy eyes called Ennoshita, and 2 others, Narita and Kinoshita. And a 3rd-year girl with dark hair and glasses who sat at the side of the court with a clipboard and didn't speak to anybody. Tanaka bowed to her so low, every time he passed her, that his head nearly touched his knees.

Hinata and Kageyama were there too, on the far side of the net, still arguing. They'd been arguing since 4. Kageyama wanted Hinata to prove he could receive. Hinata wanted to prove it. Daichi, warming up his shoulders at the net, kept saying "You 2," in a voice that was getting quieter every time, which Sho had already learned was the wrong direction.

"1 serve," said Kageyama. "If you can receive 1 serve..."

"I can receive 100 serves!"

"1."

"FINE."

Daichi turned his head. "You 2..."

The side door opened. A short, balding man in a gray suit came in with his hands behind his back. Tanaka's whole body went straight. "Vice principal," he breathed. "He's been looking for a reason."

Kageyama served anyway.

Sho watched it go. The same hard topspin serve as yesterday. The same orange kid under it, and the same elbows bending, and Sho's own arms twitched at his sides as if they could fix it from there.

The ball came off Hinata's forearms in a perfect high arc across the gym and came down on top of the vice principal's head.

The top of the vice principal's head came off.

It went up, turning over once, a small brown shape against the ceiling lights, and came down, and landed on Daichi's head, and stayed there.

Nobody breathed.

Hinata had gone white. Kageyama stood behind the end line with his serving hand still up. Tanaka had both hands over his mouth and his shoulders were shaking, and his eyes were full of water. Sugawara was looking at a spot on the floor with his whole face. The girl with the clipboard had stopped writing.

Sho kept his face still.

Satoshi had sat through 9 years of team meetings where a general manager said something unbelievable and everybody nodded. He could have kept a straight face through anything. He looked at the clock over the door and thought about the quadratic.

Daichi lifted the hairpiece off his head with both hands, carefully, the way you'd lift a cake out of a tin, and turned, and held it out to the vice principal with a deep bow. The vice principal snatched it. He said several things. *Sawamura* was in most of them. Then the side door shut behind him, hard, and the gym was quiet.

Daichi turned round.

He was smiling.

"You 2," he said to Hinata and Kageyama, in the kindest voice Sho had ever heard.

He went over to the bench, to his jacket, and took 2 forms out of the inside pocket. He held 1 out to each of them.

"Until you can act like teammates," he said, "you're not in this club. Out."

"But, Daichi-san..." said Hinata.

"Daichi-san, we..." said Kageyama.

"Out."

They took the forms. They went. Hinata looked back twice, at the court, at the net, at all of them. Kageyama went straight out, holding his form so tight it crumpled.

The door slid shut. Straight away somebody hit it from outside with both fists.

"WE'LL GET ALONG!" That was Hinata. "WE'LL GET ALONG, DAICHI-SAN, WE PROMISE!"

Then Kageyama's voice, flat through the door: "I'll get along with him. I'll do it." A pause. "I'd still rather spike and toss and receive all by myself."

"KAGEYAMA!"

Daichi slid the door open 10 centimeters, looked out, and slid it shut again. The hammering stopped. Daichi breathed out through his nose, and turned to the rest of them, and clapped once.

"Right," he said. "Warm-up."

---

The receiving drill was the simplest drill in the sport. Daichi stood on a box at the net and hit easy balls at whoever was in line, and you passed them up to Sugawara at the setter's spot.

Satoshi had done this drill maybe 50,000 times.

Daichi's 1st ball to Sho went into the ceiling, rang off a light, and dropped on Tanaka.

"Oi!"

"Sorry, senpai."

He'd swung at it. His legs had been late, so his arms had gone and done the work for them, the way they would until his legs learned to get there 1st.

The 2nd ball went off his thumb into the net.

The 3rd went straight back at Daichi, who caught it.

"Your feet are late," Daichi said.

"Yes, Captain."

"Again."

He went again. The next 1 was late too. He went to the back of the line, and Narita went, and Kinoshita, and Ennoshita, who passed every 1 to Sugawara so neatly that Sho wanted to ask him how, and then it was Sho again. Late. Back of the line. Tanaka laughed every time, loud, with his head back, the way you'd laugh at a friend who'd fallen off a bike and was fine.

On the 11th ball, Sho's feet got there 1st.

Arms locked. Platform flat. The ball went up in a soft arc and came down into Sugawara's hands as if it had been aimed there.

"Nice receive!" Sugawara called.

Sho stood very still with his arms out.

It was a beginner's pass. A normal, boring, beginner's pass, the kind Satoshi used to do 1,000 of before breakfast. He wanted to run round the gym shouting.

"Next," said Daichi.

He went to the back of the line.

---

Then spiking, and for about 3 seconds Sho thought it was going to be his day.

Sugawara set. Sho ran in the way Satoshi had run in 10,000 times: left, right, left, arms swinging back, plant, up.

The floor went away.

He went up. It was a tall boy's jump: long legs, long arms, and nothing loaded behind them. Satoshi's last step used to go down deep and throw him; these legs skipped that part and just left, quick and shallow, the way you'd hop a puddle.

He was early. He was much too early. He was at the top with nothing to hit, swung at empty air, and came down on his left foot alone, knee folding in, in time for the ball to land on his head.

His left hand was on his left knee before he'd straightened up. The knee was fine. He took the hand off it.

Tanaka sat down on the floor.

The 2nd time he waited too long, and the ball dropped past his hand while he was still on his way up.

The 3rd time, he caught it right. He met it lower than he wanted, below the top of his reach, but he met it with the whole hand, and it left his hand with a sound like a door slamming. It hit the floor near the end line and kicked up and hit the back wall so hard the sound came back off it.

It was about a meter out.

The gym had gone quiet anyway.

Tanaka had stopped laughing. He was looking at the back wall, then at Sho, and his jaw had gone tight.

"Again," Tanaka said to Sugawara, already walking to the line. "Me."

Sugawara set. Tanaka took 3 hard steps and went up, and his arm came through like a whip, and at the very top his wrist turned over and the ball went cross-court, sharp, inside the line, into the floor with a crack.

He landed, turned, and grinned at Sho with every tooth.

"That's how it's done, rookie."

Sho had already stopped hearing him. He was watching the place in the air where the wrist had turned. Late snap, high contact, the wrist rolling over the top at the last instant so the ball went across the body. That snap was worth stealing.

"Teach me," Sho said.

Tanaka blinked. "Huh?"

"That. The cross. The thing your wrist does at the end." He bowed, properly, from the waist. "Please teach me, Tanaka-senpai."

Tanaka looked at him. He looked at Daichi, at Sugawara, at the girl with the clipboard, who was writing, and back at Sho. His mouth moved.

"...2 meat buns," he said.

"Okay."

"Big ones."

"Okay."

Tanaka turned to the net with his fists clenched at his sides. "DAICHI-SAN. He wants ME to teach him."

"I heard," said Daichi.

---

The last part of practice was running.

By lap 10, Sho's lungs had a taste in them like an old coin. By lap 15, Tanaka was swearing at the floor and Sho was in the back half with Kinoshita, who was a 2nd year and was being kind about it. By lap 20, his legs had stopped belonging to him below the knee. By lap 25, even Daichi was breathing through his mouth, and Sho came in 2nd to last and stood bent over at the line with his hands on his knees and sweat running off the end of his nose onto the boards.

Ennoshita was lying flat on his back on the stage steps with his arm over his eyes. He lifted the arm and looked at Sho upside down.

"It's like that every day," he said. "In case you were wondering."

"Thank you, senpai."

"Don't thank me," said Ennoshita, and put his arm back.

Tanaka had gone to the back window, the high 1 over the fire door, and was standing on the bench under it with his hands on the sill, looking out at the dirt behind the gym. Sho could hear the ball from out there: a toss, a run, a slap, somebody falling.

"MORNING PRACTICE IS AT 7," Tanaka said to the window, very loudly, to nobody. "NOBODY'S ALLOWED IN THIS GYM BEFORE 7. NOBODY."

He got down off the bench.

"What?" he said, to Narita, who was looking at him.

His legs were shaking. Satoshi's half of his head was already doing the sum, the way it used to do sums on other people's bodies: no base under any of it, a body that had quit swimming at 10, every good thing it had borrowed from being tall. Passing. Legs. Lungs. Landing. It was a long list. He'd seen worse lists turn into players. It had taken them a year.

Sugawara came over, still breathing hard, and handed him a bottle of water from the crate.

"Have you played before?" Sugawara said. "Properly? On a team?"

"No."

"So where did that spike come from? The 1 off the back wall."

"I don't know, Sugawara-san."

Sugawara looked at him a bit longer. Then he laughed, and patted him on the shoulder, and went to put the balls away.

---

At 6, while everyone was pulling down the net, Sho went to Daichi and bowed.

"Please give me 20 more minutes," he said. "Passing. I'll mop."

"You'll mop anyway. You're a 1st year."

"I'll mop twice."

Daichi looked at him. Behind Daichi, Tanaka was at the bench with his bag over his shoulder, waiting for Daichi to be free, shifting from foot to foot. He came over.

"Daichi-san," said Tanaka. "I'll lock up tonight. If you give me the key. And tomorrow morning, I'll open. I'll come early."

Daichi turned his head. "You? Early?"

"I'm turning over a new leaf."

Daichi looked at him. Tanaka looked at the ceiling. Then Daichi took the key off the hook by the door, a single brass key on a red string with a plastic tag that said 第二体育館, and dropped it into Tanaka's hand, and didn't ask why, and Tanaka didn't offer. He went and sat on the stage steps with his bag to wait for them to finish.

Sho saw Sugawara see it. Sugawara was at the cart with a ball in each hand, and he watched Tanaka sit down on the steps with the key in his fist, and smiled, and put the balls in the cart.

"20 minutes," Daichi said to Sho. "Suga, can you..."

"I'll feed him," said Sugawara. "Go home, Daichi. You look tired."

"I'm not tired."

"Go home."

Daichi went home.

---

Sugawara threw. Sho passed.

The gym was nearly empty. The lights over the far court were off, so only their half was lit, and the dark half beyond the net was full of stacked chairs and the folded-up stage curtain, and their voices came back off it.

Sho got 1 good pass in 6. Then 1 in 4. He didn't talk. Sugawara didn't push him to. He threw easy, high, the same height every time, and said "Feet" when it was feet and "Good" when it was good and nothing at all when it went into the dark.

"Sugawara-san," said Sho, when the 1 in 4 had become 1 in 3. "Are you a setter?"

The next ball didn't come right away. Sugawara was holding it.

"I am," he said. He threw it. Sho passed it, late, off to the left. Sugawara went and got it and came back. "So is he," he said. He tipped his head at the door Kageyama had gone out of. "So is Kageyama."

He threw the ball. Sho passed it, and it was good, and Sugawara caught it and threw again.

Sho had a lot to say about it, from the older life. None of it was anything a 1st year could say to a 3rd year on his 2nd day.

"Your tosses are easy to pass," he said in the end. "They come down the same every time."

Sugawara laughed. "That's throwing, Kiyose. Setting's harder."

Sugawara threw him another 1. He threw for the full 20 minutes and then 5 more, and on the last 1 Sho's feet went before he told them to, and the ball went up and hung, and came down into Sugawara's hands without him having to move.

"There," said Sugawara. "Mop."

Sho mopped the whole floor twice while Tanaka sat on the steps and complained about it, and then Tanaka locked the big door behind the 3 of them, and turned the key twice to be sure, and put it in his pocket with his hand over it.
---

Behind the gym, under the 1 light that worked, he found Hinata and Kageyama.

They hadn't gone home. There was a strip of bare dirt between the back of the gym and the fence, and the light on the wall above the fire door made a circle on it, and they were in the circle. Kageyama was hitting balls at Hinata from 6 meters away, hard, 1 after another, and Hinata was chasing every 1 he didn't get his arms on across the dirt, falling, scraping his knees and getting up again. Their school bags were in a heap against the wall. Hinata's form was sticking out of the top of his.

They were still arguing. They were still playing.

Hinata saw him 1st. "Ball-to-the-face guy! You made the team?"

"Provisionally."

"You got in 1st." Hinata stood there with his scraped knees and the ball under his arm. "On your 2nd day. With your face."

"What's provisionally?"

"I don't know yet," said Sho.

"We'll be on it too!" Hinata pointed at the gym. "Soon. Really soon. Right, Kageyama?"

Kageyama caught the ball and held it with both hands, close to his chest.

"We're going to ask for a match," Kageyama said. "Tomorrow. Us against the 2nd years. If we win, he has to let us in."

"And then we're going to WIN," said Hinata.

"I just said that."

"I'm SAYING it!"

"Can I watch?" Sho said. "Now. For a bit."

Kageyama looked at him. Then at the spot where Sho was standing, which was right in the middle of the circle of light, on the edge of where Hinata had been landing.

"...Don't stand there," he said.

Sho moved to the wall, by the bags. Kageyama hit the next 1. It came off Hinata's arms high and wild, back over his head toward the fence, and Hinata turned and ran after it, 3 steps, so fast the dirt sprayed, and jumped.

There it was again. The jump. Higher than any 163 centimeters had a right to go, his whole head over the height where a net would be, his arm up and back like a drawn bow. He slapped the ball out of the air into the fence, and the fence rang, and he came down and fell over and got up laughing.

"Receive it," said Kageyama. "Don't hit it."

"It was going AWAY!"

Sho stood by the wall and watched them for 20 minutes.

He was supposed to be taking it apart. That was what his head did. It went to Hinata's feet, which were too fast and in the wrong place, and to Kageyama's hands, which were perfect even when all they were doing was hitting a ball at somebody. It found all of that, and kept coming back to the jump, which it couldn't sort at all.

If somebody ever put a set exactly where that kid's hand was going to be...

"Oi," said Kageyama, at 7, catching the ball. "Kiyose."

"Yes?"

"If we get a match." Kageyama turned the ball in his hands. "You're on their side. You know that."

Hinata stopped on the far edge of the light, panting, and looked at Sho too.

Sho thought about it. He thought about standing on a court with that jump coming at him from the other side of the net, and a set that perfect behind it, and what it would be like to be the 1 who had to stop it.

"I know," he said.

---

His mother had left a plate on the table under cling film, and a note on top that said *miso in the pot*, and gone up to run Mio's bath. His father was at the table with the newspaper. When Sho came in, he folded it small and put it down and got up and went to the drawer.

He came back with a pencil and a tape measure and the hardback dictionary from the shelf.

"Shoes off," said his father.

"They're off."

"Stand there."

Sho stood against the door frame between the kitchen and the hall, in his socks. His father put the dictionary flat on top of his head and pushed it down, gently, until it sat, and held it there, and drew a line on the wood under it with the pencil. Then he took the dictionary away and hooked the end of the tape measure on the floor and ran it up to the line and read it, close, with his face near the tape.

He wrote it next to the line, small. *182. Apr.*

There were other lines under it. Sho had forgotten they were there. They went down the frame in steps, lower and lower and closer together, some of them labeled in his father's handwriting and some in his mother's, and beside his were Mio's, on the other side of the frame, in pink felt-tip at 1st and then in pencil. The lowest of his was at about the height of his hip. *Sho 4.*

"Mm," said his father. He put the tape measure and the pencil in the drawer, and the dictionary on the shelf, and sat down, and unfolded the newspaper.

"Saturday," he said, turning a page.

Sho stood in the doorway in his socks and looked at the line, the new 1 at the top, a long way up from the rest.

Tomorrow, Kageyama was going to ask for his match.

**Continuity (ch 2)**

- Revised Nov 13 (revision pass of ch 1 to 29). No status card.
- Canon: Hinata and Kageyama argue at the gym; Kageyama serves at Hinata while the vice principal is in the gym; the ball comes off Hinata and knocks off the vice principal's wig, which lands on Daichi's head; Daichi returns it with a bow, then hands Hinata and Kageyama back their forms and throws them out until they act like teammates; they pound on the door, promise to get along, and Kageyama says he'd still rather spike, toss and receive by himself. Kageyama comes up with challenging the 2nd years to a match (theirs, not Sho's). Tanaka asks Daichi for the gym key, and Sugawara notices. Tanaka announces at the window that nobody uses the gym before 7 (canon: so the 2 hear it).
- Divergence (ours): the wig happens at after-school practice, the day after the ball hits Sho (canon: club day 1); the ball comes off Hinata's forearms (ch 1 used the face). Hinata and Kageyama were at morning practice before the wig. Daichi warns them at 6:50 a.m. ("This club is a team"); in canon he says it when he throws them out. Ennoshita, Narita and Kinoshita appear at this practice (canon introduces Ennoshita at the 3v3). Kiyoko sits at the side with a clipboard, unnamed to Sho (her introduction is ch 4).
- Invented: Tuesday, the day after ch 1. Morning practice 7 to 8; 6 people (the 2nd years who live far out come only after school). Class 1-5: window row at the back (arc3 ch 31), knocking radiator, the teacher's "Well then", Okada asleep in front of him. Bento with *eat all of it*. Lunch at the vending machines with Kageyama: "You wrote ace." "You can't receive either." "No. Not yet."; Kageyama learned the jump serve in junior high 1st year, bad for a year; "The 1 yesterday was a normal 1. Standing."; "It hit your face." "His elbows bent." "I know that."
- The form: Daichi reads "'Position,' 'Ace.'" aloud. Tanaka: "Oi. 1st year. You think you can walk in here and be the ace?" "I'd like to be." "Are you the ace, senpai?" "...I'm not technically the ace." "Sorry. You looked like him." Tanaka, to the wall: "Technically." Hinata: "I'm going to be the ace. I told you." "I'd still like to." Daichi: "Any experience?" "In a past life." (said aloud; never the name).
- 1st practice: receive drill, the 11th ball is his 1st clean pass ("Nice receive!"). Spikes: too early (ball on his head), too late, then 1 heavy hit about 1 m out off the back wall. Tanaka's cross with the late wrist snap; "Teach me." "...2 meat buns." "Big ones." (paid ch 3). 25 laps, 2nd to last, legs gone below the knee (Nov 22: body behind the brain; the jump is a tall boy's jump, quick and shallow, no load; landed on the left foot alone, knee in, hand to the knee); Ennoshita on the stage steps: "It's like that every day... In case you were wondering." "Thank you, senpai." "Don't thank me." Sugawara: "Have you played before?... So where did that spike come from? The 1 off the back wall." "I don't know, Sugawara-san."
- Tanaka takes the gym key (brass, red string, tag 第二体育館; ch 27: "lived on a red string") to lock up and "open early"; he waits on the stage steps and locks up after Sho and Sugawara (ch 3: he lets Hinata and Kageyama in at dawn).
- Extra 20 (+5) minutes with Sugawara feeding (throwing, never setting him in a match; P4/06 relies on this): 1 in 6, 1 in 4, 1 in 3, last 1 clean. "Are you a setter?" "I am... So is he. So is Kageyama." "Your tosses are easy to pass." "That's throwing, Kiyose. Setting's harder." Sho mops twice.
- Behind the gym: bare dirt, the 1 working light above the fire door (ch 3, ch 27). "Provisionally." Hinata: "You got in 1st. On your 2nd day. With your face." Kageyama: "We're going to ask for a match. Tomorrow. Us against the 2nd years. If we win, he has to let us in." Hinata: "And then we're going to WIN." "Don't stand there." Sho watches 20 minutes. Kageyama, at 7: "You're on their side. You know that." "I know."
- Home: Mom's covered plate, *miso in the pot*. Dad measures him at the kitchen door frame (dictionary flat on his head, pencil line, tape measure): *182. Apr.* Older lines step down the frame, Sho's and Mio's (pink felt-tip, then pencil); the lowest of Sho's, *Sho 4*, at hip height (ch 45). "Saturday."
- Ace line: the 1 heavy hit off the back wall, and Tanaka's "Again. Me."
- Next: Ch 3, dawn practice (Tanaka's key), Tsukishima and Yamaguchi, Kageyama asks for the match and Daichi makes it a 3v3.

## Chapter 3: Dawn reps

Sho's alarm went off at 4:30 a.m., and he lay there and hated the man he used to be.

Satoshi had been a morning person. Satoshi had liked the gym at dawn, when the floor was cold and nobody was watching. Sho, until 2 days ago, had believed 4:30 a.m. was a rumor.

He got up anyway.

He went downstairs in his socks with his bag over his shoulder, planning to be gone before anyone stirred. The kitchen light was on.

His mother stood at the counter in her cardigan, pressing rice between her palms. 2 onigiri already sat on a plate, wrapped in plastic. A 3rd was taking shape.

He looked at her hands before she said anything. They were pink from the hot rice, and they were turning the ball of it the way they did for salmon, with the dent pressed in the middle for the filling, and the little pot of flaked salmon was open on the counter beside the cutting board, and the umeboshi jar was still in the fridge door with its lid on.

"Mom. It's 4:40."

"I know what time it is." She kept her eyes on the rice. "Salmon or umeboshi?"

"...How did you know?"

"You set your alarm to the loudest 1. The whole street knows." She wrapped a strip of nori round the rice and set it with the others. "Salmon, then. You hate umeboshi."

He did hate umeboshi. He had since he was 4. The man in the other half of his head used to eat it out of the jar with a spoon at his desk at 2 in the morning. He stood in the kitchen in his socks with both of those true at once, and then the kettle clicked off, and he was a kid watching his mother make him breakfast in the dark.

She put all 3 onigiri in a cloth bundle and tied the corners and held it out.

"Ittekimasu," he said at the door.

"Itterasshai." A pause. "Wear the jacket. It's cold."

It was 6 degrees. He wore the jacket.

---

The konbini by the shrine had 2 nikuman left in the steamer, the last of the season, a bit wrinkled from sitting there all night. Sho bought both. Then he ran.

The streets were blue and empty. The cherry trees by the shrine were still in bud, tight and dark against the sky, and the road was wet from the night. His breath fogged. His legs wanted to go faster, so he let them, and he reached the 2nd gym at 4:58, barely breathing hard.

Hinata and Kageyama were already there, sitting on the concrete step outside the locked door.

Hinata shot to his feet. "Kiyose? What are you doing here?"

"Training. What are you doing here?"

"Tanaka-senpai said nobody's allowed in before 7," Hinata said, "so we came at 5!"

"So we get 2 hours," said Kageyama, as if it were obvious.

Sho looked at the locked door. Then at the 2 of them, red-nosed with cold, with a ball between them they'd clearly been passing in the dark already. Hinata's knees were still scraped from last night. Kageyama had a carton of milk in his jacket pocket.

"Did anyone tell you who has the key?"

Silence.

Kageyama's face did something slow. Hinata's mouth opened and closed again.

"...Oh," said Hinata.

At 5:04, footsteps came slapping up the path. Tanaka appeared in a tracksuit, yawning so wide his jaw cracked, with the key on its red string looped round 1 finger.

"I knew it," he said. "I knew you idiots would come at 5." Then he saw Sho. "Why are YOU here?"

"My lesson," said Sho, and held out the plastic bag. "Cross-court. The wrist. You said 2. They're the big ones."

Tanaka looked into the bag. 2 nikuman, still steaming a little in the cold.

"Where'd you even get these at 5 in the morning?"

"The konbini by the shrine. They were the last 2."

Tanaka's ears went pink. He shoved the key into the lock with a lot more force than it needed.

"...Senpai doesn't need bribes," he said, to the lock.

"It's for the lesson."

"I know what it's for."

He ate the 1st bun in 3 bites while he wrestled the door open. The 2nd 1 he wrapped back up in its paper and put inside his jacket, against his chest, and zipped the jacket up over it, and Sho looked at the door.


---

Inside, the gym smelled of cold wood and somebody's old knee pads. Hinata bowed at the door and shouted "Onegaishimasu!" at the empty court. Kageyama bowed too, shorter and stiffer. Sho found himself bowing before he'd decided to. The older half of him had done it every morning for 12 years, and the habit had come back with everything else.

They put the net up in the blue light from the high windows. Hinata stood on the post base to reach the top and nearly fell off. Kageyama fixed the antenna 3 times. Tanaka sat on the edge of the stage with his jacket zipped up to his chin and supervised, which mostly meant pointing.

Then it started.

"Toss to me!" said Hinata.

"No," said Kageyama.

"Why not?"

"Because you can't receive." Kageyama turned the ball over in his hands. "In a match, I toss to whoever's going to win us the point. That's not you. Not till you can receive."

Hinata went red to the ears. "I'll get better!"

"Then get better 1st."

Sho, stretching his hamstrings against the wall, was watching Kageyama's hands while he said it. Big hands, long fingers, very still round the ball. Setter's hands.

"Kageyama-kun," Sho said. "Could you toss me 1? Please."

Both of them turned.

"You?" said Kageyama.

"Just 1."

Kageyama looked at him. Then at Hinata, who was staring at Sho with the face of a boy betrayed by a stranger he'd known for 2 days. Kageyama nodded and picked up a ball.

"Fine. Watch, Hinata."

The set was beautiful. It came off Kageyama's fingers in a clean high line and stopped right where an outside hitter would want it, high and a little off the net, as if he'd measured Sho in the doorway and built the toss to fit him.

Sho ran in, planted, and went up.

He was early again. He was at the top of his jump with his hand back while the ball was still coming down to him, and then he was on his way down, and the ball arrived at the place he'd been. It clipped his wrist on the way past and rolled off toward the stage.

Tanaka coughed into his sleeve.

"...Your timing," said Kageyama, "is garbage."

"Yes," said Sho.

"Then why did you ask?"

"To see the toss." Sho rubbed his wrist. He'd watched national team setters on a screen in slow motion for 9 years, and they were men of 30. This was a 1st year in a cold gym at 5 in the morning. "I've never had 1 like that."

Kageyama opened his mouth. Closed it. Red came up his neck.

"...Don't say weird things," he said, and went to get the ball.

Behind him, Hinata had started laughing, a helpless, hiccupping laugh, bent over with his hands on his knees. "You're bad too! You're SO bad!"

"I'm bad at different things."

"We're both bad!" Hinata straightened up, grinning all over his face. "We're both really bad!"

"Hinata," said Kageyama, from under the stage. "Receive."

He took Hinata to the far court and started hitting balls at him, hard, 1 after another. Sho went to the stage.

"Tanaka-senpai."

Tanaka looked down at him from the edge of the stage. He had his hands in his jacket pockets, round the 2nd bun.

"The lesson," said Sho.

"Now?"

"You've got the key. And it's 5:15."

Tanaka looked at the far court, where Hinata was diving at a ball and missing it, and at the ball cart, and at Sho. He got down off the stage.

"Fine," he said. "Fine. Get a ball."

---

It turned out Tanaka couldn't explain it.

He tried. He stood at the net on a chair from the stage, with a ball in his left hand, and tossed it up for himself and hit it standing on the chair, and the ball went across the court sharp, cross, into the far corner. "Like that," he said.

"Can you do it slower?"

"It's a SPIKE. You can't do it slower."

"Sorry."

Tanaka got down off the chair and stood in front of Sho and held his right arm up, bent, as if he were about to hit. "Okay. Okay. So you come in, and you're up there, and the ball's there." He pointed at the air. "And your hand's going like this, straight, at the ball. And then right at the end, you just..." He turned his wrist over. His hand went from facing forward to facing across his own body, fast, like turning a doorknob. "You just do that."

"When?"

"At the END."

"At the end of the swing? Or when you touch the ball?"

Tanaka looked at his own hand as if it had betrayed him. "...When you touch it. I think. I don't know. I just do it." He frowned. "You think about it way too much, Kiyose."

"Yes, senpai. Sorry."

But it was enough. Sho had the swing in his head already, the whole shape of it, from 10,000 of his own and from hours of other people's on tape. What he hadn't had was Tanaka's turn at the very top, so late it looked like an afterthought. He stood on the chair, the way Tanaka had, and tossed for himself, and hit it straight into the net.

"No," said Tanaka.

The 2nd went out the side.

"No."

The 3rd went across the court flat and fast, and hit the floor just inside the far sideline, and bounced up into the curtain of the stage.

Tanaka stared at the curtain.

"Huh," he said.

"Was that it?"

"That was..." Tanaka scratched his head. He had a strange look on his face, as if he'd lent somebody his bike and they'd ridden it better than him on the 1st try and he hadn't decided yet how he felt about it. "That was kind of it."

"Thank you, senpai."

"Don't THANK me. That was 1. Do 50."

Sho did 50, off the chair, while Tanaka stood beside him and said "no" and "no" and "closer" and, once, "THAT 1," loud enough that Hinata missed a ball on the far court to look round.

At the end Tanaka took the ball off him and looked at it, and then at Sho, and then up at the back wall, where yesterday Sho's 1 heavy hit had gone and slammed.

"Yesterday," he said. "The 1 off the wall."

"It was out."

"I know it was out." Tanaka tossed the ball from 1 hand to the other.

---

Sugawara found them at 5:40.

He came through the door in a scarf with his hands in his pockets, looked at the 4 of them, and sighed through his nose.

"Tanaka," he said. "You asked Daichi for the key yesterday with the worst poker face I've ever seen."

"Suga-san!" Tanaka nearly fell off the stage. "It's not... We're..."

"I won't tell him." Sugawara slipped off his shoes, set them side by side by the wall, and walked onto the court in his socks. "But if you're going to sneak in, at least do some real receiving. Hinata. Kiyose. Over here."

So the 2 of them stood side by side while Sugawara fed them easy balls, and they were, together, terrible.

Hinata's elbows bent. Sho's feet were late. Hinata dove for balls he didn't need to dive for and slid on his chest into the wall. Sho stood still and let 1 hit him in the stomach while he was still working out which way to move.

"Kiyose," said Sugawara. "Stop thinking. Just go to it."

"Yes."

Hinata, beside him, threw himself at the next ball before it had left Sugawara's hands, and Sugawara held on to it and waited for him to get up off the floor.

By 6:30, Sho's forearms had gone from pink to red to a blotchy purple, and he was landing maybe 1 clean pass in 3. Hinata was about the same. Every time 1 of them got a good 1, the other yelled "NICE!" as if it were match point at Nationals. Sugawara kept his eyes on the ball cart, and the corner of his mouth kept going.

---

At 6:45, Sho went out to the vending machines by the bike sheds for water. Kageyama came too.

They stood at the machine. Sho fed in his coins and got his water. Round the corner of the gym, voices came on the cold air: Sugawara and Hinata, sitting on the step.

Kageyama had stopped with his coin halfway to the slot, so Sho stopped too.

"...I'll set for you, if you want," Sugawara was saying. "I mean it. I'm a setter too. You wouldn't have to wait for him to decide you're good enough."

A pause. A bird somewhere on the gym roof.

"No," said Hinata.

"No?"

"Sorry, Sugawara-san. Sorry. Your sets are really..." Hinata's voice went up and down. "But if I took yours now, it'd be like losing. To him. I only played 1 match in junior high. 1. And it was against his team, and they beat us so fast, and he was just standing there on the other side being... I want to beat that. I want to beat him. If I let somebody else set me so I don't have to deal with him, then I already lost."

The wind came round the corner and took some of what Sugawara said next. Sho caught the end of it.

"...the person you most want to beat. Sometimes that's the same person who's going to make you strongest."

Kageyama stood in front of the vending machine with the 100-yen coin in his fingers and didn't put it in.

Sho put in 100 yen of his own and pressed the button for milk. The carton thumped into the tray. He took it out and held it out to Kageyama.

Kageyama looked at it. "...What."

"Milk. You were going to buy milk."

"I have money."

"I know you have money."

He kept holding it out. After a bit Kageyama took it, carefully, as if it might go off.

"Why do you keep saying weird things," he said to the carton.

Sho didn't have an answer to that, so he didn't give 1. They walked back round the corner. Hinata looked up from the step, and his eyes were a little red. Sugawara was sitting beside him with his elbows on his knees. Kageyama stopped in front of Hinata and drank half the milk in 1 go, standing there.

Then, to the carton again: "Get up. We've got 10 minutes."

Hinata got up.

Sugawara looked up at Sho from the step. He held the look a bit longer than people usually do, as if he were working out where to put him.

At 6:50 he stood up and clapped his hands.

"Right. Out, you 2. Daichi gets here at 7, and you were never here. Tanaka, the net stays up. Daichi likes it up."

"Yes, Suga-san."

Hinata and Kageyama went, with their bags, at a run, out of the door and round the back of the gym toward the dirt.

---

Morning practice was the 4 of them, with Daichi in his scarf saying "Good morning" to the net and the net already up. He looked at it. He looked at Tanaka, who was yawning, and at Sho's purple forearms, and at the floor, which had wet footprints on it where somebody had dragged the ball cart through the dew at the door.

"Good," he said, and nothing else, and they stretched.

They did passing in pairs again. When Daichi split them up for the last 15 minutes, Sho went to Sugawara at the cart.

"Sugawara-san."

"Mm?"

"Could you toss to me? Real tosses. In practice." Sho bowed. "Whenever there's time. I know you're busy."

Sugawara had a ball in each hand. He stood there with them.

"You want my tosses," he said.

"Yes."

"Kageyama's tosses are better." Sugawara said it the way you'd say the bus was late. "You saw them. I watched you see them, this morning."

"Kageyama-kun is busy. He's got Hinata." Sho thought about what else to say, and the 29-year-old in him had a lot of things ready, and none of them were true enough. "And I like yours. They come down where I can find them."

Sugawara laughed, quick, through his nose. He looked down at the 2 balls in his hands, and then at the far side of the gym, where the door was, as if somebody had just gone out of it.

"Okay," he said. "After school, at the end. 10 minutes, if Daichi says."

"Thank you."

"Your timing's still garbage, though. He's right about that."

"Yes, Sugawara-san."

He tossed 10 before the bell. Sho was early on 6 and late on 3, and on the last 1 he was neither, and the ball went down into the far court, hard, and Sugawara said "There!" and Daichi, across the gym, turned his head and looked.

---

At 3:50 that afternoon, before warm-ups, Hinata and Kageyama were at the gym door again.

They stood outside on the step in their gym clothes with their school bags at their feet, because they weren't allowed in, and when Daichi came across the court to the door, Kageyama bowed. He bowed low, from the waist, and stayed there. Hinata bowed beside him, so fast he nearly fell down the step.

"Please let us play a match," said Kageyama, to the concrete. "Against the 2nd years. Us 2. If we win, let us in the club."

"Please!" said Hinata.

Behind them, on the path, 2 boys Sho didn't know had stopped walking. 1 was blond and very tall, with glasses and headphones round his neck. The other was shorter and freckled. They had their gakuran jackets over their arms and they were watching the bowing with interest.

Daichi looked at the tops of the 2 heads.

"Us 2," he said. "Against Tanaka and the others."

"Yes."

"Kageyama. You'd set. Who would receive?"

Kageyama didn't answer. Hinata's ears went red.

Daichi looked past them, up the path, at the tall blond boy and the freckled 1, and his face changed a little, as if a problem had just solved itself.

"You 2 are Tsukishima and Yamaguchi," he said. "You're on the forms."

"Yes," said the blond 1. "Chiwassu."

"Good. Then here's the match." Daichi turned back to the bowing pair. "Saturday. 3 on 3. You 2 and Tanaka, against these 2 new 1st years and 1 more. If you win, you're in. If you lose..." The smile didn't move. "You're in. And Kageyama doesn't play setter while I'm on this team."

Kageyama came up out of the bow. His jaw had gone tight. He looked at Daichi, and Daichi looked back, pleasant, and waited.

"...Yes," said Kageyama.
