"Tsukishima and Yamaguchi make 2," Daichi said, to the gym behind him. "I'll play with them. No." He turned his head. "Kiyose."

Sho had been mid-stretch on the floor. He stopped.

"You want to play, right?"

Sho's hand was already up. It had gone up on its own, the way Hinata's whole body went whenever a ball was in the air.

"Yes, Captain. Please."

Daichi looked at him a bit longer than he needed to, with something under the smile that Sho couldn't read.

"I want to see you in a real match," he said. "And I want to watch this 1 from outside. Take my spot. You're with Tsukishima and Yamaguchi."

"WHAT?" said Tanaka, from the ball cart. "He's on the ENEMY team?"

"Sorry, senpai." Sho got up and bowed. "I'll use the lesson. On you."

Tanaka stared at him. Then a slow, terrible grin spread across his face.

"Oh, you little... Good. GOOD. I'll crush you, Kiyose!"

"Please do your best, senpai."

Hinata, on the step, was looking at Sho over Daichi's arm. He looked like somebody who'd just been told where the finish line was.

"Out," said Daichi to the pair, kindly. "Go and practice somewhere I can't see you."

They went. The blond boy watched them go, and came up the step, and bowed to Daichi at exactly the right angle with nothing behind it.

---

Tsukishima Kei was 188 centimeters, which Sho found out by asking.

"Kiyose. 1st year," he said, during the stretches, because he was sitting next to him and because a 1st year who looked down at you from that height was the most interesting thing that had happened all afternoon. "Did you play middle in junior high?"

Tsukishima looked at him. It took him about as long as a blink. Sho could see him look at the smile and decide what it was for.

"Yes."

"Block a lot?"

"That's what a middle does."

"How tall are you?"

"Tall enough." Tsukishima put his headphones on, then took them off again, as if he'd remembered the captain was there. "188. Why? Are you measuring the whole team?"

"Yes. Sorry."

"Great," said Tsukishima.

"Tsukki was the best blocker at our junior high!" said Yamaguchi, from Tsukishima's other side. "He..."

"Shut up, Yamaguchi."

"Sorry, Tsukki!"

Sho was grinning and couldn't stop. 188 and a middle for 3 years, and he answered every question with the smallest piece of it he could get away with, and he'd read Sho in a blink and wanted nothing from him at all.

"I like you," Sho said.

Tsukishima's face did a small thing it hadn't decided to do. "...Please don't."

"We're on the same side on Saturday."

"I heard." Tsukishima stood up and stretched his arms over his head, which took a while, because there was a lot of him. "Try not to make it a whole thing."

---

After practice the 1st years mopped. There were 3 of them in the club now, officially, and 3 mops, so Daichi said "1 each, end to end," and Yamaguchi took off.

Sho hadn't known it was a race until he was 4 meters behind. He went after him. Yamaguchi ran a mop the way some people ran the 100 meters, head down, elbows out, and he wouldn't let Sho past on the turn, and at the far wall he slapped it with his mop and turned round with his freckled face shining.

"Won," said Yamaguchi.

"I didn't know we were racing."

"You were racing. You were racing really hard." Yamaguchi looked over at Tsukishima, who was leaning on his mop at the other end with the mop standing still under his hands. "Tsukki, I won."

"Congratulations," said Tsukishima.

Sugawara gave Sho his 10 minutes. Then Sho changed and went out, and the light behind the gym was on.

Hinata and Kageyama were on the dirt under it. Kageyama had a bag of balls and was hitting them at Hinata from 6 meters away, hard, 1 after another, and Hinata was getting in front of them, or getting a hand to them, or being hit by them, and getting up, and saying "AGAIN," and Kageyama was saying "Elbows" every time in the same flat voice.

Sho stopped by the bike racks to put his jacket on, and while he had 1 arm in it, Tsukishima and Yamaguchi came round the other end of the gym, walking toward the gate.

They stopped at the edge of the light.

Kageyama hit the next ball. It came off Hinata's arms high and wild, toward the path, and Tsukishima put 1 hand up, without even jumping, and caught it.

"Ah," said Tsukishima. "Sorry. Is this yours?" He turned the ball in his long fingers. "You're the 2 who were bowing at the door. That was very moving."

"Give it back," said Hinata.

"You're really small, aren't you." Tsukishima said it pleasantly, as if he were telling Hinata the time. "Are you really a 1st year? I thought you were someone's little brother."

Hinata's shoulders went up round his ears.

Then Tsukishima turned his head and looked at Kageyama, and smiled.

"And you're the King," he said. "The King of the Court. Everybody at the tournaments knew who you were. The setter who tossed however he liked, and yelled at his team when they couldn't keep up. I'd have liked to see what that was like from the inside. It sounded awful."

Kageyama moved. He had Tsukishima by the front of his shirt before Sho had his other arm in his jacket.

Sho's body went half a step forward and stopped there. He stood in the dark by the bikes and kept still.

Tsukishima looked down at the hand on his shirt.

"Saturday, then," he said. "Honestly, I don't care much who wins. It's a practice match. If it matters that much to you, I don't mind losing it. I could just not try very hard. Would that help?"

Kageyama let go, and Hinata ran.

He went from the edge of the light, 3 steps on the dirt, so fast it sprayed. Tsukishima had lifted the ball up over his own head, out of reach, holding it there in 1 hand the way you'd hold a toy away from a dog. Hinata jumped. He went up past Tsukishima's shoulder, past his chin, past the top of his head, and at the top he reached out and took the ball out of Tsukishima's raised hand from above.

He landed. He turned round and pointed up at Tsukishima, who stood more than a head above him.

"Don't you dare not try," said Hinata. "I'm going to spike over you. Over your head. On Saturday. So you'd better try."

Tsukishima looked at his empty hand. Then at Hinata. His mouth went flat, and then it went back to the smile, a little late.

"Let's go, Yamaguchi," he said.

They came past the bike racks on their way to the gate. Tsukishima's eyes went over Sho in the dark and didn't slow.

"Tsukishima-kun," said Sho.

Tsukishima stopped. Yamaguchi stopped a step behind him.

"Were you going to?" Sho said. "Not try."

Tsukishima looked at him. In the edge of the light his glasses had the bike sheds in them.

"Does it matter to you?"

"Yes."

"Hm," said Tsukishima, and went.

Yamaguchi stayed a beat. He looked at Sho, and then after Tsukishima, and then at Sho again, and opened his mouth, and shut it, and ran to catch up.

Behind the gym, Kageyama picked up the ball Hinata had dropped and looked at Hinata. Then he put it in Hinata's hands and walked back under the light, and Hinata followed him.

Sho went home.

---

His mother had gone to bed. A plate of ginger pork sat on the table under cling film, and there was miso soup in the pot on the stove with the lid on to keep the heat in.

Mio was on the sofa with the TV on low, pretending to be asleep. She opened 1 eye.

"Okaeri."

"Tadaima."

She sat up and grabbed his arm before he could stop her and turned it over and looked at the purple blotches up the inside of it, close, with her nose almost on them.

"Gross," she said. "They're purple. Does it hurt?"

"Only when 10-year-olds poke it."

She poked it.

"Ow."

Mio got up and went to the freezer and came back with the bag of frozen edamame wrapped in the dish towel. She dropped it on his arm, sat back down, and turned the TV up 2 notches.

Sho sat at the table with edamame on his forearm and ate his ginger pork with his other hand.

"Mio."

"What."

"Thanks."

"It's for the bet," she said, with her eyes on the TV. "If you get hurt and quit, it doesn't count. I want to beat you fair."

"Right."

"Right."

He went upstairs with the edamame still on his arm. Under the floor, he heard her switch the TV off.

In bed he lay on his back with his arm on top of the covers, still cold.

3 days till Saturday.

*Would that help?* Tsukishima had said, as if it were nothing. A match. 3 on 3, on a Saturday, with a net and a ball and people on the other side who wanted it, and his own middle blocker offering to give it away.

He lay there and counted Hinata's run-up steps until he fell asleep.

**Continuity (ch 3)**

- Revised Nov 13 (revision pass of ch 1 to 29). No status card.
- Canon: Hinata and Kageyama come at 5 a.m. after Tanaka's "nobody before 7" (ch 2); Tanaka has the key and lets them in; Kageyama refuses to toss to Hinata until he can receive and drills his receive instead; Sugawara catches them ("You asked Daichi for the key yesterday with the worst poker face") and keeps quiet; Sugawara offers to set for Hinata and Hinata refuses (he wants to beat Kageyama), with Kageyama overhearing; Hinata and Kageyama bow at the gym door and ask for a match against the 2nd years; Daichi makes it a 3v3 against the new 1st years with Tanaka on their side; the penalty (if they lose they're in, but Kageyama doesn't play setter while Daichi's on the team); Tsukishima and Yamaguchi arrive; the evening taunt (small, "King", tossing however he liked and yelling at his team), Kageyama grabs his shirt, Tsukishima offers not to try, and Hinata jumps and takes the ball out of Tsukishima's raised hand ("I'm going to spike over you").
- Divergence (ours): canon sets the challenge on the 1st day; here Kageyama asks on Wednesday afternoon at the door, with Tsukishima and Yamaguchi arriving on the path. Daichi gives his 3v3 spot to Sho and watches from outside. Sho is at all 3 canon talks. Tsukishima's words are invented (canon content only). The toss nobody went for in Kageyama's last junior high match is saved for the 3v3 (ch 4, canon). Daichi doesn't catch them at dawn; Sugawara clears them out at 6:50 ("you were never here"), so ch 4's "It's good that you all got so much morning practice in this week" still lands.
- Invented: Wednesday (2 days after ch 1). Alarm 4:30; Mom at 4:40, salmon onigiri, 3 of them; Sho reads her hands before she speaks (the salmon pot open, the umeboshi jar shut; ch 23); Sho hates umeboshi since 4, the older life ate it from the jar. "Wear the jacket. It's cold." 6 degrees. Konbini by the shrine (ch 29 notebook), the last 2 nikuman; Tanaka eats 1 and zips the 2nd inside his jacket (Soul sheet). Cherry trees still in bud. The key on its red string.
- Kageyama tosses to Sho once, a set built to fit an outside hitter; Sho is early. "...Your timing is garbage." "Yes." "I've never had 1 like that." "...Don't say weird things." Hinata: "We're both bad!"
- Tanaka's lesson, 5:15 to 5:40, off a chair: he can't explain it ("You just do that." "At the END." "You think about it way too much, Kiyose."); Sho's 3rd lands cross inside the far sideline ("Huh." "That was kind of it."); 50 more; "Yesterday. The 1 off the wall." "It was out." "I know it was out." (ch 4: "You USED my hand.")
- Receive with Sugawara: about 1 clean pass in 3 by 6:30.
- Vending machine: Sho buys Kageyama milk. "...What." "Milk. You were going to buy milk." "I have money." "I know you have money." "Why do you keep saying weird things." "Get up. We've got 10 minutes."
- Sho asks Sugawara for tosses in practice (never in a match; P4/06): "You want my tosses." "Kageyama's tosses are better." "I like yours. They come down where I can find them." "Okay. After school, at the end. 10 minutes, if Daichi says." "Your timing's still garbage, though." Daichi sees 1 hit.
- Tsukishima: "Yes." "That's what a middle does." "188. Why? Are you measuring the whole team?" "Yes. Sorry." "Great." "I like you." "...Please don't." "Try not to make it a whole thing."
- Tanaka: "Oh, you little... Good. GOOD. I'll crush you, Kiyose!" "Please do your best, senpai."
- Mop race (Wednesday; ch 6 must say Wednesday, ledger WRONG-1): Yamaguchi wins. "You were racing really hard." "Tsukki, I won." "Congratulations."
- Sho to Tsukishima at the bike racks: "Were you going to? Not try." "Does it matter to you?" "Yes." "Hm."
- Home: ginger pork under cling film, miso in the pot; Mio's frozen edamame: "It's for the bet. If you get hurt and quit, it doesn't count. I want to beat you fair." (ch 20, ch 26). Mio checks his forearms here; her door check as a habit starts later (ch 8).
- Ace line: Tanaka's wrist turn, and the 3rd ball landing cross.
- Ch 2 fix in this pass: behind the gym in ch 2, Kageyama drills Hinata's receive (he doesn't toss to him until ch 4).
- Next: Ch 4, Thursday to Saturday: Tanaka's "Early" ritual can start; Kageyama's 1st toss to Hinata; the 3v3 begins.

## Chapter 4: The King

On Thursday Tanaka was there 1st.

Sho came up the path at 4:56 with the konbini bag, and the big door of the 2nd gym was already open, with the lights on inside, and Tanaka was sitting on the step in his tracksuit with the key on its red string round his finger, watching him come.

"Early," said Sho, and bowed.

"EARLIER THAN YOU." Tanaka stood up and held out his hand for the bag. There was 1 nikuman in it, the last 1 the steamer had, a bit flat on 1 side. Tanaka looked at it, and at Sho, and took it, and went in eating it.

Hinata and Kageyama came at 5 on the dot, running, each trying to get to the door 1st.

Kageyama still wouldn't toss to Hinata. He stood on a chair at the net and spiked at him, full power, into the far corners, and Hinata chased every 1. Kageyama would hit the next before Hinata had got back. Hinata would chase that 1 too.

By 6:20 Hinata's knees were red and his shirt was stuck to his back, and he was breathing as if somebody had been sitting on him. Kageyama hit 1 more. Deep, far right, impossible. Hinata got it. He got 1 forearm under it on his way to the floor, and it popped up in a slow, ugly arc toward the net, and he lay there with his cheek on the wood, watching it come down.

Kageyama got down off the chair and caught it.

He held it. He looked at it, and then at Hinata on the floor.

"Get up."

Hinata got up.

Kageyama tossed.

The ball came off his fingers soft and high toward the left side of the net. Hinata's head went round. He ran 3 steps, jumped, and hit it. It was ugly, and it landed in.

The gym was quiet. Sugawara, at the cart, had stopped with a ball in each hand.

"Saturday," Kageyama said, to the floor. "We win."

Hinata made a sound like a kettle.

Sho was by the stage, holding a ball he'd forgotten about. He'd wanted to see that toss go where it belonged since Monday, since the step and the tissues. On Saturday he'd be on the other side of the net from it.

Hinata spun round, still red, still panting, and pointed straight at him.

"Kiyose! You're going to play seriously, right? Saturday? No going easy!"

"I wasn't going to."

"Promise!"

Sho looked down at that face, 163 centimeters of it, glaring up at him with its chin out.

"I promise," he said. "I'll try my hardest to make you lose."

Hinata's grin split his face in half. "GOOD!"

---

Tsukishima came to morning practice at 7 like everybody else, and to the dawn 1 never. On Thursday afternoon, Sho found him at the shoe lockers.

"10 minutes after practice," Sho said. "You, me, Yamaguchi. For Saturday."

Tsukishima was changing into his indoor shoes. He went on doing it. "It's a club activity."

"So is this."

"No."

"5 minutes, then."

"No." Tsukishima straightened up and looked down at him. "Why do you even care? You've been playing for a week."

Sho thought about how to answer that. There were true answers, and none of them could be said at a shoe locker on a Thursday. A desk. A clock at 3:14. 9 years of other people's Saturdays.

"I promised Hinata," he said. "That I'd try my hardest."

Tsukishima looked at him as if he'd said something in poor taste. "That's worse."

Behind him, Yamaguchi made a small noise. He looked at Tsukishima, then at Sho, then leaned round Tsukishima's arm and said, very fast, "Tsukki likes strawberry shortcake."

"Yamaguchi."

"Sorry, Tsukki."

---

The bakery by the station sold strawberry shortcake by the slice for 380 yen. On Friday Sho bought 1 on the way to school and carried it all morning in its plastic box inside his bag, and at lunch he moved it into the shade under his desk because the window row got the sun. At 4, before practice, he held it out to Tsukishima at the gym door with both hands, and bowed.

Tsukishima looked at the box for longer than he looked at most things.

"...This is bribery."

"Yes."

Tsukishima took the box. He put it on the bench by his bag, unopened, and went to warm up. Yamaguchi, passing the bench, looked at it as if it might get up and leave.

After practice, Tsukishima stayed. He stood by the net with his arms folded while everybody else rolled the balls in, and when Daichi had gone and Sugawara had gone and it was the 3 of them in the gym, he stayed 10 minutes, which Yamaguchi said afterward was 10 minutes longer than he stayed for anyone.

He spent them telling Sho how bad his receiving was.

"Kageyama will serve at you," Tsukishima said. "Every time. Every serve he gets. You're the hole."

"I know. So hide me."

Tsukishima stared at him. "You want me to receive for you."

"You and Yamaguchi split the court. I stand in the corner. I take what comes straight at the corner, and you take the rest. Then when it's time to hit something, give it to me."

"That's shameless."

Sho bowed.

Tsukishima looked at the top of his head. Then he unfolded his arms and walked to the back line and moved Yamaguchi half a meter to the left with 2 fingers, and moved himself half a meter to the right, and looked at the gap they'd made in the corner.

"There," he said. "Stand there. Move if it comes straight at you. Otherwise stay put." He pushed his glasses up. "Who's setting?"

Sho looked at Yamaguchi.

Yamaguchi looked behind himself, as if somebody else might be standing there.

"Me?"

"Tsukishima's blocking. I'm hitting. That leaves you."

"I'm a middle," said Yamaguchi. "Sort of. I've never... I've never set. Not in a match."

"You've got soft hands," said Sho. "In warm-ups yesterday, when a ball came off the cart, you caught it with your fingers, up in front of your face, like a setter."

Yamaguchi looked at his hands.

"Tsukki," he said.

"It's 3 on 3," said Tsukishima. "Nobody cares." He picked up his bag and the cake box and went to the door. "That was 10 minutes."

The door slid shut behind him.

Yamaguchi stood at the net with a ball in his hands, looking at the door.

"He's going to watch me mess it up," he said. "On Saturday. In front of everybody."

"Probably."

"Thanks."

"So mess it up now," said Sho. "Then on Saturday you'll have done it already."

Yamaguchi laughed, short, surprised. He turned the ball over. Then he threw it up to himself and set it, high and nervous and a little too far off the net, and Sho ran in under it and hit it into the floor. It was out by a meter. They both looked at where it had gone.

"Again," said Sho.

They did 30. Yamaguchi's sets went everywhere, high, low, tight, loopy, a rainbow every time. Sho was early on almost all of them, and couldn't work out why, which bothered him more than the sets. By the end Yamaguchi's sets were coming down roughly where he aimed about half the time, and he'd stopped apologizing after each 1. At 7:10 the caretaker put his head in and switched off the far lights to tell them to go home.

At the door, putting his shoes on, Yamaguchi said, "Don't tell Tsukki we stayed."

"Okay."

"He'd think it was..." Yamaguchi stopped. He tied his laces. "Just don't."

"I won't."

---

His father was reading the volleyball rules off his phone at dinner.

He'd found them on Thursday, on a website with a blue background and a lot of diagrams, and he'd been reading them in the evenings since, and tonight he'd decided to share. He held the phone at arm's length because his reading glasses were upstairs.

"'A team scores a point by grounding the ball on the opponent's court,'" his father read. "'The team that wins the rally serves.'" He looked up. "So if you win, you serve."

"Yes."

"And if you lose, they serve."

"Yes."

"Mm." He scrolled. "'A set is won by the team that 1st scores 25 points with a minimum lead of 2 points.' So 25-24 isn't enough."

"You keep going."

"How long?"

"Till somebody's 2 ahead."

"Mm." His father thought about it. "That could go on all night."

"It can."

"'The libero,'" his father read. "'A specialized defensive player who wears a different color...'"

"Eat," said his mother.

His father put the phone down next to his plate, face up, still on the page, and ate. Every so often his eyes went to it.

Mio had been watching all this with her chin on her hand.

"Are you playing tomorrow?" she said. "A real game?"

"3 on 3. It's practice."

"Are you going to win?"

"I'm going to try."

"That means no." She went back to her rice.

His father picked up his tea. "The shoes," he said, to the tea. "Tomorrow. After your game. The shop by the station shuts at 7."

"Thank you, Dad."

"Mm."

---

Saturday was gray and cold.

Hinata and Kageyama raced each other to the gym from the school gate. Sho, coming in the other way from the shrine, saw them go past the bike sheds at full speed, elbowing each other, and heard Hinata shout "I WON" and Kageyama shout "YOU DIDN'T, I TOUCHED IT 1ST," and the door bang. By 8 the whole club was inside.

Daichi clapped once. He was smiling his normal, kind smile.

"It's good that you all got so much morning practice in this week," he said.

The gym went dead.

Tanaka went the color of paper. Sugawara looked at the ceiling. Hinata made a small squeak. Kageyama stood up straighter than anybody needed to.

Daichi let it sit. Then he picked up a ball and walked to the sideline. "Warm-ups. 10 minutes."

"He knew," Tanaka whispered. "He knew the WHOLE TIME."

"Of course he knew," said Sugawara. "He's Daichi."

Then the dark-haired girl in glasses walked past the open doorway with a clipboard, the same 1 who'd sat at the side on Tuesday, and Tanaka forgot about Daichi completely. His whole body turned to follow her, like a sunflower.

"Who's that?" Sho asked.

"Kiyoko-san," Tanaka breathed. "Shimizu Kiyoko. Our manager. 3rd year. Don't look directly at her."

"Why?"

"Out of respect!"

On the bench, Ennoshita and Narita and Kinoshita started a soft chant of "baldy, baldy, baldy" that Tanaka didn't even hear.

Kiyoko sat down at the scoreboard, a flip chart on a stand, and turned it to 0 and 0, with her eyes on the chart.

---

On Sho's side of the net, Tsukishima stretched his arms over his head and looked across at the other side.

"So who do we shut down 1st?" he said, to Yamaguchi, at a normal speaking volume that carried to the far wall. "The small 1, I think. He's only got 1 thing. Once that's gone, the King's got nobody to set."

"Tsukki," said Yamaguchi. "They can hear you."

"Can they?"

Hinata could. His ears had gone red. Kageyama's shoulders had gone tight.

Daichi blew the whistle. Tsukishima served 1st, an easy float.

Tanaka passed it. Kageyama set him high on the left, and Tanaka came in fast and hard. Tsukishima went up to block, and Tanaka hit it straight down the line, past Tsukishima's outside hand, into the floor.

0-1.

Tanaka ripped his shirt over his head and swung it in circles. "HOW'S THAT, TSUKISHIMA?"

"Put your shirt on," said Daichi from the sideline.

Tanaka put his shirt on.

Then Kageyama went back to serve, and Sho found out what it was like to be the hole.

He was standing in the corner, where Tsukishima had put him. Kageyama looked at him from the end line. Only at him. He tossed, and hit it.

The serve came flat and hard and dipped at the end, straight at the corner. Sho moved his feet. Late. He got his arms on it, and it went off them sideways into the wall.

0-2.

"Nice serve," Sho said. He meant it.

Kageyama, at the end line, frowned at him as if he couldn't work out what kind of thing that was to say.

The next serve came to the corner too. Sho got it up this time, high and short, nowhere near Yamaguchi, and Tsukishima had to run under it and send it back as a free ball, and Tanaka hit the free ball off Yamaguchi's arm. 0-3.

The 3rd came to the corner as well, and Sho was there in time, and his arms were flat, and it still went off them into the net. 0-4.

After that Tsukishima stopped standing where he'd said he would. He took 2 steps to his right, into the corner, and Kageyama's next serve came there, and he passed it, flat and easy, to Yamaguchi.

Yamaguchi set Sho.

---

It was a high, loopy, nervous ball, exactly like the 30 on Friday night. Sho read it. He knew where it was going to come down. He started his approach, left, right, left, plant, and went.

He was at the top of his jump with his arm back, and the ball was still a meter above him.

He came down. The ball came down after him and landed on his forearm on the way, and dribbled over the net, and Hinata dove and dug it, and Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tanaka hit it into the floor between Sho and Yamaguchi before either of them had turned round.

0-5.

Sho landed and stood with his arms at his sides while Hinata's side cheered, and the analyst in him did the sum he hadn't been able to do on Friday night.

The timing he was using had been built for a different body. Satoshi's legs had sat down deep on the last step and jumped 68 centimeters on a good day, with a bad knee waiting. Sho's didn't sit down at all. They left quick and shallow and topped out lower, and got to the top too soon. Every approach he'd made since Tuesday had started on Satoshi's clock, and Sho's legs ran ahead of it.

So: wait. Watch the ball go up. Watch it stop. Count 1. Then go.

He tried to picture doing it. It was like being asked to hold your breath while somebody counted very slowly.

Kageyama served again, at the corner. Tsukishima took it. Yamaguchi's set drifted tight and nearly over, and Tanaka slammed it, and Tsukishima got a hand on it and sent it back up, and Hinata, going for it, ran into Tanaka, and the ball fell between them.

1-5.

"Sorry, Tanaka-senpai!"

"WATCH WHERE YOU'RE GOING!"

Yamaguchi served, a careful underhand serve that floated over the tape. Hinata passed it badly, and Kageyama had to run, and his set went to Tanaka a little too tight, and Tanaka hit it into Tsukishima's hands. 2-5.

Yamaguchi served again and put it in the net. He stood at the end line looking at the net as if it had moved.

"Sorry," he said, to the gym. "Sorry, Tsukki."

2-6.

---

Tsukishima's 1st block on Hinata came at 3-6.

Hinata served from the end line, a plain overhand that came in high and slow, and Tsukishima passed it to Yamaguchi, and Yamaguchi set Sho, and Sho was early again and hit it off his own fingertips into Hinata's side, where Kageyama dug it to the net. Kageyama set Hinata. Fast, flat, out toward the left.

Hinata ran in from the 3-meter line and jumped. He went up higher than anybody had a right to expect, high enough that Daichi's eyebrows went up on the sideline.

Tsukishima stood there.

He waited, tall and still, with his hands at his chest, and watched Hinata go up, and went up himself when Hinata was nearly at the top, late, unhurried, and put his hands out over the net. Hinata swung into them. The ball came straight back down on Hinata's side.

4-6.

"Amazing jump," Tsukishima said, landing. "Shame about the rest."

Hinata glared up at him with his mouth shut tight.

He did it again at 6-7, the same way: Hinata up, Tsukishima waiting, Tsukishima late, the ball down. Sho watched it from the corner and saw what Tsukishima was doing. He was going up last on purpose. He let Hinata spend his jump, and then put his hands where Hinata had to hit. It was cold and patient and very good.

At 7-8, while Yamaguchi chased a stray ball into the corner, Tsukishima turned to Hinata at the net as if they'd been chatting.

"You know why they call him King?" he said. "His own teammates gave him that name. In junior high. He shouted at them every rally. *Faster. Higher. Why can't you keep up.* And in his last match, they stopped jumping for his tosses. He set the ball, and nobody went. They just let it fall." He smiled. "I heard about it from someone who was there."

Hinata looked at Kageyama.

Kageyama's jaw was set. When he spoke, it came out low and flat.

"It's true," he said. "I set it, and I turned round, and there was nobody. A toss to nobody." He looked at the floor. "Then I was on the bench."

Sho, in the corner, felt that 1 land somewhere under his ribs.

"That's what scares me," said Kageyama.

Hinata blinked at him. Then he shrugged.

"That's middle school," he said. "I'm here now. I'll jump for every 1. So toss it."

Sho was 3 meters from Kageyama. He saw Kageyama's face before Kageyama could fix it.

---

Tsukishima blocked Hinata a 3rd time at 9-9, and Hinata walked back to his spot with his fists clenched. Tanaka hit 2 off Yamaguchi's arms. At 10-10, Kageyama served an ace into the corner, and Sho, on the next 1, got his feet there 1st and his arms flat and passed it, high, nearly to Yamaguchi. Yamaguchi set Tsukishima, and Tsukishima tipped it over Hinata's head into the empty middle. 11-11.

Nobody cheered the pass. Sho didn't need them to.

Yamaguchi served. Tanaka passed it, and Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tsukishima got a touch on it, and it came up off his fingers soft and high on Sho's side. Yamaguchi ran under it and sent up another nervous rainbow toward the left.

This time Sho waited.

He watched it rise. He watched it stall at the top. His whole body wanted to go, and he let it want, and counted 1 in his head, and then he went.

Left, right, left. Plant.

The ball was right there.

Tanaka was in the air in front of him, both hands up, big, a little late. Sho had it before his hand did: a big blocker, and a hitter who couldn't get past him. So use him. Hit flat and hard into the outside of Tanaka's right hand, toward the antenna.

Sho swung.

The ball cracked off Tanaka's fingertips, spun sideways, and went out past the antenna into the folded bleachers with a bang.

12-11.

It was the 1st ball he'd hit right in 2 lives that counted for anything. His palm stung. He could feel his heart in it.

"Nice kill!" Yamaguchi yelled, and then looked startled that he'd yelled it.

Tanaka shook his hand out and stared at it. Then he looked at Sho through the net, and his face went from shock to outrage to an enormous, gleeful grin.

"You used me," Tanaka said. "You USED my hand."

"Sorry, senpai."

"I DID NOT TEACH YOU THAT."

Satoshi had learned it at 19 from a grumpy setter in Osaka, who'd said *if you can't get round the wall, hit the wall.* Sho bowed anyway.

Yamaguchi served again and lost the rally to a Tanaka spike off his forearm. 12-12. Hinata served into the net. 13-12.

That made it Sho's serve.

He went to the end line with the ball. His 1st serve in a game, in this body. Satoshi's serve had been a jump topspin, hard, the kind that came off a long toss and a full approach, and Sho's arms had no idea how to do it. So he stood still at the line and tossed it low and hit a plain float, flat-handed, the serve a junior high kid hits, and it went over the tape by a hand's width and dropped in front of Hinata, who passed it straight up in the air and screamed "MINE" at it on the way down.

Kageyama set Tanaka on the left. Sho, at the net now, went up to block him, too early again, and Tanaka waited, actually waited, hanging there, until Sho's hands had started to come down. Then he hit a cross over them into the floor.

13-13.

He landed and pointed at Sho with both index fingers.

"THAT'S how it's done, rookie!"

"Please teach me that 1 too, senpai."

Tanaka opened his mouth, and closed it, and went back to his spot grinning at the floor.

---

Tanaka served. Tsukishima passed, Yamaguchi set Tsukishima, and Hinata got a hand on the attack and kept it alive for Kageyama, who set Tanaka on the left.

Sho read him: shoulders open, chest turned toward the middle of the court.

"Cross," Sho said. "He's going cross."

Tsukishima set up on the line anyway.

Tanaka hit it cross. Right where Sho had said. Into the empty floor.

13-14.

Tsukishima landed and walked back to his spot. Sho went back to his. Neither of them looked at the other.

Tanaka served again, at the corner, where Tsukishima was already standing. Tsukishima passed. Yamaguchi set Sho, and Sho watched the ball stop and counted 1 and hit it past Hinata's hands into the floor. 14-14.

Tsukishima served. Hinata passed it, high, and Kageyama set Tanaka on the left.

Sho read him again. Shoulders square this time. Hips square to the net.

"Line," he said, low.

Tsukishima went up half a step to the outside.

Tanaka hit line, straight into Tsukishima's hands, and the ball went down on Tanaka's side.

15-14.

Tsukishima landed, pushed his glasses up with 1 finger, and walked back to his spot. On the way past Sho he said, with his eyes on the floor, too low for anybody else:

"Don't do that again."

Sho looked at him.

"...Okay," he said.

"Tsukki!" Yamaguchi whispered, scandalized. "You listened to him!"

"I didn't."

"You moved!"

"I was going to move anyway."

---

Tsukishima kept serving. At 15-14 Hinata hit Kageyama's set into the net. At 16-14 Kageyama set Tanaka so fast that Tanaka was still turning round when it went past him.

The accident happened at 17-14.

Tsukishima's serve was long and safe. Tanaka passed it. Kageyama had the ball, and he hesitated, a tiny hitch in his hands, and Sho saw his eyes go to Tanaka. Then Hinata came flying in behind him from nowhere, shouting "I'M HERE! I'M HERE!" as if the gym were on fire, and Kageyama's hands changed their mind in the middle of the set.

The ball went to Hinata. Low and quick and wrong.

Hinata hit it anyway. It barely cleared the tape, dribbled off the top of the net onto Sho's side, and Sho dove for it and missed by a meter.

17-15.

"Toss to me like that again!" Hinata was yelling. "I'll hit anything! I'll jump anywhere!"

"Can you 2 even do quicks?" Tanaka asked.

"No," said Kageyama.

"No!" said Hinata, happily.

Kageyama served. Tsukishima passed, and Yamaguchi set Sho, and Sho hit it straight into Hinata's raised hands, and it dropped on his own side. 17-16.

Kageyama served again. Tsukishima passed it, Yamaguchi set him, and Tsukishima's attack came off Tanaka's block high into Hinata's side, where Tanaka dug it up to the net.

Kageyama's next set to Hinata was fast. Fast and perfect, a straight line to the spot where a hitter should be. Hinata was a step behind it, jumping at empty air while the ball went past him and out.

18-16.

"FASTER!" Kageyama yelled.

"HOW?"

"You just... you see it, and you go to the... You go where it's going to be before it gets there, and then the ball's there, and you hit it!" Kageyama made a shape with his hands in the air, a fast line, then a fist. "Like that. Bang."

"WHAT DOES THAT MEAN?"

Yamaguchi served. Kageyama set Hinata again, faster. Hinata missed it by more. 19-16.

"You're too slow!"

"YOU'RE TOO FAST!"

"If you'd just keep up, I wouldn't have to..."

Kageyama stopped in the middle of it, with his mouth still open and his hands up in the air.

*Faster. Higher. Why can't you keep up.* Tsukishima had said those words across this net 10 minutes ago, telling the story of the King and the junior high team that got shouted at until they stopped jumping. Kageyama had just heard himself start shouting them again. Sho saw it land on his face.

The match had more or less stopped round them. Tanaka stood between the 2 of them with his hands up, saying "Oi, oi, oi."

Sho could see what was wrong. Kageyama was putting the ball where a finished hitter would be, a hitter with 5 years of timing, and Hinata had a week of it and twice anybody's speed. Kageyama was setting for the hitter in his head and yelling at the real 1 for being somebody else. Sho could have said so in 2 sentences.

He breathed in, and held it, and let it out. They were on the other side of the net. He'd promised to try his hardest to make them lose.

Sugawara said it for him.

---

He came to the sideline beside Daichi and said Kageyama's name, quietly, and the shouting stopped.

"Kageyama. Can I say something?"

Kageyama turned round.

"Hinata's fast," Sugawara said. "Faster than anybody here. And he can jump higher than anybody here. That's what he's got. And you're setting the ball to a place where he isn't yet, and then telling him to get there. You're cutting off the only things he has." He said it gently, the way you'd point out a step somebody was about to miss. "I think you could put the ball where he's going to be. You're good enough to do that. I don't know anybody else who is."

Kageyama was looking at Sugawara. His mouth had gone tight.

"I'm a little jealous, honestly," said Sugawara. He smiled. "Your hands. Your control. How fast you decide. I've had 3 years. I don't have that."

Sho watched Sugawara's face while he said it. He was smiling, and it was a very good smile. Satoshi had seen that smile on a teammate in college, a 3rd-year setter, the morning the coach told him a freshman was starting ahead of him. He'd smiled exactly like that, and then he'd carried the ball cart out to the court by himself.

Kageyama stood on the court with his hands at his sides.

Then he walked over to Hinata.

"I'm jealous of you," Kageyama said. It came out stiff and low, each word dragged up. "Your speed. Your jump. You've got things I don't have. And you can't use them, because you can't do anything else yet. That's a waste." He breathed. "So it's my job. As the setter. To use them."

Hinata looked up at him.

"So go," said Kageyama. "Full speed. Jump as high as you can, to where you want to hit it. Don't even look at the ball. Just go, and swing. I'll put it in your hand."

Hinata's whole face went still, for once.

"Okay," he said.

---

19-16. Yamaguchi served again. Tanaka passed it. Kageyama, still red to the ears, set him high and safe, and Tanaka hit it into Tsukishima's hands, both of them, and it went down on Tanaka's side.

20-16.

On Sho's side of the net, Tsukishima rolled his eyes. Yamaguchi bounced on his heels at the end line with the ball, his serve again. Sho bent his knees and sank into his ready position in the corner and watched.

Yamaguchi served. Tanaka took it, a clean, ugly bump up toward the net, and Kageyama slid under it.

Hinata was already running.

Before Kageyama had touched the ball. A small orange blur tore in from the left at full speed toward a place in the air where there was no ball yet, no set, nothing at all, and his eyes were shut.

The analyst in Sho, the 1 who'd rewound 10,000 rallies, looked at that run and found nothing to hold.

**Continuity (ch 4)**

- Revised Nov 13 (revision pass of ch 1 to 29). No status card.
- Canon: Kageyama spikes at Hinata at dawn and won't toss to him; Hinata digs a deep ball on his way to the floor; Kageyama's 1st toss to Hinata; "Saturday. We win."; Daichi knew about morning practice ("It's good that you all got so much morning practice in this week"; "He knew the WHOLE TIME"); Kiyoko's walk-past and Tanaka's worship ("Don't look directly at her"); Hinata and Kageyama race to the gym; Tsukishima talks loudly about who to shut down 1st and Yamaguchi says "They can hear you"; Tanaka's 1st point down the line and shirt swing; Tsukishima blocks Hinata repeatedly, going up late; Tsukishima tells the King story mid-match (his teammates stopped jumping for his tosses in his last junior high match; he was benched); Kageyama: a toss to nobody is what scares him; Hinata: "That's middle school. I'm here now."; the failed fast sets, Kageyama's bad explanation and his yell stopping halfway; Sugawara tells Kageyama he's cutting off Hinata's speed and jump, and that he envies him; Kageyama tells Hinata he envies him, and to jump without looking at the ball ("I'll put it in your hand"; ch 5 can say "He said don't look"). Strawberry shortcake is Tsukishima's canon favorite.
- Divergence (ours): Sho plays in Daichi's spot; Tsukishima and Yamaguchi split receive to hide him; Yamaguchi sets for their side (he's never set). Kageyama's 1st toss to Hinata is Thursday (canon: the day before the match). The accidental 1st quick scores (canon: it goes out). Set scores are the fic's own.
- Invented: Thursday 4:56, Tanaka 1st with the door open; Sho: "Early." Tanaka: "EARLIER THAN YOU." (the ritual starts; ch 16, ch 27, ch 30). 1 flat nikuman. Hinata's promise: "I promise. I'll try my hardest to make you lose." "GOOD!" Shoe lockers: "You've been playing for a week." "I promised Hinata." "That's worse." Friday: the cake (380 yen, bakery by the station): "...This is bribery." "Yes." Tsukishima stays 10 minutes: "You're the hole." "So hide me." "You want me to receive for you." "That's shameless." He places them; Yamaguchi sets. Sho and Yamaguchi stay till 7:10 practicing sets (30; Sho early and can't work out why); "Don't tell Tsukki we stayed." "I won't."
- Friday dinner: Dad reads the rules off his phone (the winner of a rally serves, 25 by 2, the libero) until Mom says "Eat" (ch 23 relies on this). Mio: "Are you going to win?" "I'm going to try." "That means no." Dad: shoes after the game on Saturday, the shop by the station shuts at 7.
- Match, set 1 (3 on 3, rally scoring). Servers: Sho's side Tsukishima, Yamaguchi, Sho; Hinata's side Kageyama, Hinata, Tanaka. Tsukishima serves 1st; 0-1 Tanaka line; Kageyama serves at Sho to 0-5 (the corner, then Tsukishima moves into it; Sho early on Yamaguchi's 1st set); the timing fix (Satoshi jumped 68 cm; wait, watch it stop, count 1). 1-5 side out; Yamaguchi serves 2-5, then into the net (2-6). Tsukishima's late blocks on Hinata at 3-6 (to 4-6), 6-7 and 9-9. The King story at 7-8 ("Then I was on the bench."). Kageyama's ace at 10-10; Sho's 1st good pass in the match, Tsukishima tips (11-11). 12-11: on Yamaguchi's serve, Sho tools Tanaka's right hand past the antenna (1st real kill in 2 lives; "You USED my hand." "I DID NOT TEACH YOU THAT."; Satoshi learned it at 19 from a grumpy setter in Osaka). 12-12; Hinata serves into the net, 13-12. Sho's 1st serve: a plain standing float (Satoshi's was a jump topspin). 13-13: Tanaka waits out Sho's early block, cross. Tanaka serves: 13-14, Sho says "Cross", Tsukishima ignores it; 14-14, Sho hits past Hinata. Tsukishima serves: 15-14, Sho says "Line", Tsukishima moves and stuffs Tanaka; "Don't do that again." "...Okay." (Sho never calls Tsukishima's blocks after this; ch 6 must drop "Sho called 'line' twice and 'cross' once"); 16-14, 17-14. 17-15: the accidental quick. Kageyama serves: 17-16; 18-16, the 1st failed fast set (side out). Yamaguchi serves: 19-16; Sugawara's and Kageyama's talks; 20-16 (Tsukishima stuffs Tanaka). Yamaguchi serves at 20-16; Hinata runs before Kageyama touches the ball, eyes shut.
- Ch 5 servers from here: 20-17 Hinata; 21-17 and 22-17 Sho; 22-18 to 22-20 Tanaka; 23-20 Tsukishima; 23-21 onward Kageyama. Hinata's side serves 1st in set 2.

- Next: Ch 5, the freak quick; Emperor's Eye partially awakens.

## Chapter 5: Eyes closed

Kageyama's hands touched the ball, and the set left them going sideways.

It went fast and flat, a short hard line toward the right side of the net, toward a place in the air where nobody was. A set was supposed to climb and hang and give the hitter time to arrive. This 1 gave nobody time for anything.

Somebody was already there.

Hinata had gone before the set. He was in the air now, high, much too high, rising past the tape with his right arm already swinging, and the ball was still on its way to him.

The gym went quiet.

It happened between 1 heartbeat and the next. The squeak of shoes, the 2nd years on the bench, Tanaka's breathing, all of it went out at once, as if somebody had pulled the plug on a speaker. Sho could hear his own pulse in his ears.

And Hinata changed.

His body stayed where it was, but Sho saw it as joints. Shoulder, elbow, wrist, hips, knees: a skeleton made of points of light, and the brightest of them was the right forearm, burning white. A pale shape came off it and moved ahead of the rest. A ghost of the arm, 1 blink early, swinging down and hard across Hinata's body, toward Sho's left.

Sho was in the back right corner, where Tsukishima had put him. His legs went before he told them to, left and back, the long way across the back of the court, after the place a swing like that would reach.

They were too slow.

The ball hit Hinata's palm with a crack like a door slamming in an empty house. It went past Tsukishima's fingertips and down into the deep left of the court, and hit the floor 2 meters short of Sho's arms.

20-17.

The sound came back all at once. Shoes. Breathing. Somebody on the bench saying, "Eh?"

Sho stood with his arms still out, halfway across the back court, a long way from where he'd started. His calves knew they'd moved before the ball was hit. He could feel it in them.

Then he looked at Hinata.

Hinata had landed. He was staring at his right hand as if it belonged to somebody else. And Sho, who'd been watching his face come up past the net under all that light, said it before he could stop himself.

"His eyes were closed."

Tsukishima, at the net, turned his head. "What?"

"Hinata. When he hit it. His eyes were shut."

On the other side, Tanaka's mouth fell open. "Wait. Wait wait wait. You hit that BLIND?"

"I can't look at the ball!" Hinata said, still staring at his hand. "If I look at it, I can't stop looking at it! He said don't look, so I didn't!"

Kageyama had turned round. He stared at Hinata.

"You closed your eyes," he said.

"You said you'd put it in my hand!"

"I said don't LOOK at it. I didn't say close your..." Kageyama stopped. His mouth stayed open on the rest of it.

"Over a toss," said Tsukishima, to nobody. "That's insane."

Sho kept quiet. Satoshi had watched setters and hitters on tape for 9 years, the timing, the hours it took 2 people to trust each other, and he had seen a lot of hitters trust a set. He'd never seen 1 hand his eyes to somebody. It had taken them a week.

Tanaka came running across his own court and slapped Hinata on the back so hard he staggered. "THAT WAS AMAZING! You little monster! Do it again!"

Hinata looked down at his palm. He closed his hand slowly, and opened it again.

"It felt..." He couldn't find it. "It hit right here. Right in the middle."

On the sideline, Sugawara was smiling so hard it looked as if it hurt.

---

Hinata served. A plain overhand, high, that Tsukishima passed to Yamaguchi with a bored face, and Yamaguchi set Tsukishima, and Tanaka dug Tsukishima's hit up off the floor to Kageyama.

Kageyama set Hinata.

Sho watched Hinata the whole way up. He watched the run, the jump, the arm, with everything he had, and waited for the gym to go quiet.

The gym stayed loud. Hinata was a short kid jumping too high, and the set was perfect, and Hinata swung with his eyes shut a fraction late, and the ball hit him in the face.

It went off his cheek and over the sideline. 21-17.

Hinata landed holding his face. Kageyama, behind him, picked up the ball, and Sho caught the corner of his mouth going up.

"DON'T LAUGH!"

"I'm not laughing."

"YOUR FACE IS LAUGHING!"

Kageyama's mouth went back down. It had been there, though. Sho had seen it.

That made it Sho's serve. He went back to the line and served his plain float, and Tanaka passed it, and Kageyama set Hinata again, and Sho, coming in from the end line to the back court, watched again. The light stayed away. Hinata swung early this time, and the ball went under his fingers into the net.

22-17.

Tsukishima pushed his glasses up. "How long are you going to keep trying that?" he called across the net. "It worked once. Lucky."

Hinata glared at him. Kageyama walked back to his spot, flexing his fingers, as if Tsukishima were weather.

Sho was looking at his own hands.

Nothing since. No light, no ghost. Twice Hinata had gone up and twice Sho had watched him with everything he had, and twice he'd seen a small boy jumping too high, and nothing more.

*Observation Haki,* said a voice in his head, the part of him that had grown up on One Piece. He told it to be quiet.

---

He served again at 22-17. It went in. Tanaka passed it high, and Kageyama ran under it, and Hinata was already going.

This time it worked.

Sho was in the back right, watching for the light, and the light didn't come. He saw only what everybody saw. A fast flat set. A small body at the top of a jump that shouldn't have been possible. Hinata's arm coming round with his eyes screwed shut. A crack.

And Sho was standing still.

His body had been waiting. It had been standing there with its weight even, its feet flat, ready to go wherever the light told it to, and the light had stayed off, and his feet had stayed flat. The ball went into the floor between Yamaguchi and the sideline before he'd shifted his weight an inch.

22-18.

He looked down at his feet.

He knew what that was. Satoshi had seen it on tape in young defenders: a boy who'd been lucky once and spent the rest of the match waiting to be lucky again. You could see it in their feet. They stopped reading the hitter and started waiting for a feeling.

*Stop it,* he told himself. *Play the ball you can see.*

Tanaka served. Tsukishima passed it, and Yamaguchi set Sho, and Sho waited and counted and hit it hard and cross, and Tanaka got his arms under it, and Kageyama set Hinata from a pass that was nowhere near him, and Hinata went anyway.

Sho played the ball he could see. He watched Hinata's run, which was coming from the right this time, and his shoulders, which were turned, and he took 2 steps to his left before Hinata left the floor, because a boy running that way at that speed couldn't hit anywhere else. He was right about the direction and wrong about the depth. The ball went down beside Tsukishima's left foot, short, half a meter in front of Sho's hands.

22-19.

Closer. A lot closer. And he'd had to get there on his own.

"Stop setting to him," Tsukishima said to Kageyama on the dead ball, in the voice of somebody giving a friend advice. "You'll get bored of it eventually."

Kageyama looked at him. "No."

Tsukishima's jaw tightened a little.

Tanaka served it in, and Sho, from the back, watched Tsukishima's feet at the net while the ball was in the air. They went half a step toward the middle. Then, as the pass came up, a whole step, toward wherever Hinata was going to be.

Sho saw the gap open on the left, where Tanaka was coming. He saw it a beat before Kageyama did. He had it in his mouth: *Tsukishima, left.*

He kept it there.

Kageyama saw it too. He went high and outside to Tanaka on the left, and Tanaka came in like a falling wardrobe and hit it into the open court where Tsukishima wasn't.

22-20.

"Tsukki," Yamaguchi whispered. "They're catching up."

"I can count, Yamaguchi."

Tanaka served again. Tsukishima passed it badly, off to the side. Yamaguchi ran under it and sent up another nervous rainbow, and Sho, at the left now, waited, and counted 1, and went.

There was a small orange blocker in front of him. Hinata jumped like a boy who wanted to touch the ceiling, which meant his arms went up high and wide, and that left a gap just under his outside elbow. Sho hit low line, through it, hard.

23-20.

Hinata landed and spun round. "WHOA! Kiyose, that was so cool!"

Sho didn't answer. He was looking at Hinata's feet, at where they'd been when he took off, and at where they were now, a whole meter to the right of it. That boy went sideways in the air.

---

Tsukishima served, and then Hinata did something new.

Tanaka passed Tsukishima's serve high, and while it was in the air Hinata ran up beside Kageyama at the net. So close he could have used Kageyama's shoulder as a step. He stood there on his toes, bouncing, and Sho watched Tsukishima's eyes go from Hinata to Kageyama and back. From there, Hinata could be going anywhere.

"Yamaguchi," said Tsukishima. "With me."

Yamaguchi ran up to the net beside him. 2 blockers. That left Sho alone in the back court, with 9 meters of floor and 1 of him.

Hinata ran left. Both blockers went with him.

Then Hinata cut. He planted and turned on the spot and went right, across the front of Kageyama, eyes squeezed shut, away from both blockers, and Kageyama's hands were already moving.

The gym went quiet.

The light came back.

Hinata's skeleton flared up in front of Sho, the forearm burning, and the ghost came off it 1 blink early, swinging hard and cross, a long swing across his body. Sho had watched swings like that on tape for 9 years. His head had the place before the ghost had finished: the floor in front of the right sideline, 2 steps away.

He didn't wait.

Every coach Satoshi ever had had said the same thing. Be still when the hitter makes contact. Don't guess. A defender who moves early gets burned.

Sho moved early.

He was already falling forward when the ball left Hinata's hand, already going down toward the right sideline. There was no time to make a platform, no time for anything, so he put his forearms in the path of the swing.

It hit him.

It hit the inside of his right forearm so hard that the arm went numb to the elbow, and it bounced off at a terrible angle, straight up, high and spinning, toward the lights.

Up.

Somebody on the bench yelled "NICE!" before it had started coming down.

Yamaguchi scrambled under it and got it, barely, a clumsy push that floated over the net to the other side, soft and easy.

"CHANCE BALL!" Tanaka roared, and passed it straight to Kageyama.

And Kageyama set Hinata again.

Sho was still on the floor. He saw it from below: Hinata already in the air, eyes shut, the ball meeting his palm in that impossible place. The light stayed away this time. Sho's arms lay where they were. The ball hit the floor a meter from his face.

23-21.

He lay on his back and looked up at the gym ceiling. His forearm was buzzing. His eyes were burning and dry, as if he'd been staring into the sun, and there was a pressure behind them, a dull push, like a thumb pressing from the inside of his skull.

He'd dug it. He'd dug a ball nobody should have been able to dig, by moving before it was hit, and 2 seconds later they'd done it again, and it had made no difference at all.

He started to laugh. He lay there on the floor with his arm buzzing and laughed at the ceiling.

Tsukishima's shadow came over him. "Are you concussed?"

"Maybe." Sho wiped his eyes with the back of his wrist. They were watering.

Tsukishima looked down at him with his face perfectly flat.

"Get up," he said. "You're in the way."

Sho got up.

Yamaguchi was at his elbow. "Kiyose. That dig. That was, um." He waved a hand at the floor. "How did you know?"

Sho looked at the spot. His head throbbed once, low and slow.

"I didn't," he said, and wasn't sure if he was lying.

---

It cost him the next 3 minutes.

Kageyama served at him, of course. A plain, flat, ordinary serve, the kind Satoshi had read on tape by the 1,000.

Sho misread it.

His eyes slid off the ball. The depth went wrong: the ball looked closer than it was, then farther, then flat, like a picture of a ball pasted on the air. He stepped forward when he should have stayed, and it went over his shoulder and landed on the end line behind him.

23-22.

"Kiyose," said Daichi from the sideline. Only that.

"Sorry."

He blinked hard. The gym stayed flat. The net was a gray stripe painted on the far wall, and Tanaka was a cutout standing in front of it, and Sho couldn't have told anybody how far away either of them was. He held his hand up in front of his face and it looked the same size as the scoreboard.

"Your eyes are red," said Yamaguchi.

"Allergies."

Yamaguchi looked at him for a bit, as if he'd never heard of anybody being allergic to a gym.

Kageyama served again, at him again. Tsukishima got there 1st this time. He came across from the left in 2 long steps and put himself in front of Sho, in the corner, the place he'd told Sho on Friday to stand and not move from, and passed it, flat and easy, and went back to his own spot.

He kept his eyes front. Sho stood behind him, in the corner of a world gone flat as paper, and watched Tsukishima's back.

Yamaguchi set Sho on the left, and Sho went up for a ball that was coming down a meter from where his eyes said it was. He swung late. It hit his wrist and went into the net.

23-23.

He stood at the net with his hand stinging. *Wait,* said the analyst. *A brain that's done too much pays for it. You wait. You don't make it worse.*

Kageyama served. Tsukishima passed. Yamaguchi had the ball over his head, and Sho saw his eyes go to Sho, at the left, waiting, and go away again, and Yamaguchi set Tsukishima. Tanaka dug Tsukishima. Kageyama set Hinata. Hinata, eyes closed, a crack, and a hole in the floor that Sho didn't move toward.

23-24.

The depth came back somewhere in the next dead ball, slowly, like a camera finding focus. The net stepped back off the far wall to where it belonged. Tanaka grew a front and a back. Sho stood in the corner and watched the gym fill back in, and his eyes stopped watering, and the thumb behind them let go.

3 minutes, maybe less. It had felt like a whole set.

---

Set point. Kageyama served, and ran in behind it to the net.

It was a soft serve this time, a short 1, and Tsukishima had to come forward for it and passed it high, too high, back over the net. Tanaka dug it up on the other side, and it went to Kageyama.

Everybody in the gym was watching Hinata. Sho was. Tsukishima was, drifting toward the middle with his hands up. Hinata was already running from the right, eyes on Kageyama's hands, and Yamaguchi took a step toward him too.

Kageyama turned his shoulders and sent the ball the other way, high and lovely, to the left.

Tanaka was coming. He'd been coming the whole time, the loud 1, the 1 nobody had been watching, and the left side of the net had only Sho on it.

Sho went up. He was even on time. He got both hands up and out, high, over the tape.

Tanaka looked at them, and turned his shoulders a fraction, and hit it straight down the line past Sho's outside hand, so close that Sho felt the wind of it on his fingers.

23-25.

Tanaka landed and turned. He kept his shirt on. He looked at Sho through the net, breathing hard, and grinned.

"That's the set, rookie."

Sho bowed. His arm still hurt. His eyes still burned.

"Thank you for the lesson, senpai."

Tanaka's grin wobbled. He opened his mouth, shut it, then turned round very fast and walked off rubbing the back of his neck.

---

They had a few minutes between sets.

Sho sat on the bench with a towel over his head because the gym lights hurt. Something cold touched his hand: a bottle of water. He took it.

"Drink," said Daichi's voice.

Sho drank. Daichi stayed.

"That dig," Daichi said.

"Lucky."

"Mm." A pause. "You moved before he hit it."

Under the towel, Sho's hands stopped on the bottle.

Daichi waited. When nothing came, his footsteps went back toward the sideline.

Sho sat in the dark under the towel and listened to his own heart. It had come twice and gone twice, and both times it had been on Hinata, and the 4 times he'd watched for it, it had stayed away. He thought about telling somebody. He thought about how that would go. *Excuse me, Captain. Sometimes I see Hinata's arm before it moves. Also I'm 44, sort of.*

He kept the towel on.

The bench creaked. Somebody long had sat down at the other end of it, and Sho knew who from the sigh.

"If you're going to faint," said Tsukishima, "do it on your side of the bench."

"I'm not going to faint."

"Good." A bottle cap turned. "You stared at him the whole set. The shrimp. Every rally."

"He's the 1 scoring."

"Did it help? Staring."

Sho thought about it. "Once."

"Hm." Tsukishima drank. He sat there with the bottle hanging from 2 fingers and his elbows on his knees, looking at the far court. "In the corner. Earlier. You were standing in the wrong place."

"You moved in front of me."

"You were standing in the wrong place," said Tsukishima again, and got up, and went back to the court.

Something hit the net post beside the bench, lightly. Sho lifted the towel.

Hinata was at the post, on his own side, hanging on to it with both hands, looking at him.

"Kiyose! That dig! You were trying your hardest!"

"I promised."

"I KNOW." Hinata hung off the post, bouncing on his toes, his cheek still red where the ball had hit it. "I'm still going to win. Just so you know."

"I know."

Hinata grinned and let go of the post and ran back to Kageyama, who was standing on his own, flexing his fingers, with his eyes on the far wall. Hinata stopped beside him and started opening and closing his right hand, staring at the palm. Soon Kageyama's fingers went in the same rhythm.

Set 2. 25 more points against that, with a pair of eyes that worked when they felt like it.

Sho pulled the towel off, blinked until the lights stopped stinging, and stood up.

**Continuity (ch 5)**

- Revised Nov 13 (revision pass of ch 1 to 29). Status card removed Nov 22 (user: grounded tone, no cards).
