I didn't say close your..."); "It hit right here. Right in the middle."; the next try hits Hinata in the face and Kageyama smiles ("DON'T LAUGH!"); Tsukishima: "It worked once. Lucky." and "Stop setting to him"; Tsukishima cheats his block and Tanaka scores in the gap; Hinata stands beside Kageyama; Tsukishima pulls Yamaguchi into the block and leaves the back court; the cut-across freak quick; Tanaka scores the last point of set 1 down the line; set 1 to Hinata's side 25-23 (canon score).
- Divergence (ours): Sho (in Daichi's spot) is the 1 who notices Hinata's closed eyes. On the cut-across, where canon Daichi lunged and missed [memory, unverified], Sho digs it; Yamaguchi pushes a free ball over and Hinata scores on the next quick anyway.
- Servers (3 on 3, rally scoring): Yamaguchi at 20-16 (the 1st quick, 20-17); Hinata at 20-17 (the ball hits Hinata's face, 21-17); Sho at 21-17 (Hinata early into the net, 22-17) and 22-17 (the quick works with Sho flat-footed, 22-18); Tanaka at 22-18 (Sho reads the direction on his own, short, 22-19; Tsukishima's shifted block, Tanaka in the gap, 22-20; Sho's low line through the gap under Hinata's outside elbow, 23-20); Tsukishima at 23-20 (Hinata beside Kageyama; the cut-across; the dig; Yamaguchi's free ball; Hinata, 23-21); Kageyama at 23-21 (misread serve 23-22; Tsukishima covers Sho, Sho swings late into the net 23-23; Hinata 23-24; set point, Tanaka down the line past Sho's block, 23-25).
- Emperor's Eye, partial awakening (ledger section 2): firing 1 at 20-16 on the 1st freak quick (silence like a pulled plug, Hinata as a skeleton of points of light, the right forearm burning white, a ghost of the swing 1 blink early; from the back right corner Sho's legs go on their own, too slow; the ball lands deep left 2 m short of his arms). Firing 2 at 23-20 on the cut-across (the ghost's swing goes hard and cross; Satoshi's tape memory turns the swing into a place; Sho moves early and digs it off the inside of his right forearm, numb to the elbow). The eye shows the hitter's swing only. Cost: burning dry eyes, a thumb of pressure behind them, watering; depth gone about 3 minutes across 3 rallies (misread serve, late swing, a ball he doesn't move for; flat "like a picture", back "like a camera finding focus"). 4 times he watched for it and got nothing. He can't call it up. At 22-18 he learns the trap (waiting for it makes him flat-footed) and plays the ball he can see (22-19).
- At 22-20 Sho sees Tsukishima's block shift and the gap open, and keeps "Tsukishima, left" in his mouth (ch 4: "Don't do that again.").
- "Observation Haki" is a thought only; Sho never says it aloud (bible, ledger and handoff updated Nov 13). Tsukishima: "Are you concussed?" "Maybe." "Get up. You're in the way." Yamaguchi: "How did you know?" "I didn't." "Your eyes are red." (ch 22 echo) "Allergies."
- Sho laughs on the floor after the dig (the origin of the habit; ch 32, 33, 37).
- Tanaka: "That's the set, rookie." "Thank you for the lesson, senpai." (his grin wobbles).
- Set break: Daichi: "Drink." "That dig." "Lucky." "Mm." "You moved before he hit it." (ch 13 Kageyama, ch 35 Ikkei repeat it). Sho tells nobody. Tsukishima: "Did it help? Staring." "Once." "You were standing in the wrong place." Hinata at the net post: "You were trying your hardest!" "I promised." "I'm still going to win. Just so you know." "I know."
- Ace line: the low line through the gap under Hinata's elbow, 23-20.
- Next: Ch 6, set 2 (canon: Hinata's side wins; Hinata as the decoy frees Tanaka), the handshake, Takeda and the Seijoh match, jackets, the notebook. Ch 6 must drop "Sho called 'line' twice", "Ball landed exactly where the ghost pointed" and "It showed me exactly where the first ball would land" (the eye shows the swing). Sho's receive cost 4 points in set 1 (ch 4: 0-2, 0-3, the 10-10 ace; ch 5: 23-22).

## Chapter 6: Jackets

Set 2 began on Kageyama's serve, and Sho spent the 1st 9 points watching Hinata with everything he had.

Every run, every jump, every swing. He stared until his eyes watered. The gym stayed loud. Hinata stayed a small boy in the air, and the ball kept arriving in his palm from somewhere Sho couldn't follow, and the floor kept taking it.

At 3-6 he made himself stop. *Play the ball you can see.* He took his eyes off Hinata's face and put them where Satoshi would have put them, on the setter's hands and the hitters' feet, and played with what he had.

What he had was long arms, legs that were already heavier than they'd been in set 1, and a brain that had watched a lot of volleyball. At 8-10 that was enough for 2 good points. Yamaguchi set him high and loopy off a pass from Tsukishima, and Sho waited for the ball to stop and counted 1 and hit line past Hinata's elbow, the same gap as before. Then, on the next rally, a roll shot over Tanaka's hands, soft, into the empty middle of the court where nobody was standing.

"He's reading you," Sugawara called to Tanaka from the sideline.

"I KNOW HE'S READING ME, SUGA-SAN."

Kageyama was reading too, and he was getting better every rally.

Sho could see it from the back court. Each set to Hinata came a little cleaner than the last, the ball arriving right at the top of the jump. And by the middle of the set, Kageyama had started using him.

Hinata's side took the next 3, and at 10-13 Tsukishima stuffed a tired Tanaka, and at 11-13 Tsukishima served. Tanaka passed it high. Hinata came in from the left at full speed, and Tsukishima and Yamaguchi went with him, both of them, because they'd seen what happened if they didn't, and they were up in the air in front of him with all 4 hands out before Kageyama had touched the ball. Kageyama turned his shoulders the other way and sent a high, easy set to the right, where Tanaka was coming in on his own with the whole court in front of him and nobody up to meet him. Tanaka hit it into the floor beside Sho's foot.

11-14.

Hinata had run, and taken 2 blockers with him, and never touched the ball.

"Tsukki," Yamaguchi said. "What do we do?"

Tsukishima stood at the net with his hands on his hips, looking at Hinata, and the usual boredom had gone out of his face.

"We can't block both," he said. "So we pick."

"Which 1?"

"The 1 we can actually touch."

So they went for Tanaka.

On the next rally Tsukishima planted himself in front of Tanaka's approach and left Hinata to Yamaguchi. Kageyama saw it. He set Hinata, fast, and Yamaguchi went up alone in front of the orange blur, a beat late and half a meter short, and Hinata hit it over the top of his hands. Yamaguchi landed and stood looking at his own fingers as if the ball had gone through them.

11-15.

They went for Hinata, and Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tanaka hit it off the antenna. 12-15.

Sho kept his mouth shut. He could see Tanaka's shoulders open or square from the back court as well as he'd seen them in set 1, and twice he had *line* in his mouth, and twice he kept it there. Tsukishima picked his own way. He got a stuff block on Tanaka at 14-17, both hands, straight down, and Yamaguchi made a sound like a kettle and then put his hand over his mouth.

At 16-19 Kageyama set Tanaka again, and Sho, in the back left, read the shoulders square and took 2 steps to his left before Tanaka hit it. He was there. The ball came into his arms hard, and his platform was flat, and it went up, high, toward the net.

He'd watched a hitter's shoulders and gone, and got there on his own.

Yamaguchi set Tsukishima, and Tsukishima tipped it short into the space Hinata had left, and it was 17-19, and Tsukishima, landing, looked back at Sho over his shoulder, a short look, and turned round again.

Kageyama kept choosing, and almost every time he chose, Tsukishima had picked the other 1.

---

At 21-24, it was match point.

Tanaka served, a hard float that Tsukishima took. Yamaguchi set Sho, and Sho hit it off Hinata's hands, and Tanaka dug it up, and the ball went to Kageyama.

Sho stood in the back court and watched Hinata line up beside Kageyama, bouncing on his toes, and thought very clearly: *Come on. Come on. Show me again.*

He asked it. He stood there with his weight on the balls of his feet and his whole head turned toward Hinata, and asked.

Hinata ran. Kageyama set. Sho's eyes stayed what they were, a pair of 15-year-old eyes in a gym, and the gym stayed loud.

He moved anyway. A little left, a little right, ready for both, the way a defender does who's been told nothing. He was a step short both ways.

Hinata hit it with his eyes shut, hard and cross, the same long swing from set 1, and the ball hit the floor a meter to Sho's left while Sho was still deciding which way to fall.

21-25.

He stood there with his arms half out and looked at the ball rolling away toward the stage. Then he turned round and went to the net, because that was what you did.

---

They lined up at the net and bowed. "Arigatou gozaimashita!"

Then Hinata ducked under the net and walked straight up to Tsukishima with his hand out.

"Good game!" he said. "Let's get along! Daichi-san kicks people out if they don't get along!"

Tsukishima looked at the hand as if it had been left on a train. "You 2 got kicked out because you ignored the captain, started your own match, and knocked the vice principal's wig off. That's on you."

"That's... true, but..."

Tsukishima turned away.

Hinata lunged, grabbed his hand with both of his, and shook it hard. "GOOD GAME!"

"Let go."

"No!"

"Tsukki!" Yamaguchi hovered beside them, looking from 1 to the other.

Sho stood there and laughed until his stomach hurt. It was the 1st time since 20-16 in set 1 that his eyes felt normal.

Daichi walked over from the sideline and looked at Tsukishima, who'd got his hand back and was wiping it on his shorts.

"So," Daichi said. "How did that feel?"

Tsukishima shrugged. "I didn't really care. I figured we'd lose to the King anyway."

Daichi smiled. "You got pretty serious out there, though."

Tsukishima's mouth closed. He pushed his glasses up and looked at the far wall, and the tops of his ears went pink.

Then Daichi turned his head. "Kiyose."

"Captain."

"How about you?"

Sho thought about it. The true answer had a dead man in it, and a room in Osaka, and something that happened behind his eyes that he couldn't explain to anybody. He gave Daichi the part of it he could.

"I wanted to win," he said. "More than I thought I would."

Daichi waited.

"I was late on every quick but 1," Sho said. "And I couldn't pass Kageyama's serve."

"3 of their points in set 1 came off your receive."

"4," said Sho. "You're being generous."

Daichi looked at him a bit longer than he needed to. He'd asked a question with 2 answers and been handed 1 of them, and Sho could see him put the other 1 away for later.

"Mm," he said. "Good."

He went to talk to Tanaka.

---

Yamaguchi was sitting on the bench holding his towel in both hands, dry. He kept looking at Tsukishima and then away.

Sho sat down beside him.

"I set badly," Yamaguchi said to the towel. "You kept having to wait. If I'd been better..."

"Nobody asked you to set before Friday."

"...No."

"Every point I scored today came off your sets. All 5."

Yamaguchi turned the towel over in his hands, his eyes on it.

"I'm not good at anything," he said, fast and low, to the towel. "Tsukki's good at blocking. He's always been good. I'm just... there."

Sho thought about what to say to that. A coach would have had a speech. Satoshi had heard every speech there was, and none of them had ever helped.

"You were good at mopping," he said.

Yamaguchi made a sound that was nearly a laugh.

"I'm serious. You beat me by 4 meters on Wednesday. I think about it every day."

"You're so weird."

"You set 30 balls on Friday night," Sho said. "The 30th was better than the 1st."

Yamaguchi looked at him then. He opened his mouth, and shut it, and looked at Tsukishima at the end of the bench.

Tsukishima had his headphones on. He was looking at the floor. The volume had gone down, though, because Sho couldn't hear the music leaking out of them anymore.

---

On the other side of the gym, Tanaka had Daichi and Sugawara cornered by the ball cart.

"Did you know? Did you know they could do that? Was that the plan the whole time?"

"No," said Daichi. "We hoped they'd learn to work together. 10 plus 10. Make 20."

"And?"

"And Kageyama took everything Hinata had and multiplied it." Daichi shook his head, smiling. "10 times 10."

Sho, wiping down a ball at the cart, heard it and did the sum without meaning to. 10 times 10 was 100. He'd got 1 dig in set 1 that changed nothing, and 1 dig in set 2 that changed 1 point, and both of them had been his own.

He put the ball in the cart.

---

Then Kiyoko came in with a cardboard box.

"Shimizu," said Daichi. "Did those come?"

She nodded, set the box on the bench, and opened it. Inside, folded flat, were black jackets.

Hinata made a sound like a kettle.

She handed them out 1 by 1. Hinata. Kageyama. Tsukishima. Yamaguchi. Then she held the last 1 out to Sho, and he took it in both hands.

"Kiyose," she said.

"Thank you, Shimizu-senpai."

It was heavier than he expected. Black, with KARASUNO across the back in white.

Satoshi had owned a jacket like this once. A university 1, navy, with his name stitched on the sleeve. He'd worn it to every match for 2 years, and then to the hospital for the knee, and then for months after that, until 1 day he couldn't look at it anymore and folded it into a box at the back of a cupboard.

Sho put the new 1 on. It fit.

Hinata already had his on and was running laps of the gym with his arms out like a plane while Tanaka shouted advice about posing. Kageyama stood in his with his arms held a little away from his body, looking down at the sleeves as if they might come off if he moved.

---

The gym door banged open.

A small man in glasses and a green track jacket over a shirt and tie came in at a near run, waving a sheet of paper. His hair was a mess. He was about Hinata's height and looked about 20, though he was apparently a teacher.

"Everyone! I have news!"

"Takeda-sensei," said Daichi. "Our advisor," he added, to the 1st years.

Takeda stopped in front of them, out of breath, beaming. He bowed. Then he bowed again, as if the 1st 1 hadn't counted.

"A practice match," he said. "Against Aoba Johsai. Next Tuesday!"

The 2nd years went quiet. Even Tanaka stopped posing.

"Seijoh?" said Sugawara. "They're 1 of the top 4 in the prefecture."

"They agreed!" Takeda looked so happy about it that it was hard to look at him straight on. "I asked several times. Quite a few times. But they agreed! There's 1 condition."

He looked down at his paper.

"Kageyama-kun has to play setter. For the whole match."

Everybody turned to look at Kageyama.

Kageyama had gone stiff inside his new jacket.

Sho looked at his face, and then at the paper in Takeda's hand. Seijoh was a strong school. Kageyama had come from a strong junior high. If a strong school asked for a 1st-year setter by name, for a whole match, somebody over there knew exactly who he was.

Hinata spoke 1st. "A real match," he said slowly. "With the whole team." Then, louder: "A REAL MATCH!"

Sugawara was the 1st to speak after him.

"That's fine," he said, and smiled. "That's good, actually. It's Seijoh. They want our best setter." He put his hand on Kageyama's shoulder, briefly, and took it away. "So they'll get him."

He meant it. Sho could hear that he meant it. Kageyama stood under the hand and then without it, looking at the floor, and couldn't lift his eyes to Sugawara's face.

Takeda looked from 1 to the other, and lowered his paper. "I've been reading," he said, "about how a team is built. It said a team isn't decided on the day it plays. It's decided in all the days before, by who turns up. You've all been turning up at 5 in the morning." He blinked. "Haven't you? Sawamura-kun told me."

Later, while they were packing up, Sho saw Kageyama find Sugawara by the door and bow, low, and stay bowed. He couldn't hear what was said, and he walked the other way so he wouldn't.

---

Practice ran on till half past 5. His father was waiting at the school gate at 5:40, in his weekend jacket, with his hands in his pockets.

He looked at the new black jacket. He looked at Sho's arms under it, the purple coming out past the cuffs. He said, "Mm," and they walked to the station.

The sports shop was on the 2nd floor over a pharmacy, up a narrow staircase with posters of baseball players on the wall. It smelled of new rubber. There was a wall of shoes at the back, and a man of about 60 behind the counter reading a newspaper, who folded it when they came in.

"Volleyball," said Sho's father, before anybody could ask.

The man looked at Sho, up and down, the way a tailor looks at somebody. "Size?"

"28."

"Position?"

Sho opened his mouth. *Ace,* he'd written on the form. He'd only said it out loud on his 2nd day, to people who'd laughed or turned to face a wall.

"Wing spiker," he said. "I want to be. I jump a lot."

The man brought down 3 boxes. Sho tried on all of them, and walked up and down between the shelves, and did a small jump in the aisle that made the man say "Not in here," and stood with his weight on the balls of his feet and rocked. The 3rd pair held his ankles like hands. When he landed in them, the floor came up to meet him a little later and a little softer than it had in his school indoor shoes.

"These," he said.

His father looked at the price on the box. He took his wallet out and counted it out on the counter, slowly, in notes, and the man put the shoes back in the box and the box in a bag.

"Knee pads," said his father.

"I've got the money for those," said Sho. "From New Year's."

"Knee pads," said his father again, to the man, and the man brought down a pair in black.

On the way home, at the konbini by the shrine, Sho stopped. "1 minute," he said, and went in, and came out with a cheap gray notebook with a plain cover, 120 yen. His father didn't ask.

They walked the rest of the way with the bag between them. At the top of the hill, outside the house with the dog, his father said, "Did you win?"

"Lost."

"Ah." The dog barked. They walked on. "By how much?"

"21 to 25. In the 2nd set. We lost the 1st 1 too."

"Mm. That's 4." His father thought about it. "More than 2."

"It is."

"Mm."

At the gate his father stopped and looked at the bag in Sho's hand. "Thank you, Dad," said Sho. "For the shoes."

His father nodded at the gate. Then he went in.

---

His mother put an extra piece of grilled mackerel on his plate at dinner. Mio watched him eat it with narrowed eyes. She'd seen the new jacket on the back of his chair and the shoe bag by the door, and she was working something out.

She came into his room at 9 without knocking, in her pajamas, with a pudding cup she was clearly planning to eat in front of him.

He was at his desk. The jacket was hanging on his chair. She looked at it.

"Is that yours?"

"Yes."

"For the club?"

"Yes."

She came and touched the sleeve, as if checking it was real. She turned the cuff over and looked at the inside, at the white tag with *KARASUNO VBC* printed on it.

"Do they take it back," she said, "if you quit?"

Sho looked at her. She was looking at the tag.

"I don't know," he said. "Probably."

"Hm." She let go of the sleeve. She peeled the lid off her pudding and licked it. "So you have to keep it."

"I'm going to keep it."

"Golden Week's in 2 weeks," said Mio, and went out with her pudding.

---

When she'd gone, he sat at the desk with his hands flat on it and looked at them.

They were steady. His eyes felt fine now, just tired. He thought about the gym going quiet, and Hinata turning into points of light, and his legs moving before the ball was hit, and his own eyes, which he'd asked at match point and which had given him nothing.

His heart went fast, once, hard, against his ribs. He let it.

Then something older came up under it, from the analyst room at 2 a.m., the thing Satoshi had chased for 9 years: a pattern on the screen that nobody had noticed yet.

*Okay,* he thought. *Weird. Weird is data nobody's logged yet.*

He opened the gray notebook and wrote the date at the top of the 1st page. Under it he started to write a name, and wrote it in the wrong characters, 日名田, because he'd only ever heard it shouted, and crossed it out and wrote it again in kana. Under that he drew Hinata's run-up in arrows, 3 of them, the last 2 almost on top of each other.

> **Event 1.** Set 1, 20-16. *Hinata*, freak quick, 1st time. Gym went silent. Saw the hitter as joints, hitting forearm brightest. Ghost of the swing about 1 blink early. Body moved, too slow. Swing went deep left.
>
> **Event 2.** Set 1, 23-20. *Hinata*, cut-across freak quick. Same thing. Moved before contact. Dug it.
>
> **Cost.** Eyes dry and burning. Pressure behind them. Depth gone about 3 minutes. Misread an easy serve (point lost), late on 1 set, didn't move for 1 quick.
>
> **Never fired:** set 2, at all, including match point, when I asked it.
>
> **Pattern:** both times on Hinata. Both times on a play faster than my model of the game. Both times after I'd stopped watching for it.

He sat back and looked at it.

So it came when something outran his head. When a play was too fast for 9 years of tape, something else took over for a blink, something that didn't care what Sho wanted. He couldn't train it directly. Fine. He'd train everything round it.

> **Plan.**
>
> 1. **It shows the swing. The body has to get there.** Receiving: 300 wall passes a day. Footwork: last 2 steps, 100 reps. Before school and after.
> 2. **Read without it.** Watch the setter's hands, then the hitters' shoulders and feet. Played the ball I could see at 22-19 and 16-19. Chart 1 hitter every practice.
> 3. **Make the model faster,** so less outruns it. Old V.League recordings on the family PC. 10 minutes off every hour, for the eyes.
> 4. **Recovery is training.** 8 hours of sleep. Eat. 1 rest day a week.
> 5. **Approach.** Wait for the ball to stop. Count 1. 50 swings at the side wall every night.

He stopped at number 4 and looked at it.

Satoshi's knee had gone at 20. It had gone because a 20-year-old ace who thought he couldn't break trained through 3 months of a sore knee and told nobody, and did everything twice as hard and nothing twice as smart.

Sho underlined number 4. Twice.

At the bottom he wrote:

> Aoba Johsai. Tuesday. They asked for Kageyama by name.

The door opened again. Mio, empty pudding cup in hand.

"What's that?" She pointed at the notebook.

"A diary."

"You keep a diary?"

"Of my enemies."

She considered this. "Am I in it?"

"Page 1."

"Good," she said, and went. He heard her laughing all the way down the hall.

Tomorrow was Sunday. He set his alarm for 4:15 anyway, and looked at number 4 in the notebook, and moved the alarm to Monday. He put the new shoes, still in their box, at the end of the bed, where he'd see them. The jacket hung on the back of his chair, and the streetlight through the curtain caught the white letters across its back.

## Chapter 7: Blue castle

The meat buns at Sakanoshita cost 120 yen, and Sho had been trying to get them for 100 since last week.

"No," said the man at the register.

He was maybe 26, blond, with a headband pushing his hair back and an unlit cigarette behind his ear. The shop sat at the bottom of the hill below the school, and the whole club stopped there after practice, and he rang all of them up with the same face.

"I'm a loyal customer," Sho said. "I've been here 4 times."

"So you've paid full price 4 times. You'll manage a 5th."

"What if I buy 2?"

"Then it's 240." The man held out his hand.

Sho paid 120. The man took a bun out of the steamer with the tongs, looked at it, put it back, and took out the 1 next to it, which was bigger. He dropped it in a paper sleeve.

"Thank you very much," said Sho.

"It's 120, kid. Every time."

Outside, the club was gathered round the bench in front of the shop with their bags in a heap against the vending machine. It was Monday, a little after 6, and the light was going orange on the hill. Daichi stood in front of them with a sheet of paper folded in half.

"Tomorrow's lineup," he said. "Kageyama sets. Hinata and Tsukishima in the middle."

Hinata's head came up.

"Hinata, you're the decoy."

Hinata's head went down again. "Decoy? That's the 1 who doesn't hit."

"Ha? Decoys hit," said Kageyama, with his mouth full. He swallowed. "You run every time. If 2 of them jump with you, Tanaka-san's only got 1 in front of him. If 3 jump with you, he's got nobody. Every point he scores like that is yours."

Hinata looked at him. His head came up again, slowly, a little at a time.

"Wings," Daichi went on. "Tanaka. Me." He turned the paper over, though there was nothing on the back. "Kiyose. On the left."

Sho stopped chewing.

He knew where everybody was sitting. Sugawara at the end of the bench. Ennoshita next to him, with a bun in its sleeve that he hadn't started on.

"Tanaka and I take most of the receive," said Daichi. "Kiyose, you get the strip along your sideline and nothing else. If it's anywhere near me, it's mine. Understood?"

"Yes, Captain."

Then Daichi looked at Ennoshita.

Ennoshita was already looking at him. He had a face that always seemed half asleep, and he was smiling a little, at nothing.

"Left wing, huh," Ennoshita said to Sho. "Warm up properly. Seijoh serves hard."

"Ennoshita-san, I..."

"Mm." Ennoshita took the 1st bite of his bun. "Eat. It gets cold."

He turned and asked Narita something about the English homework. Sho sat there with his own bun going cold in his hand and half a sentence still in his mouth.

---

The club went down the road in 2s and 3s. Sugawara dropped back beside Sho for a few steps at the bridge, with his hands in his jacket pockets.

"Chikara's tougher than he looks," he said, low. Then he sped up and went to steal a piece of Daichi's bun, and Daichi let him.

Sho walked behind them to the corner, where the road split and the 3rd years went left. He'd been doing a sum since the bench, and he didn't like how it came out.

"Captain," he said. "Could I ask something?"

Daichi stopped. Sugawara looked at the 2 of them, and at the sky, and found he needed to retie his shoe a little way up the road.

"Ennoshita-san's better than me," Sho said. "At receiving. At where to stand. At nearly everything. He's been here a year. I've been here a week."

"That's right."

"So why..."

"Because of the 1 thing." Daichi put the paper away inside his jacket. He seemed to have been waiting for the question since the bench. "Seijoh's blockers are tall. Tanaka can't carry the attack by himself, and I can't hit over a block like theirs. On Saturday you hit over Tanaka and through Hinata, on your 6th day, off Yamaguchi's sets. Today was receive drills from start to finish. I want to see what you do off Kageyama's."

"I'm slow on the laps," said Sho.

"2nd to last. Every day since you came." Daichi said it the way he'd say the time. "I noticed. Seijoh will notice too, by the 2nd set. So will your legs." He looked at Sho steadily. "It's a practice match. That's what they're for. I'm finding out."

"And if I'm bad?"

"Then Ennoshita goes in for you in the 2nd set, and I've found out." Daichi smiled, the ordinary 1. "He knows that. I told him before I told you."

Sho bowed. He didn't know what else to do with it.

"Kiyose."

"Yes."

"Don't play like you owe somebody. You'll swing tight." Daichi turned up the left-hand road. "Play like you did when you told me it was 4."

---

Hinata didn't sleep on Monday night. Sho knew, because Hinata told everybody at the bus on Tuesday afternoon, in detail, with gray under his eyes.

"My sister said I was being loud. I wasn't even talking! I was just lying there!"

The bus was a rented school minibus that smelled of old seats and pine air freshener. Takeda-sensei sat at the front with a notebook open on his knee. Daichi stood at the door and counted heads twice. The 3rd years took the back. Kiyoko sat alone on the 2nd row with the team bag on the seat beside her, and Tanaka took the aisle seat directly behind her and sat very straight. Hinata had the window beside him. Sho was in the row behind them.

Hinata had an onigiri in his lap. He was holding it in both hands and hadn't unwrapped it.

Hinata turned round and knelt up on his seat. "Are you scared?"

Sho thought about it. "A bit."

"You don't look scared."

"I've been waiting a long time for a match."

"It's been a WEEK."

"It felt longer," said Sho.

Hinata looked at him as if that were a strange thing to say, and then nodded hard, as if it were also true. "I waited 3 years," he said. "For a team. A real 1, with a setter, and people who..." The bus went over the railway crossing, 2 bumps. He stopped. His face went from white to a very particular green.

Sho reached over the seat back and took the onigiri out of his hands before it could go anywhere, and put it in his own jacket pocket.

There was nothing to be done about the rest. It went into Tanaka's lap.

The bus went silent. Then Tanaka said, calmly, "Hinata."

"I'm sorry I'm sorry I'm so sorry I'm..."

"Hinata. It's fine. I've got it. Sit still."

Sho already had his bag open. He took out a folded pair of practice shorts and a T-shirt and held them over the seat back.

Tanaka stared at them. "You carry spares?"

"2 sets. It's an away game."

Tanaka took them. He held them, looking at Sho, and then he reached back over the seat and hit him on the shoulder, much too hard, and went to change behind the back row while Sugawara rubbed Hinata's back and Daichi opened every window on the bus.

When Sho went up to the front for a plastic bag for Tanaka's clothes, Takeda-sensei was writing. Sho saw the top line of the page on his way past. It said *Tanaka-kun: kind*, and it was underlined.

---

Aoba Johsai looked like money.

The gym was twice the size of Karasuno's and a lot newer, with tall windows down 1 side and a floor that shone. A white and pale green banner ran along the far wall. There was space for 4 courts and 1 net up. The team lined up inside the door and bowed. "Onegaishimasu!"

On the way to the changing rooms, 2 Seijoh players came the other way down the corridor, talking, and didn't lower their voices.

"...Karasuno? Aren't they the ones who used to be good?"

"They've got the King now. Should be fun watching him order them about."

The 1 who'd said *King* was a 1st year, Sho guessed, tall, with dark hair that stood straight up. Tanaka turned round. His face did its thing, the eyebrows and the jaw. Kageyama had stopped walking. Tsukishima said, in his politest voice, "Please go easy on us. We're only crows," and Yamaguchi stood behind his shoulder and tried to look as if he'd said it too.

"Tanaka," said Daichi.

"Sorry about that," Tanaka said to the 2 of them, with all his teeth. It was the least sorry thing Sho had ever heard.

The tall 1st year looked past all of them, at Kageyama.

"I'm looking forward to it," he said. "Seeing what kind of team lets the King rule it."

"Yeah," said Kageyama, and kept walking.

Sho was watching the tall boy when he turned to go. His mouth had gone flat, and his jaw moved once.

*He isn't enjoying it,* Sho thought. *So why say it?*

---

Hinata spent most of the warm-up in the bathroom, so the hitting lines were short a middle, and Tanaka filled the gap by going twice.

Seijoh warmed up on the other half of the court. Sho waited at the back of the line and watched their number 4.

He was a 3rd year, Sho guessed, shorter than Kindaichi by a hand, with spiky dark hair and thick forearms. He took his turn in their line like everybody else. He went up without any hurry in it, the way somebody walks up the stairs in his own house, and at the top his arm came through, and the ball went into the floor and up into the wall pads with a sound that made a Karasuno head turn on every side of the court.

"Oi," said Tanaka, beside him. "You're staring."

"Their number 4."

"I've got eyes." Tanaka was bouncing on his toes, swinging his arms across his chest. He'd changed into Sho's T-shirt, which was too long for him. "So what?"

"He doesn't hurry."

Tanaka watched the next 1. Number 4 went up, unhurried, and hit, and the ball came off the floor so hard that a Seijoh 1st year chasing it gave up and let it go.

"...He doesn't, huh," said Tanaka. He rolled his shoulders. "Whatever. I'm hitting harder than him today."

"Yes, senpai."

"Don't 'yes, senpai' me like that. Like you're humoring me." Tanaka went forward to the front of the line. "Watch this."

He ran in and hit a big 1, cross, a real Tanaka ball, and turned round grinning, and Sho nodded at him, because it was.

Sho's own turn came 4 times. The swings were all right. On the 3rd he came down on his left foot alone, with the knee bending in, and Satoshi's half of him said it as he came down: *Both feet. Knees over toes. Both feet.*

He went to the back of the line and did 10 slow arm circles each way, because Ennoshita had said to warm up properly, and watched Seijoh's number 4 take his time. Kageyama, in front of him, kept looking past Seijoh's line at their bench, at the coach, and at the doors.

---

Hinata came back from his 3rd trip looking worse. He sat down on the bench next to Sho, who was retying his shoes for something to do, and talked to his own knees.

"I met him. The tall 1. At the sinks."

"From the corridor?"

"His name's Kindaichi. He told me. He said it like a threat." Hinata screwed his face up, getting it right. "He said Kageyama was his setter at Kitagawa Daiichi. And that Kageyama only sees, like... pieces. That we're all just pieces on a board to him. And I'd find out." He pulled at the hem of his shirt. "And then Tanaka-senpai came in and said the match would do the talking. Or something. It was cooler when he said it."

He folded the hem and unfolded it.

"He's wrong," Hinata said. "Right? Kiyose. He's wrong."

Sho finished the knot. On Saturday, Tsukishima had told the whole court about Kageyama's last team, and Kageyama had said it was true, and Hinata had been standing right there.

"You hit his set with your eyes shut," Sho said. "On Saturday. And you told him you'd jump for every 1."

Hinata looked down at his own right palm.

"I did," he said. "I did say that."

Then Daichi came over and put a hand on Hinata's shoulder and told him that it was only a practice match and nobody expected anything, and Hinata heard *nobody expects anything* and went a shade paler. Then Kiyoko, on her way past with the water bottles, stopped. She tapped Hinata on the other shoulder with 2 fingers.

"We're counting on you," she said, and went on with the bottles.

Hinata stopped moving altogether. His eyes had gone round and fixed. 4 meters away, Tanaka had frozen too, staring at the shoulder Kiyoko had touched.

When they lined up at the net to bow, Sho was across from Kindaichi.

Kindaichi looked him up and down: tall, new, a plain black practice shirt with no number on it. Nobody he knew. His eyes moved on along the line to Kageyama and stayed there. Next to him, a pale boy with half-closed eyes glanced at Sho and away again, as if Sho had been interesting for about as long as a blink.

"Onegaishimasu!"

They went to their places. Sho looked back once at the bench.

Sugawara and Ennoshita sat side by side in their jackets with their hands on their knees. Ennoshita had a towel folded on his lap and a bottle of water at his feet, and he sat forward, with his elbows on his knees.

*Don't play like you owe somebody.*

Sho turned round to face the net.

---

Seijoh served 1st, and the 1st rally was over in 3 seconds.

The serve was a hard float to the middle of the court, Daichi's ball, and Daichi had his arms out and his feet set. Hinata came across in front of him from the right with his eyes on the ball and nothing else, and got 1 arm to it just as Daichi did, and it went off both of them sideways into the scorer's table.

0-1.

"Sorry! Sorry, Daichi-san!"

"It's fine. Call it next time. Or let me have it."

On the 2nd, Daichi passed it clean, and Kageyama put the fast 1 up for Hinata, the Saturday 1, the 1 that had worked. Hinata was a full beat late, with his eyes wide open, staring at the 3 Seijoh blockers. The ball went past his ear and out.

"WHAT ARE YOU LOOKING AT?" said Kageyama.

"THEM! THEY'RE HUGE!"

0-2.

On the 3rd, Kageyama went high to Tanaka instead. Tanaka came in shouting. Kindaichi slid along the net from the middle with his arms already up, and the ball went straight back down at Tanaka's feet.

0-3.

On the far side of the net Kindaichi landed and looked at Kageyama. He just looked, with his mouth flat.

Tanaka got the next high ball too, and swung at Kindaichi's hands on purpose, hard, and the ball went off the top of them and up into the balcony railing. 1-3. He turned round and pointed at Sho with both fingers. "HARDER THAN HIM."

The rotation sent Hinata back to serve. Sho was in the middle of the back row now, between him and the net. He looked over his shoulder. Hinata had the ball in both hands and was holding it like an egg. His serve went up soft and high and only just cleared the tape, and Seijoh's libero passed it with no effort at all, and Kindaichi put a quick into the floor in front of Tsukishima.

1-4. It had gone in. Hinata let his breath out so loudly that Sho heard it from 4 meters away, and Kageyama, at the net, never turned round.

---

Kageyama set him for the 1st time at 4-8.

He'd moved round to the front row on the left. The serve came to Daichi, and Daichi's pass was good, and Kageyama ran in from the back row and his hands went up and the ball came out to the left, to him.

He'd spent 3 days learning to wait. Watch the ball climb, watch it stop at the top, count 1, then go. It had worked on Yamaguchi's sets, which went up like rainbows. Kageyama's set to the left came out flat and fast and never stopped anywhere, and by the time the count in Sho's head said *go*, the ball was already dropping past the place he should have met it.

He reached it late and low and hit it straight into Kindaichi's hands. It came back down on his own toes, and he came down after it, on 1 foot. The left 1.

4-9.

"Mine," Sho said, before Kageyama could open his mouth. "Sorry. I was late."

Kageyama shut it again.

"Your sets don't stop at the top," Sho said. "Yamaguchi's did. I was waiting for it to stop."

"Why would it stop?"

"His did."

"His are bad." Kageyama said it the way he'd say the time. He frowned, working at something. "Mine's the same every time. Same height, same speed. If you go on my hands, it'll be there. Go when it leaves my hands."

"When it leaves your hands."

"That's what I said."

It was. Sho went back to his spot and said it to himself twice.

---

On the next dead ball he walked over again. Hinata, in the back row, had just run into Tsukishima chasing a ball that was Tsukishima's, and Tsukishima was telling him so, at length, and there was time.

"Kageyama-kun. Their tall middle. Does he watch you, or the hitter?"

"Me." Kageyama answered before the question was over. "Kindaichi watches the setter. He always did. At Kitagawa Daiichi, in practice, he'd be across from me in 6 on 6 and watch my hands. Every day. For 3 years. He knows which way my wrists turn before I do. If I show him the direction early, he's there. He was the best blocker we had. He went up for everything, he never..."

He stopped.

His mouth stayed a little open. It was more words in a row than Sho had ever heard from him.

Kageyama looked across the net. Kindaichi was at the net post on their side, shaking out his hands, with his back to them.

"He watches me," Kageyama said. "Every time."

*I set it, and I turned round, and there was nobody.* Sho had heard Kageyama say that on Saturday.

"Okay," he said. "Does anybody watch me?"

Kageyama looked at him. "Nobody knows who you are."

Sho went back to the left side. 4-10. The whistle went.

On the very next ball he went on Kageyama's hands, exactly the way he'd been told. The pass came off Daichi's arms, Kageyama's hands went up, and Sho was already moving, and the ball came out flat to the left and was simply there, in front of his right hand, at the top. He hit it at Kindaichi's outside hand on purpose, the way Tanaka had, and it went off the fingertips and out past the antenna.

5-10.

Kageyama looked at his own hands, and then at Sho, and nodded once.

---

It didn't save the set.

By the time Karasuno had turned the rotation 4 times, Seijoh's servers had found the strip along his sideline, and they kept putting the ball in it. He passed the 1st 1 into the bleachers. The 2nd he thought was Daichi's and Daichi thought was his, and it dropped between them, and Daichi said "Mine, next time, all of them," in a level voice, and moved half a meter closer to him.

Tsukishima, walking past to his own spot, said, "They found the hole."

On Friday, after practice, Tsukishima had spent 10 minutes telling him he was the hole, and Sho had asked him to hide it.

"I know," said Sho. "Hide me better."

"That's the captain's job," said Tsukishima, and went to the back row.

He went on Kageyama's hands every time after that. Twice his legs left before his head was ready, quick and shallow, the way they did, and he got to the top too soon and swung at air while the ball went under his arm.

The next 2 serves in his strip he passed, both of them. They were ugly, and they went up.

The rally that told him the most came at 7-17.

Seijoh served deep to Daichi, and Daichi passed it, and Kageyama set Hinata's quick, and Seijoh's libero dug it. Their setter, a 2nd year with neat hair if Sho had to guess, sent a quick to Kindaichi. Sho was on the left, and he jumped beside Hinata. That was 1. The ball came off Kindaichi's arm over both of them, and Daichi dug it up from the floor behind them. Sho landed, turned, ran back out to the 3-meter line for his approach, and Kageyama set him from the front row, high and wide to the left. He went up. That was 2, and it came off the floor shorter than the 1st. He hit it hard down the line, and Seijoh's libero dug it.

Their setter put it on the right side, Sho's side, to their tall opposite. Sho went up to block. That was 3, and he could feel the difference in it, his thighs pushing and getting less back. The ball went off his fingertips, high, and Tsukishima ran it down in the back row and bumped it up high. Kageyama got under it and set Sho again, because Sho was the 1 on the left, and there was nobody else.

That was 4. He went up a full hand lower than he'd gone on the 1st 1. He hit it cross with everything he had left in his shoulder, a real swing, the best of the set.

Seijoh's libero took 1 step and dug it as if it had been tossed to him underhand.

It went up to their setter, and their setter put it up for number 4 on the left, and number 4 went up without any hurry in it, the way he did, against Kageyama alone on the right, and hit it past Kageyama's hands into Daichi's forearms so hard that Daichi went backward off his feet and the ball went into the wall pads.

7-18.

Sho stood on the left with his hands open at his sides and his legs humming.

Number 4 had 6 kills now. When he hit it, it stayed hit. Satoshi had hit like that at 20, for 1 good season, out of a body that had been built for it over 12 years.

Sho looked down at his own right arm. It was long and it was thin, and the ball he'd thought was his best had been dug by a boy taking 1 step.

*That,* he thought, looking back at number 4. *I want that back. All of it.*

---

At 10-24, Karasuno got the serve back on a Tanaka spike off the block, and the rotation brought Hinata to the end line again.

Sho was in the middle of the back row, the same place as for Hinata's 1st serve, with Hinata behind him and Kageyama at the net in front. He looked over his shoulder.

Hinata was holding the ball in both hands, and his hands were shaking, and so were his knees. His eyes were on the other side of the net and didn't seem to be seeing it.

The whistle went. Hinata threw the ball up before the sound had finished, much too low and too far in front, and swung at it with his eyes half shut.

Sho saw the toss go up crooked and knew where a swing at a ball that low would send it. It went over his head, flat. He had time to wince.

It hit Kageyama square in the back of the head, with a sound like somebody knocking on a door.

The gym went silent. The ball rolled off toward the sideline. Kageyama didn't move. Hinata's face went the color of paper. On the Seijoh side, Kindaichi had both hands over his mouth, and the pale boy beside him had turned his face away, and his shoulders were going.

Then Tanaka made a strangled noise and folded in half, and Tsukishima turned his back on the court and put his face in the crook of his elbow, and his shoulders shook.

10-25.

Kageyama turned round slowly. Sho kept his own face still, and it took some doing.

Kageyama walked back past him to Hinata. Hinata was backing away with his hands up, saying something that was mostly vowels. Kageyama stopped in front of him. He reached up and slapped the back of his own head, hard, twice, right where the ball had hit.

"Is there anything," he said, "scarier than what you just did?"

Hinata stared at him. Sho watched him actually think about it.

"...No?"

"Then there's nothing left to be scared of. You already did the worst thing. So play normally." Kageyama's voice went up. "OR I'LL HIT YOU WITH 1!"

"YES!"

---

They had 3 minutes. Tanaka used all of them.

He sat down on the bench next to Hinata with his elbows on his knees and talked to the floor between his feet, and he wasn't loud.

"You think you have to be good," Tanaka said. "Right now. Today. Or we'll pull you off and you'll never get back on. That's what's in your head."

Hinata's hands closed on his knees.

"Listen. Everybody here already knows you're bad. We watched you all week. You can't receive, you can't serve, you run into people. Nobody put you on this court thinking you wouldn't mess up." He turned his head. "And whether you come off isn't for you to decide. That's Daichi-san's. And he hasn't. So what are you carrying it for?"

Hinata opened his mouth.

"I'm not done. When you mess up, somebody covers you. Me, or Daichi-san, or that glasses bastard, or the new guy. That's what we're there for. That's the whole reason there's 6." Tanaka sat up and pointed at the net. "Everybody on this side of the net is on your side. So quit playing like you're out there by yourself."

He stood up and stretched and went to get his water.

Sho had been standing a few steps off with a towel round his neck, keeping out of it. Hinata sat on the bench, a little straighter than before, with his eyes very wide and wet at the edges. Sho went over and took the onigiri out of his jacket pocket and put it on Hinata's knee.

Hinata looked at it. He looked up.

"Eat," said Sho.

He went and sat down at the other end of the bench. His thighs had a heat in them that went all the way down into his calves, and when he sat, they kept humming, the way a bell keeps humming after you've stopped hitting it.

Daichi was at the scorer's table, talking to Takeda-sensei. He hadn't said anything about the 2nd set. Sho hadn't asked.

Somebody pushed a water bottle into his hand. He twisted the cap, and it turned easily. It had already been loosened.

Ennoshita was beside him, with his eyes on the court. On the Seijoh side, Kindaichi was drinking from a bottle of his own while a teammate said something to him that he wasn't listening to.

"You were late on the 1st 1 and early on 2 of them," Ennoshita said. "The 1 off his fingers was good."

"Their libero dug the 1 after."

"Their libero's good. It was still a good swing." Ennoshita turned the towel over on his knee. "Kindaichi. Their tall middle. When a rally goes long, he drifts. A step, sometimes 2, toward wherever Kageyama is. Every time. He wants to be the 1 who stops him."

Sho sat with the open bottle in his hand. He'd been 3 meters from that blocker all set, busy with his own feet.

"If he drifts toward Kageyama," Sho said slowly, "he's leaving somebody."

"The left side. Yours." Ennoshita's eyes stayed on the court. "And the 1 next to him doesn't jump much. He's saving his legs for something."

Sho drank. The water was cold, and his hand round the bottle wasn't quite steady, and he didn't try to hide it.

"Thank you, Ennoshita-san."

"Mm." Across the gym, Daichi had finished with Takeda and was walking back toward the bench, and his eyes went to Ennoshita, and then to Sho. Ennoshita picked up his own bottle. "Use it."

**Continuity (ch 7)**

- Revised Nov 22 (revision pass of ch 1 to 34). Old ch 7's 1st half (Sakanoshita to the set 1 break); set 2 is ch 8 "Bottle cap". No status card (cards retired Nov 22).
- Canon: the practice match at Aoba Johsai on a Tuesday, Kageyama setting the whole match (Takeda's condition); Hinata as decoy and Kageyama's case for it; Hinata's sleepless night and throwing up on Tanaka on the bus; the 2 Seijoh players in the corridor ("Aren't they the ones who used to be good?"; Kindaichi: "the King"), Tanaka's face, Tsukishima's "We're only crows"; Kindaichi at the toilets telling Hinata that Kageyama sees hitters as pieces, Tanaka walking in; Daichi's and Kiyoko's comfort making Hinata worse (Kiyoko: "We're counting on you"); Hinata's set 1 mistakes; his serve into the back of Kageyama's head; Tanaka and Tsukishima laughing; Kageyama slapping his own head ("Is there anything scarier than what you just did?"); set 1 to Seijoh; Tanaka's talk at the break [wording invented; "everybody on this side is on your side" from memory, unverified]. Seijoh's setter in sets 1 and 2 is a backup (Oikawa absent; ch 8). Ennoshita played this match in canon.
- Divergence (ours): Sho starts in Ennoshita's spot on the LEFT wing (outside hitter, diagonal to Tanaka; user decision Nov 13: never a hidden opposite). Daichi and Tanaka take most of the receive; Sho has the strip along his sideline. Kunimi (the pale boy, unnamed to Sho) is on court (canon gives him little to do). Set 1 score 10-25 and every rally in it are the fic's own (the old draft had 13-25; nothing later cites it).
- Sakanoshita, Monday after practice: the blond man at the register (Ukai, unnamed): buns 120 yen, Sho asks for 100, "It's 120, kid. Every time."; he swaps in the bigger bun (ch 10, ch 12 rely on this). Daichi's lineup. Kageyama: "Ha? Decoys hit... Every point he scores like that is yours." Ennoshita: "Left wing, huh. Warm up properly. Seijoh serves hard." "Ennoshita-san, I..." "Mm. Eat. It gets cold." Sugawara: "Chikara's tougher than he looks."
- The corner: Sho asks why him. "Because of the 1 thing." (Seijoh's tall block; Sho hit over Tanaka and through Hinata on his 6th day). Sho: "I'm slow on the laps." Daichi: "Today was receive drills from start to finish. I want to see what you do off Kageyama's." / "2nd to last. Every day since you came... Seijoh will notice too, by the 2nd set. So will your legs." Ennoshita goes in for set 2 if Sho is bad, and Daichi told Ennoshita 1st. "Don't play like you owe somebody. You'll swing tight. Play like you did when you told me it was 4." (ch 6).
- Bus: Hinata: "Are you scared?" "A bit." "I've been waiting a long time for a match." "It's been a WEEK." "It felt longer." Hinata waited 3 years for a team. Tanaka sits behind Kiyoko; Hinata beside him at the window; Sho in the row behind them. Sho takes the onigiri over the seat back into his jacket pocket; 2 spare sets, 1 to Tanaka ("2 sets. It's an away game."); Tanaka hits him on the shoulder. Takeda's notebook: *Tanaka-kun: kind*, underlined.
- Warm-up: Hinata in the bathroom; Seijoh's number 4 (Iwaizumi, unnamed) "doesn't hurry"; Tanaka: "You're staring." "I'm hitting harder than him today." Tanaka wears Sho's T-shirt (too long for him). On his 3rd warm-up swing he lands on the left foot alone, knee bending in; *Both feet. Knees over toes.* 10 slow arm circles each way.
- Hinata's report, live: "His name's Kindaichi. He told me. He said it like a threat... pieces on a board." Sho: "You hit his set with your eyes shut. On Saturday." Sho: "You hit his set with your eyes shut. On Saturday. And you told him you'd jump for every 1." Hinata: "I did. I did say that." (ch 4: Hinata heard the King story and answered it).
- Sho wears a plain black practice shirt with no number (numbers come at Golden Week, ch 15/16; ch 9: his jacket has no number). Kindaichi looks him over and moves on; Kunimi glances. In the warm-up Kageyama keeps looking at Seijoh's bench, coach and doors (seed for ch 8's "not their real setter").
- Karasuno serve order (all match): Kageyama, Tanaka, Tsukishima, Daichi, Sho, Hinata. Set 1: Seijoh serves 1st; Karasuno's starting rotation has Sho at P1 (P2 Hinata, P3 Kageyama, P4 Tanaka, P5 Tsukishima, P6 Daichi). Score flow: 0-1 Hinata cuts in front of Daichi; 0-2 Hinata late with his eyes open ("THEY'RE HUGE!"); 0-3 Kindaichi stuffs Tanaka; 1-3 Tanaka tools Kindaichi ("HARDER THAN HIM."); Hinata's 1st serve goes in soft, 1-4 (Kageyama at the net never turns round); 2-5 side-out; Kageyama ace 3-5; 3-6; 3-7; 4-7 Daichi; Tanaka serves, 4-8; Sho's 1st set (front left, Tanaka at P1), late, into Kindaichi, 4-9 ("Mine. Sorry. I was late." / "Go when it leaves my hands."); dead ball, Kageyama on Kindaichi ("He went up for everything, he never..." / "He watches me. Every time." / "Nobody knows who you are." and Sho goes back to the left), 4-10; Sho goes on the hands and tools Kindaichi's outside hand, 5-10; Tsukishima serves, 6-10, 6-11; strip serves (1 into the bleachers, 1 dropped between Sho and Daichi: "Mine, next time, all of them"); Tsukishima: "They found the hole." "I know. Hide me better." "That's the captain's job." (ch 4, Friday after practice: "You're the hole." "So hide me."); 7-14 side-out; Daichi serves, 7-15; 2 strip serves passed; twice early, swinging at air; the 7-17 rally (Daichi at P1: Daichi passes, Kageyama sets Hinata's quick, the libero digs; Sho blocks Kindaichi's quick with Hinata, jump 1; line hit dug, jump 2; blocks Seijoh's right side, jump 3, off his fingertips, Tsukishima runs it down; cross with everything, jump 4, a full hand lower than jump 1; the libero digs it "as if it had been tossed to him underhand"; number 4 hits past Kageyama alone and takes Daichi off his feet), 7-18; Iwaizumi has 6 kills; 8-20 side-out; Sho serves 9-20, nets 9-21; 9-24; 10-24 Tanaka off the block; Hinata's 2nd serve into the back of Kageyama's head (Sho in the middle back, Kageyama at P2; Kunimi turns his face away laughing; his eyes-wide beat is saved for ch 8's apology), 10-25.
- Break: Tanaka's talk in direct speech ("Everybody on this side of the net is on your side. So quit playing like you're out there by yourself."; ch 9 quotes it). The onigiri back on his knee ("Eat."). Daichi, at the scorer's table, hasn't said anything about set 2; at the end he looks at Ennoshita, then at Sho (ch 8 opens on his call). Ennoshita's loosened bottle cap; "You were late on the 1st 1 and early on 2 of them. The 1 off his fingers was good." The read: when a rally goes long, Kindaichi drifts a step or 2 toward Kageyama, leaving the left side (Sho's); the 1 next to him "doesn't jump much. He's saving his legs for something." (Kunimi; ch 29). "Use it." His hand on the bottle isn't steady and he doesn't hide it.
- Body (Nov 22 decision, shown): thighs and calves heavy and humming by the end of set 1; jump fades over a long rally (4th jump a hand lower than the 1st); lands on 1 foot (left) in the warm-up and on the 4-9 swing; the best cross of the set dug easily (strength).
- No eye events. Sho's read of Hinata's serve is plain (the crooked, low toss).
- Ace line: watching Seijoh's number 4: *That. I want that back. All of it.* (Satoshi hit like that at 20, for 1 good season.)
- Review fixes (Nov 22): Hinata heard the King story in ch 4 (no false withholding); numbered bib cut; "hole" scene is Friday; 7-17 rally gets Karasuno's attack; warm-up jump fade cut (fatigue belongs to the match); decoy pitch de-mirrored; Tanaka's line says "the net"; narrator explanations and half the similes cut; cappers cut ("Good.", "Shimizu. That was the opposite.", the 3-years callback at the onigiri); bun price; Kindaichi doesn't drink mid-set; balcony and wall pads (no seats); Seijoh years marked as Sho's guesses.
- Next: Ch 8 "Bottle cap", set 2.

## Chapter 8: Bottle cap

They changed ends. Sho carried his bottle round the net post with the cap still loose, and put it down by the Karasuno bench on the new side, and stood up, and found Daichi looking past him at the bench.

Ennoshita had sat down again the way he'd sat all set, with the towel on his knee. He stayed sitting and lifted his own bottle about 3 centimeters off his knee, toward the court, and put it down again.

Daichi turned back. "Same 6," he said.

It was settled in the time it took Tanaka to retie his shoe, and nobody else on the team seemed to have noticed it happen. Sho went out to the left side with his legs still humming from set 1, and put his hands on his knees, and looked at the floor until the humming went down to something he could play on.

*He hasn't used it yet. Give him the set.* That was what the bottle had said. Sho was fairly sure of it.

---

Karasuno served 1st. Tsukishima's serve was a tidy float to the middle that Seijoh's libero passed without moving his feet, and Kindaichi put the quick down past Hinata's hands, in front of Tanaka, before anybody had finished taking a step.

0-1.

On the next 1, Seijoh served at Daichi, and Daichi's pass went off his arms toward the sideline. Kageyama came running out of the back row after it. He got under it on the run, off balance, and pushed it toward the net for Hinata, and it came out high, much higher than the fast 1, and Hinata, already in the air with his eyes shut and his arm going, swung at nothing and came down with the ball still above him.

It landed on Karasuno's side. 0-2.

Across the net, Kindaichi said something to Hinata. Sho caught his face, which was enough: *Here it comes. Now he screams at you.*

Kageyama walked over to Hinata.

Sho watched Hinata brace. His shoulders came up round his ears.

"That 1 was too high," Kageyama said. "My fault. I'll fix it."

Hinata's shoulders stayed up. His mouth opened.

On the other side of the net, Kindaichi's face went completely blank. The pale boy beside him opened his eyes all the way and looked at Kageyama, and then at Kindaichi, and then back at Kageyama.

Kindaichi was still holding the look he'd made for Hinata, and nobody was using it, and he didn't seem to know what to do with his face. Then he turned round and walked back to his spot, fast, with his head down.

*He went up for everything,* Kageyama had said. Sho watched the back of Kindaichi's neck go red.

The next set to Hinata was the fast 1. Daichi passed it, Kageyama's hands went up, and Hinata was already running, eyes shut, before the ball had left them. It came out flat and low and arrived in his palm at the top of his swing, and he hit it straight down into Seijoh's court in front of the 3-meter line before a single Seijoh blocker had left the floor.

1-2.

Hinata came down from it with both hands up and his whole face open, and looked round at all of them. At Tanaka, who was roaring. At Daichi. At Kageyama, who was looking at his own hands. At Sho, who nodded at him, because there wasn't anything to say that his face hadn't already said.

---

After that, Hinata ran.

He ran at every set, and Seijoh's blockers had to go with him, because the 1 time they didn't, he scored. On the very next rally Sho got to feel what that meant from the inside.

Daichi was serving. Seijoh's attack came off Hinata's block hand, and Daichi dug it up from the back row. Kageyama got under it at the net. Hinata went, and Kindaichi went with him, and so did the blocker beside him, and for the 1st time all day Sho ran in on the left and looked across the net and saw 1 pair of hands. Kageyama put the ball out to him, flat, on time. He went when it left Kageyama's hands, the way he'd been told, and hit it past the lone blocker's outside hand into the floor.

2-2.

"That's YOURS, Hinata!" Tanaka shouted, and Kageyama nodded.

At 3-3, Sho served. He put a plain float in, deep, and Seijoh sided out on it. On the next rally Hinata went again, with 2 blockers chasing him, and Tanaka came in behind the fake on the left and hit into a net with nobody standing at it. 4-4.

"HARDER THAN HIM!" Tanaka bellowed at Seijoh's number 4, who had nothing to do with that rally and looked faintly puzzled.

---

At 4-5, while the referee waited for a ball to come back from under the balcony stairs, Kageyama stood at the net with his hands on his hips, frowning across it.

"That's not their real setter," he said.

Tanaka looked at him. "Huh?"

"This 1's good. He's careful." Kageyama was watching the Seijoh setter, the neat-haired 1, retie his knee pad. "He sets the safe thing. Seijoh's real setter never sets the safe thing."

Daichi had come up beside them. "You're sure?"

"Yes."

Sho had something too. It was small, and he said it anyway.

"Their coach keeps looking at the door," he said. "Every few rallies. And there's 1 more bottle on their bench than there are people."

They all looked over. The Seijoh coach, an older man with a calm, lined face and his arms folded, turned his head toward the gym's main doors, as if he'd been asked to, and turned back. On the end of the Seijoh bench, a little apart from the others, sat a full bottle that nobody was drinking from.

Kageyama went stiff all down his back, the way he did when somebody praised his sets.

"Who is it?" said Tanaka.

"Somebody from my middle school," said Kageyama, and went back to his spot, and stood in it with his eyes on the doors until the whistle went.

---

The read came at 8-7, in a long rally.

Seijoh served. Daichi passed, Kageyama set Tsukishima, Tsukishima's quick was dug by the libero, and Seijoh's setter put it up for their opposite, who came in from behind the 3-meter line on the right. Sho went up to block him. That was 1 jump. The ball went off Sho's fingertips. Daichi got it. Kageyama set Daichi in the middle of the court, a high ball, because Daichi was where the ball could reach, and Daichi hit it into the block and it came back to Karasuno's side. Tsukishima covered it. Up again. Somebody shouted "1 more!" and Sho realized it had been him.

8 touches. 9.

Sho was breathing through his mouth now. He'd been thinking about his own feet for half an hour. He made himself think about somebody else's.

He watched Kindaichi.

Kageyama had come out of the back row to run down Tsukishima's cover, and he was on the right side of the court, near the antenna. On the other side of the net, Kindaichi's feet moved. 1 step, then a half step, toward the right. Toward Kageyama. It was so small that if Ennoshita hadn't said it out loud, Sho would have taken it for somebody shifting his weight.

The left side opened. Sho's side.

In front of Sho there was 1 blocker now, the pale boy. And the pale boy had just watched a 9-touch rally go past him without leaving the floor once.

Kageyama saw it too, or he saw Sho, or both. The ball came out to the left, fast and flat, and Sho was already moving, because he'd been going on Kageyama's hands for 20 minutes now and his body had finally stopped arguing about it. He went up. It was a lower jump than he wanted. His thighs gave him what they had.

The pale boy went up late, and not very high, with his arms out in front of him like somebody holding a door for a stranger.

Sho hit it high off the top of his fingertips, hard, and it went off them and out over the Seijoh bench and bounced off the wall behind it.

9-7.

He came down on both feet. He'd been doing it on purpose all set, *knees over toes*, and it cost him half a beat getting back, and he did it anyway. Then he turned round and lifted 1 hand at the Karasuno bench as he walked back to his spot.

On the bench, Ennoshita lifted his bottle 3 centimeters off his knee. Sugawara, beside him, said something, and Ennoshita said "Mm," and kept his eyes on the court.

At the net, the pale boy shook out his hand and looked at Sho with the same mild, sleepy attention as before.

Sho was still breathing hard. He'd gone up 3 times in that rally, and the boy hadn't gone up once, and the boy didn't look tired at all.

"You're coasting," Sho said.

The boy thought about it. "...And?"

Then he went back to his spot.

Sho liked this 1. Satoshi had known pros like that, the ones who worked out to the gram what they needed to spend.

---

Seijoh noticed. It took them 3 rallies.

At 10-8, with Karasuno receiving, Seijoh's setter said something to Kindaichi with his hand over his mouth, and after that Kindaichi stopped drifting. He stayed in the middle where he belonged, and he waited, and the next time Kageyama set Sho on the left, Kindaichi was there beside the pale boy, 2 pairs of hands, and he stuffed Sho straight back down onto Karasuno's floor.

10-9.

That was 1 thing. The other thing was Sho's legs.

On the next rally he blocked, and landed, and ran out for his approach, and blocked again on the transition, and landed on both feet the way he'd decided to, and ran out again, and when the set came to him he went up and knew in the air that it was short. A full hand short. He tipped it, because tipping was all he could reach, and the libero was waiting under it as if he'd been told.

10-10. Then 10-11, on a Seijoh serve that took the tape and dropped in front of Hinata.

Daichi came over at the next dead ball, while the Seijoh server was bouncing the ball.

"Kiyose. Legs?"

At 20, a coach had asked Satoshi about his knee in almost that voice, in another gym, and Satoshi had said *fine*. He'd said it all that spring.

"Heavy, Captain," Sho said. "Since about 7-all."

Daichi nodded. "Then pick your jumps. You're jumping with Hinata on their quick. He's got it. You're going up on balls you can't reach." He went back to the right. "Save them for the left."

*Pick your jumps.*

Sho stood on the left and looked across the net at the pale boy, who was standing in his spot with his arms loose.

*Stealing is learning,* he thought, and almost laughed.

For the next 4 rallies he only jumped when he could reach. When Seijoh's quick went to the middle and Hinata was already up with the hitter, Sho stayed down and turned and got ready to dig. When the ball went to Seijoh's right side, where he could actually get his hands on it, he went. He felt the difference in his thighs almost straight away. There was a little more left in them each time he did go.

At 12-12, Kageyama set him on the left again. Kindaichi had gone round to the back row by then. Their other middle and the pale boy were both there. Sho went up, and it was a better jump than the last 1, and he looked at the 4 hands in front of him and hit the ball hard off the pale boy's outside hand, the 1 he held out late, and it went off the hand and out past the antenna.

13-12.

The pale boy looked at his hand, and then at Sho, and his eyes were a fraction more open than they needed to be.

"...Huh," he said.

Tanaka came past on the way to his spot and hit Sho on the back, hard, the way he did everything.

"That shot was mine, you know," Tanaka said. "Off the late hand. I've been doing that all set."

"Yes, senpai. I copied it."

Tanaka opened his mouth and shut it again.

"Next 1's mine," he said, and went.

At 14-13 the next 1 was his, a high ball on the left, and he hit it off the end blocker's hands so hard it went into the balcony, and he turned round and pointed at Sho with both fingers, and Sho bowed.

---

Tsukishima and Kageyama had their argument at 15-14.

Seijoh's number 4 hit from their left, against Karasuno's right side, where Kageyama was blocking at the net with Tsukishima beside him. Kageyama went up on the line. Tsukishima went up a step inside, on the cross. There was a gap between them a ball wide, and number 4 hit it through the gap, and it went into the floor behind them.

15-15.

"You were late," said Kageyama.

"You were on the line," said Tsukishima. "I was on the middle. That's where the middle goes."

"He always hits the line from there."

"He hit the gap, King. Which you made."

Kageyama turned and walked back to the net with his jaw set. Tsukishima pushed his glasses up and went to his own spot. Sho, standing in the middle of the back row between them, decided he had never wanted less to be asked for his opinion.

The next ball went to number 4 again. Same side. Same set, high and outside.

Sho watched number 4's approach the old way, the way he'd watched hitters on tape for 9 years. Number 4 came in from outside the sideline, wide, and that made the cross easier for him than the line. His hitting hand went all the way back and disappeared behind his head. Full swing. No tip coming.

Kageyama and Tsukishima went up together this time, shoulder to shoulder, 4 hands on the line with no gap between them. Neither of them had said a word about it.

Sho moved before the ball was hit. 2 quick steps left and back, into the lane the cross would have to use.

Number 4 hit it cross. It hit Sho's forearms dead in the middle, hard enough to sting all the way to the elbows, and went up, high and clean, back toward the net.

It was a read, the plain kind he'd made from a desk chair more times than he could count, and his feet had got there 1st.

"NICE RECEIVE!" Hinata screamed from the back corner, as if it were his.

Kageyama ran under it and set Tanaka on the left, and Tanaka hit it straight down into the floor in front of the 3-meter line.

16-15.

Number 4 landed and looked across the net, past the block, at the back row. At Sho. He frowned. It was an honest frown, a player's frown, the frown of somebody who'd hit that ball cross a lot of times and seen it come back up less often than he liked.

Sho looked back at him with his forearms stinging. That hand going back behind the head, all the way, so nobody could see what it was going to do until it did it. Satoshi used to have that. *I'm having that again,* he thought.

---

Seijoh's coach called a timeout at 18-15, after 2 of Kageyama's serves in a row that Seijoh couldn't pass.

In the huddle, Hinata talked fast. Everybody else was drinking. Hinata had a bottle in his hand and had forgotten it.

"In middle school, I didn't have a team," he said. "Not really. I had 3 1st years and 2 guys we borrowed from other clubs. So I had to do everything myself. Receive, and set, and everything. And I was bad at all of it." He looked round at them, at Tanaka and Daichi and Kageyama, and even at Tsukishima, who was looking at the ceiling. "But here I don't have to. I can just jump. And trust you guys to do the rest."

Daichi smiled at him. It was the real 1, the 1 that reached his eyes. "That's a real weapon, Hinata."

Sho stood at the back of the huddle with a towel round his neck, watching Kageyama. Kageyama was looking down at his own hands while Hinata talked, turning them over.

*I waited 3 years,* Hinata had said on the bus. *For a team.*

Sho drank. The water had gone warm.

---

After the timeout Tanaka went back to serve and put 2 in a row deep into the corner, hard and flat and angry, and Seijoh passed both of them off the court. 21-16. He served the 3rd into the net, 21-17, and stood at the end line with both hands on his head, saying something to himself that Daichi pretended not to hear.

Seijoh came back with 2 straight points off their right side, both over a block Sho got to late, with his hands still rising when the ball went past them. 21-19. Then Daichi got a high ball on the right and put it off the tape and down, 22-19, and Tsukishima went back to serve.

His 1st serve Seijoh passed short, and Hinata put the overpass down. 23-19. His 2nd hit the tape, rolled along it, and fell on Seijoh's side, and Tsukishima looked at it as if it had embarrassed him. 24-19.

Set point. The careful setter did something that wasn't careful for once. He set number 4 a ball so fast and low that Hinata had no time to close the block, and number 4 hit it straight down beside Daichi's feet.

24-20.

Then Seijoh's server went at Sho.

The 1st 1 came hard and flat at his left hip. He got his arms to it and it went up, a little toward the net, and Kageyama ran it down and set Daichi, and Seijoh's libero dug Daichi's hit, and their opposite put the return into the gap behind Hinata. 24-21.

The server went at him again. Sho stood in his strip and watched the toss and knew his legs were slow before he'd moved them. It took the tape on its way over and dropped, and he lunged for it, and Daichi lunged for it from the other side, and it landed in the space between their 2 sets of arms, closer to his.

24-22.

Sho stayed down on 1 knee for a breath longer than he needed. His thighs had gone from humming to a dull steady ache, the kind that meant there were not many good jumps left in them. When he got up, his left knee wanted to come up last. He made it come up 1st.

Kageyama walked past him toward the net, and stopped.

"Can you go?" Kageyama said. He said it low, to the floor, and it took Sho a beat to understand that he was asking about Sho's legs.

"Once," said Sho. "1 good 1."

Kageyama looked at him. Then he looked across the net, at the pale boy, who was standing in front of Sho's lane on the left, and at Kindaichi in the middle, back in the front row again.

"Run it," Kageyama said. "All the way. Like it's yours."

He went to his spot.

Seijoh served. It came to Daichi, and Daichi passed it high and perfect into the setter's spot. Sho was already going. He ran his full approach on the left with everything his legs had left, the long last step, arms back, and he made his eyes go to Kageyama's hands and his whole body say *me, me, it's mine.*

The pale boy jumped with him.

He jumped properly, for the 1st time in the match, a real jump, high, with his arms out over the net and his eyes on Sho. Whatever he'd been saving his legs for, he spent 1 on Sho.

Sho was at the top of the best jump he had left, with his arm back, when he heard the sound to his right. The small flat slap of a ball hitting a palm.

Kageyama had set Hinata.

Hinata had gone, eyes shut, before Kageyama's hands had touched the ball, and Kindaichi had been watching Kageyama's hands, the way he always had, and the ball was past his arms and in the floor before he'd left the ground.

25-22.

Sho came down on both feet, and his legs folded, and he sat down on the floor of Aoba Johsai's gym with his arms on his knees and laughed.

Across the net, the pale boy landed a meter from him, on his own side, and looked at the ball, and at Hinata, and then down at Sho.

"Tch," he said, to nobody. It was the most effort Sho had heard him put into anything.

1 set each.

Hinata ran a lap of their half of the court with his arms out. Tanaka chased him. Daichi let them. On the 2nd time round, Hinata stopped in front of Sho, who was still on the floor, and grabbed both his hands and pulled. Sho came up about halfway and stuck there.

"They jumped with YOU!" Hinata said. "The sleepy 1 jumped! Did you see? He went right up!"

"I saw. I was there."

"So now you know!"

"Know what?"

"What it's like!" Hinata was still holding his hands, bouncing on his toes, his face bright red. "Everybody goes with you, and you don't even get the ball, and it's still yours! It's the best!"

Sho got the rest of the way up. His thighs told him exactly what they thought of it.

"My legs are gone," he said.

"Mine too!" said Hinata, as if that were good news, and let go of him, and ran off to tell Kageyama something at full speed.

---

At the bench, Sho took 2 bottles out of the crate. He twisted the cap of each 1 open and shut again, the way it had been done for him, and gave 1 to Ennoshita.

Ennoshita looked at the bottle. He tried the cap with his thumb. It turned.

"Mm," he said, and drank. Then, in the same voice: "You came down on both feet. On the 1 off his fingers."

"Yes, senpai."

"You didn't, in set 1."

"No."

Ennoshita nodded and turned the towel over on his knee.

Sho sat down beside him. It was the 1st time he'd sat down since the set started, and his legs went on aching as if they hadn't noticed.

"Ennoshita-san," he said. "Before the set. Captain looked at you."

"Mm."

"You could have said yes."

Ennoshita drank again, and screwed the cap back on, and looked at the court, where Hinata was still explaining something to Kageyama with both arms.

"You'd only just found it," he said. "The left side. It'd be a waste." He turned the bottle round in his hands. "And I'd have had to warm up again."

Sho looked at him. Ennoshita's face was as half asleep as it always was.

"Thank you, senpai."

"Stop thanking me. It's embarrassing."

On Ennoshita's other side, Sugawara had watched all of it with his chin on his hand.

"He's been watching your feet all set, you know," Sugawara said to Sho. "Not even the ball. Your feet."

"Suga-san."

"What? You have." Sugawara smiled at Ennoshita with his eyes, and Ennoshita looked at the ceiling, and Sugawara turned back to the court looking pleased with himself.

---

Then the screaming started.

It came from the balcony. Girls, maybe 10 of them, all at once, leaning over the rail and waving. Sho looked up, and then followed their eyes down to the doorway.

A tall boy in a Seijoh jacket had just walked in. Brown hair, arranged. He waved up at the balcony with both hands and a huge smile, and the screaming got louder. When he took the jacket off, he had a green bib on over his T-shirt, and the number on it was 13.

He walked straight to the Seijoh bench and bowed to the coach, who looked up at him, and away from the door at last.

"Ah, you're back," the coach said. "How's the foot?"

"All better, sir."

"Don't overdo it. Take a long warm-up."

"Yes, sir."

He picked up the bottle at the end of the bench, the 1 nobody had been drinking from, and drank from it.

"Who," said Tanaka, with feeling, "is that nice-looking guy?"

Kageyama answered with his eyes still on him.

"Oikawa-san. My senpai at Kitagawa Daiichi. Their captain. He's their real setter." A pause. "I learned to serve from watching him. And to block." Another pause. "His personality's worse than Tsukishima's."
