"Hey," said Tsukishima.

Hinata stared across the gym with enormous eyes. "So he's like... the King's king. The Great King."

Nobody corrected him. Kageyama looked as if he'd have liked to and couldn't find the words.

Oikawa started stretching on the sideline behind the Seijoh bench, chatting to Kindaichi, smiling, waving at the balcony every so often. Then, bent over his own knee, he looked up across the net.

His eyes went to Kageyama 1st, and stayed there, and the smile didn't change at all, which was worse than if it had.

Then they moved along the line. Hinata. Tanaka. Daichi. Tsukishima. They went over Sho without stopping, the way you'd skim the name of a stranger in a list.

Then they came back.

It was a small, polite, curious look. It went to Sho's face, and then down to his legs, and then back up, and Oikawa smiled at him exactly the way he'd smiled at the balcony.

Sho looked back at him. The back of his neck prickled, once.

Satoshi had looked at people like that for 9 years, through a monitor, with his thumb on the rewind button: a quick, friendly pass over a whole team, to see who would break 1st.

*He watches like I do,* Sho thought.

Oikawa stood up, and stretched his arms over his head, and went to take a ball from the cart. Sho sat back down on the bench next to Ennoshita, and his legs went on aching, and he watched Oikawa's hands.

**Continuity (ch 8)**

- Revised Nov 22 (revision pass of ch 1 to 34). Old ch 7's 2nd half (set 2 and Oikawa's arrival), rebuilt and slowed. No status card (cards retired Nov 22).
- Canon: Kageyama apologizes for a high set ("That 1 was too high. My fault. I'll fix it."), and Kindaichi and Kunimi are stunned; the quick starts working and Hinata as decoy frees the hitters; Kageyama says Seijoh isn't using its real setter; Kageyama and Tsukishima block together; Hinata's timeout talk (no real team in middle school, did everything himself; "trust you guys"); set 2 to Karasuno; Oikawa walks in to the balcony girls' screams, in a green #13 bib over his T-shirt (wiki history: the green #13 bib), bows to Seijoh's coach, who tells him not to overdo it; Kageyama: Oikawa is his Kitagawa Daiichi senpai, their captain and real setter, he learned serving and blocking from watching him, "His personality's worse than Tsukishima's." "Hey." [fic wording]; Hinata: "the Great King". Coach's "How's the foot?" and the injury are [unverified; kept off the knee]; "Don't overdo it. Take a long warm-up." is fic wording.
- Divergence (ours): Sho plays all of set 2 on the left wing. Ennoshita, asked with a look (ch 7 ends on it), lifts his bottle 3 cm and Daichi keeps "Same 6". Oikawa arrives after set 2 (canon: during set 2, just after Kageyama notices the setter; moved). Score 25-22 and all rallies are the fic's own.
- Set 2 rotation: Karasuno serves 1st, Tsukishima at P1 (P2 Daichi, P3 Sho, P4 Hinata, P5 Kageyama, P6 Tanaka). Flow: 0-1; 0-2 high set, Hinata swings at air, the apology ; 1-2 the quick lands (Kindaichi's neck goes red; *He went up for everything*); 2-2 (Daichi serving) Sho kills with 1 blocker left after Hinata pulls 2 ("That's YOURS, Hinata!"); 2-3; 3-3; Sho's float, 3-4; 4-4 Tanaka behind the decoy into an empty net ("HARDER THAN HIM!"); 4-5, dead ball: Kageyama: "That's not their real setter... He sets the safe thing. Seijoh's real setter never sets the safe thing."; Sho: "Their coach keeps looking at the door... And there's 1 more bottle on their bench than there are people." Kageyama goes stiff: "Somebody from my middle school." (Oikawa drinks from that bottle on arrival); 5-5; 6-5; 6-6; 7-6; 8-6; 8-7; **9-7 the read** (9-touch rally; Seijoh's opposite attacks from behind the 3-meter line, Sho blocks, jump 1; Kindaichi drifts 1 and a half steps toward Kageyama at the right antenna; Sho on Kageyama's hands, lower jump; Kunimi late and low; off his fingertips over the Seijoh bench); Sho lands on both feet on purpose (all set); lifts a hand to the bench, Ennoshita lifts his bottle; Sho went up 3 times, Kunimi none, and isn't tired: **"You're coasting." "...And?"**; 10-7; 10-8; Seijoh's setter tells Kindaichi to stop drifting, and Kindaichi stuffs Sho, 10-9; Sho's 3rd jump of a rally is a full hand short, tipped, 10-10; 10-11 off the tape; **Daichi: "Kiyose. Legs?" "Heavy, Captain. Since about 7-all." "Then pick your jumps. You're jumping with Hinata on their quick. He's got it... Save them for the left."** (Satoshi at 20 told a coach *fine* about his knee all spring); Sho copies Kunimi: jumps only where he can reach (*Stealing is learning.*); 11-11; 12-11; 12-12; 13-12 off Kunimi's late outside hand (Kindaichi now back row; Seijoh's other middle and Kunimi block) ("...Huh."); Tanaka: "That shot was mine, you know." "Yes, senpai. I copied it." "Next 1's mine."; 14-12; 14-13; 15-13 Tanaka off the end blocker into the balcony, points at Sho, Sho bows; 15-14; 15-15 Iwaizumi through the gap between Kageyama (line) and Tsukishima (inside), their argument ("You were late." "...That's where the middle goes." "He hit the gap, King. Which you made."), unresolved, both walk off; **16-15 the dig**: they block line together without a word; Sho (P6) reads number 4's wide approach and full swing, moves 2 steps before contact, digs the cross; Tanaka kills; Iwaizumi's frown; the hitting hand hidden behind the head: *I'm having that again.*; 17-15, 18-15 on Kageyama's serves; Seijoh's timeout; Hinata's talk ("3 1st years and 2 guys we borrowed from other clubs", matching ch 1); Daichi: "That's a real weapon, Hinata."; Kageyama turns his hands over; 18-16; 19-16; Tanaka serves 20-16, 21-16 (deep corner), nets 21-17; 21-18, 21-19 off Seijoh's right over Sho's late block; 22-19 Daichi off the tape; Tsukishima serves: 23-19 Hinata on the overpass, 24-19 tape ace; set point; 24-20 (fast low set to number 4, Hinata can't close); 24-21 (Sho passes, Daichi's hit dug, opposite behind Hinata); 24-22 (off the tape between Sho and Daichi; Sho on 1 knee; his left knee "wanted to come up last. He made it come up 1st."); **Kageyama (asking about his legs): "Can you go?" "Once. 1 good 1." "Run it. All the way. Like it's yours."**; Sho's full approach as the decoy, Kunimi jumps properly with him for the 1st time; Hinata's quick past Kindaichi: **25-22**. Sho's legs fold; he laughs on the floor; Kunimi: "Tch."
- Hinata pulls Sho halfway up: "They jumped with YOU!... So now you know!... Everybody goes with you, and you don't even get the ball, and it's still yours!" "My legs are gone." "Mine too!"
- **The bottle cap:** Sho loosens 2 caps and gives 1 to Ennoshita (repaying ch 7). Ennoshita: "You came down on both feet. On the 1 off his fingers." "Yes, senpai." "You didn't, in set 1." Sho: "Before the set. Captain looked at you... You could have said yes." Ennoshita: "You'd only just found it. The left side. It'd be a waste. And I'd have had to warm up again." "Thank you, senpai." "Stop thanking me. It's embarrassing." Sugawara: "He's been watching your feet all set, you know. Not even the ball. Your feet." (ch 9: Ennoshita's bus reads include the left foot turning out). Sho still owes Ennoshita (ch 9 melon bread).
- Oikawa's look: Kageyama 1st, then the line; it skims Sho, comes back, goes to his face and legs. *He watches like I do.* (Satoshi's quick, friendly pass over a whole team on a monitor, to see who'd break 1st; ch 17's Kenma line "He knew that look..." is left to ch 17). Last image: Sho on the bench beside Ennoshita, watching Oikawa's hands. Ch 9: Oikawa's long warm-up, shopping for receivers; at 24-21 he serves at Tsukishima (canon).
- Body (Nov 22): legs heavy from set 1; lower jumps on the left; both-feet landings cost half a beat and he keeps doing them; jump 3 of a rally a hand short; "pick your jumps" works (a little more left each time); thighs a dull ache by 24-22; legs fold after the last point. Measured: Daichi "Since about 7"; 1 good approach left at set point.
- No eye events (the dig is a read).
- Ace line: *I'm having that again.* (the hidden hitting hand), and the decoy run "like it's yours".
- Review fixes (Nov 22): Seijoh rotation (Kindaichi back row at 12-12; the 8-7 opposite attacks from the back row); landing "all set" (ch 7: 1 foot in set 1); Oikawa can't know the strip; "didn't X, he Y" lines cut; the narrator's Kunimi essay and Kindaichi aphorism cut; set point told point by point; Tanaka's serve run bridges the timeout; bench scene grown (Ennoshita, Suga); mirrored "inside/outside" pair cut; the bib; the empty bench item is a bottle (2 jackets otherwise).
- Next: Ch 9 "Grand King", set 3: Oikawa's warm-up and scouting; Oikawa's pinch serves on Tsukishima at 24-21; Karasuno win 25-23.

## Chapter 9: Grand King

Oikawa warmed up for the whole of set 3.

The coach had told him to take it slow, and he took it slow. He stretched on the strip of floor behind the Seijoh bench. He jogged a lap of the free half of the gym. He bounced a ball, caught it, bounced it again, and every few minutes he looked up at the balcony and waved, and the balcony screamed.

And every time Karasuno received a serve, his head came round.

Sho noticed it on the 4th rally. Daichi passed, and Oikawa's eyes went to Daichi's arms. Tanaka passed, and they went to Tanaka's. Hinata ran into a serve with his elbows bent and sent it into the net, and Oikawa, bent over his own knee, smiled at the floor.

*He's shopping,* Sho thought.

That was what Satoshi would have done, with a tape and a remote. Watch who receives what, and how, and where it goes when it goes wrong, and write a list. The list was always short. It was always the same 2 or 3 names.

Sho's own name was going to be on it. He'd known that since the strip along his sideline had started filling up with Seijoh serves in set 1.

At 3-4 the server put 1 in his strip. Sho passed it, a little high, a little toward the net, and Kageyama took it. At 5-6 another came, flat at his hip, and it came off his forearms and went straight into the scorer's table.

Behind the Seijoh bench, Oikawa's eyes followed it all the way in. Then he went back to stretching his calf.

---

Sho's legs had come back about halfway over the break.

He'd sat on the bench with the bottle Ennoshita had loosened and his hands on his thighs, and the humming had gone down, and for the 1st few rallies of set 3 he could jump nearly as high as at the start of the day. By 8-7, that was gone again. His thighs remembered the 2 sets they'd already played, and told him so on every approach.

So he couldn't hit over them. Seijoh's blockers were taller than him with his legs fresh, never mind now. He'd have to go through them, or off them.

At 9-8, Kageyama set him on the left, and Kindaichi and the blocker beside him went up together. Sho went up into the best jump he had, which wasn't much. The tape was somewhere around his eyes. In front of him were 4 hands, and the outside hand of the end blocker was a little apart from the rest, turned in.

He hit the ball at that hand. Hard and flat, at the fingertips. It went off them sideways and out toward the scorer's table.

10-8.

He came down on both feet, and it cost him half a beat getting back, and he did it anyway.

At 12-11, with Daichi serving, he did it again, off the other blocker's hand, and the ball went out toward the Karasuno bench, and Ennoshita moved his feet so it wouldn't hit them. 13-11.

The 3rd 1 was harder.

Daichi served at 13-11 and won 1 more, 14-11. Then Seijoh sided out, 14-12, and won the next on their own serve, 14-13, and Karasuno were receiving with Daichi at the back right and Sho at the net. Seijoh's serve came to Tanaka. Tanaka passed it a little long, and Kageyama had to step back off the net to get under it, and Sho saw his shoulders square to the left before the ball reached him.

Sho went. He knew before he left the floor that it was short. The ball came out to him a little low because Kageyama had been off balance, and Kindaichi was already there in front of him, tall, with his arms all the way over the net, and the blocker beside him closing in.

The block was tall and closed. The 1 thing he could reach was Kindaichi's outside hand, the 1 nearest the antenna, held a little stiff, a little turned.

Sho hit it at that hand with everything his shoulder had. It went off Kindaichi's little finger and out past the antenna, high, into the balcony rail.

15-13.

Kindaichi came down and shook his hand out, and looked at it, as if it had done something without asking him.

3 points, and not 1 of them over anybody. Satoshi would have hit those down into the floor. Sho had hit them off people's fingers, because that was where his legs could get him to.

---

At 16-all, Karasuno were receiving with Hinata at the back right, and the whole front row was Kageyama, Tanaka and Tsukishima.

Seijoh served deep. Daichi passed it, and Kageyama set Tanaka on the left, high, and Tanaka hit cross, hard, and Seijoh's libero dug it up off his chest. Their setter put the ball up for number 4 on their left, high and outside, the ball he'd been giving number 4 all afternoon.

Sho was in the middle of the back row. He had the whole thing in front of him.

Kageyama went across to the right to block, and Tsukishima came across from the middle to close next to him, and in set 2 that was where the gap had been, a ball wide, with the 2 of them arguing about it afterward. This time Tsukishima kept coming. He came all the way, until his shoulder was against Kageyama's, and they went up together with 4 hands over the net and no light between them.

Number 4 hit it straight into them. It came straight back down on Seijoh's side, at number 4's own feet.

17-16.

The Seijoh bench groaned out loud. Kageyama landed and turned away and walked back to his spot. Tsukishima landed and pushed his glasses up and walked back to his. Neither of them said a word about it, and Tanaka, between them, looked from 1 to the other as if he were waiting for a fight, and then grinned, because there wasn't 1.

After that the score went up in ones and twos. Kageyama served 2 points, the 2nd off the tape, and then Seijoh sided out, 19-17. Tanaka put Karasuno back on serve with a cross into the open court, 20-17, and served 1 into the corner and 1 into the net, 21-18. Seijoh's number 4 scored twice in a row, 21-20. Daichi scored on a high ball off the block, 22-20, and Tsukishima went back to serve. His 1st serve Seijoh passed short, and Hinata put the overpass down. His 2nd took the tape and fell on Seijoh's side, and Tsukishima looked at it as if it had embarrassed him.

24-20.

Then Seijoh sided out on a tip that dropped behind Hinata. 24-21.

Match point. Seijoh's rotation came round, and the pale boy walked to the end line to serve.

The Seijoh coach lifted his hand.

The pale boy turned round, saw who was coming on, and walked off the court with no visible feeling about it. Oikawa walked on, waving.

---

He took a ball from the referee's stand and walked back behind the end line, and kept walking. 4 big steps. He stopped, turned, and bounced the ball 3 times.

Then he lifted 1 hand and pointed across the net.

Sho, in the front row in the middle, had his weight on his toes already. He'd been getting ready for this since 5-6. The strip. The 1 who'd sent the ball into the scorer's table.

Oikawa was pointing at Tsukishima.

"You can have all the attacking power in the world," Oikawa said to him. "But if the receive doesn't connect, it doesn't mean anything."

Tsukishima's face didn't change. His hands, Sho noticed, went flat against his thighs.

Sho ran it back, fast. Oikawa had watched him shank 1 into the table. He'd watched Tsukishima pass all set, and Tsukishima's passes had mostly gone up. And Tsukishima had a face that gave nothing away, all day, every day, and Oikawa had just pointed at it in front of the balcony. If it broke, the whole team would see it break.

*He picked the 1 who'd mind most,* Sho thought.

Some small, shameless part of him was a little offended. The rest of him was taking notes.

The whistle went.

Oikawa tossed with his right hand. High, about 2 meters over his head, and out in front of his hitting shoulder, so that he'd have to go and get it. 4 steps, getting longer and faster. He went up into the air behind the end line and hit the ball at the top of his jump with his whole body behind it.

The sound was a flat crack. The ball came over the net with heavy topspin, low and fast, and at the last instant it dove, and Tsukishima got his arms on it, and it went off them sideways and out over the sideline.

Oikawa landed a meter inside the court and was already smiling.

24-22.

On the bench, Sugawara said something to Ennoshita, and Sho, at the net, heard the end of it: "...stronger than Kageyama's. And he puts it where he wants."

Sho kept his eyes on the net and ran it back at quarter speed, the way Satoshi would have. Satoshi had seen that serve on tape 1,000 times, from the best servers in the V.League. He'd never seen a high school kid toss it that clean.

Oikawa got the ball back and walked to the same spot. 4 steps. 3 bounces. He pointed at Tsukishima again.

This time Sho watched nothing but the toss.

Same hand. Same height. The same place in the air, as if somebody had hung it there on a thread. The ball came over and hit Tsukishima's forearm and went somewhere useless.

24-23.

The gym was loud now. The balcony was screaming. The Seijoh bench was on its feet. On the Karasuno side it was quiet enough that Sho could hear Tsukishima breathing. Sho kept his eyes on the net.

Hinata looked.

"Hey!" Hinata shouted across the net. "Great King! Aim at me instead!"

"Shut up," said Tsukishima.

"No! Everybody on this side of the net is on your side!" Hinata looked back over his shoulder at Tsukishima, red in the face and completely serious. "That's what Tanaka-senpai said!"

Tanaka made a choked sound. When Sho glanced over, he had a hand pressed flat to his chest.

Tsukishima's jaw tightened, and he looked at the floor in front of his feet, and set them again.

Sho had a suggestion ready. He'd had it since the 1st serve. *Spread out. Give him less to hit. Make him take power off to find his man.* He opened his mouth.

"Spread out," Daichi said, calm. "Hinata, Tsukishima, Tanaka, Kiyose. Wider. I'll take more court."

They spread out. Daichi slid over until he was covering almost half the back of the court by himself, his arms already out.

Sho closed his mouth.

He'd been about to say it. Daichi had said it 2 seconds earlier, with no tape, no 9 years of other people's matches, nothing. A captain had looked at his own team and known where the hole was.

Oikawa looked at the new shape from behind the end line, and his smile changed, a little, and he bounced the ball 3 times.

He served.

It was softer. Only a little. To get the ball past Daichi's arms to Tsukishima, he'd had to take some of the power off it, and Tsukishima got his arms under it and it went up. It went high, and floated, and drifted back over the net, slow and easy.

A free ball. For Seijoh.

"Chance ball!" someone on their side shouted.

It went to the setter, and the setter went to Kindaichi, and Kindaichi came in hard and swung on Karasuno's left, where Hinata was already up. Hinata got the tips of his fingers on it, and the ball slowed and dropped, and Tanaka dug it up out of the back court, ugly and high.

Hinata hit the floor and kept going. He ran the whole width of the net, left to right, past Sho and past Daichi's back, toward a place where there wasn't any ball yet.

Kageyama was under the ball. His eyes flicked right, once, the way they always did, and the set went out fast and flat toward the right antenna, and Hinata was already there, already in the air, with his eyes shut.

The ball went past Oikawa's left ear and into the floor.

25-23.

---

Sho stepped back. His body did it before he'd decided to. At 20, on the good days, he'd done that for a teammate who'd made the last play: stepped back and given him the court.

Hinata was screaming with both arms up, and Tanaka was screaming with him, and Kageyama stood very still with his mouth fighting something it was losing.

Sho looked across the net.

Oikawa hadn't moved. He was standing where he'd come down after the dig he hadn't made, turned halfway toward the spot where the ball had hit the floor. He was smiling. It was small and sharp, a different thing from the face he gave the balcony, and it was pointed at Kageyama.

---

They lined up and bowed. "Arigatou gozaimashita!"

Karasuno ran across to bow to the Seijoh bench, and ran back to bow to their own, and Sho found himself standing next to Ennoshita in the line. He stepped half a pace back, so that Ennoshita was in front of him. Ennoshita looked round at him once, and faced front again.

Sugawara, passing behind them with the ball bag, reached out and flicked Sho in the forehead.

"Ow."

"Don't make that face at Chikara," Sugawara said, low. "He's fine." Then he was gone, helping Daichi with the bag, and Sho rubbed his forehead and didn't know what face he'd been making.

Tsukishima was at the water bottles on his own, at the end of the bench.

Sho went over for his own bottle. Tsukishima drank, put his down, and spoke before Sho had opened his mouth.

"Don't."

Sho had been going to say something. He couldn't remember now what it had been. Something about the 3rd serve, probably, which Tsukishima had passed, and which had turned into the last point.

He picked up Tsukishima's towel off the floor, where it had fallen, and put it on the bench next to his bottle.

"Bus is at 6," he said.

"I know when the bus is."

Tsukishima put his headphones round his neck and picked up his bag and went toward the door. He left his bottle on the bench, next to Sho's.

Sho picked up both of them and carried them out.

In the corridor, Takeda-sensei had them all stand in a line by the bag pile, and gave a speech.

"I don't know very much about volleyball yet," he said. "I'm learning. But I watched something today that I think I'd have understood even if I'd never seen a match in my life." He was holding his notebook shut against his chest. "Hinata-kun and Kageyama-kun are like a chemical reaction. 2 substances that are quite ordinary on their own. And you put them together, and you get something that wasn't there before, something new, and it gives off light and heat, and you can't put it back the way it was. I think that's what a team is. I think I watched 1 start today."

He stopped. He'd gone pink to the ears.

"I'm sorry. That was too poetic."

"No, that was good, sensei," said Hinata, who had understood maybe half of it.

Before Takeda closed the notebook, Sho saw a word on the open page, underlined twice. *Coach.*

---

Oikawa was waiting at the gate.

He was leaning on the gatepost with his jacket over his shoulders, and when Karasuno came out he pushed off it and smiled at all of them. Hinata and Tanaka puffed up like a pair of cats. Kageyama stopped walking.

"Hey, hey. Don't look at me like that," Oikawa said. "I came to say good game."

He turned to Hinata. "Shrimpy-chan. That jump of yours. And the speed. And that quick." He shook his head, slowly. "Amazing. Really."

Hinata started to glow.

"Your receive's terrible, though," Oikawa went on, in exactly the same voice. "And so is the glasses-kun's. You saw what happened when I served at him."

At the back of the group, Tsukishima put his headphones on.

"If you don't fix that before the Inter-High prelims, there isn't much point turning up," said Oikawa. He tipped his head toward the gym behind him. "And next time it's official, we'll be playing with everybody, from the very start. So."

Then he looked at Kageyama.

"Tobio-chan," he said. "Next time, I'll crush you properly."

Kageyama's mouth stayed shut, and his hands were fists at his sides.

Daichi stepped forward. "You're right about the receive," he said, calmly. "It's going to get a lot better. 1 of our members is coming back soon." He smiled his ordinary smile. "Our Guardian Deity."

Hinata's head snapped round. "Our WHAT?"

Oikawa looked at Daichi with his eyebrows up, interested, and seemed about to go.

Up the path, a broad, short-haired boy in a Seijoh jacket had stopped walking to wait for him. Sho knew the shape of him before the face: number 4.

Sho stepped forward.

He'd decided somewhere around 24-22, while he was watching the toss for the 2nd time.

He bowed, a little too deep.

"Please teach me your jump serve," he said. "I'll pay."

The whole of Karasuno went silent.

Oikawa looked at him. The smile stayed exactly where it was. Behind it, his eyes shut down, like a door closing in a lit house. It was there and then it wasn't, a half beat at most.

Kageyama was right beside Sho, close enough that Sho felt him go stiff. When Sho glanced at him, Kageyama was looking at Oikawa, and his face had gone very young, and very careful.

"And you are," said Oikawa, "Karasuno's..." He looked at Sho's jacket, which had no number on it. "Tall-kun?"

"Kiyose."

"Kiyose-kun." Oikawa said it as if he were holding it up to the light. "I'll have forgotten that by dinner."

"That's fine," said Sho. "I'll remind you at the prelims."

Oikawa's smile got wider.

"Then watch closely at the prelims, Kiyose-kun," he said. "I'll show you my serve. From very, very close."

"OIKAWA."

Number 4 came down the path, took Oikawa by the back of the collar without slowing down, and turned him round.

"Iwa-chan! I was being nice!"

"You were being you." Number 4 looked over his shoulder. His eyes went to Kageyama 1st, and stayed there a beat, and his mouth went hard, the way a mouth goes when it remembers something. Then they went to Sho. "Great," he said. "There's 2 of them now."

Then, to the rest of Karasuno, with a short real bow: "Sorry about him."

He walked Oikawa away up the path. Oikawa was still complaining. Number 4 didn't let go of the collar.

Kageyama let his breath out. He turned to the team. "Sorry," he said, stiffly. "About what he said."

Daichi waved it off.

Tanaka turned on Sho with his eyes enormous. "You just ASKED him? To his FACE?"

"He said no."

"He didn't say no!"

Sho picked up his bag. Kageyama fell in beside him on the walk to the bus, and was quiet for most of it.

"He won't teach you," Kageyama said.

"Did he teach you?"

Kageyama walked 3 more steps.

"...I watched," he said.

---

On the bus, Sho sat next to Ennoshita.

He'd bought 2 melon breads from the vending corner by Seijoh's front door. He tore 1 in half, unevenly, and held out the bigger half. Ennoshita took it.

They ate. The bus pulled out past the gate, where nobody was waiting now. The sky had gone orange and then a darker orange, and the streetlights were coming on along the main road.

"Ennoshita-san," said Sho. "What else did you see? From the bench."

Ennoshita looked at him sideways.

"In set 3," said Sho. "Anything. I was on the court. I couldn't see anything that wasn't on top of me."

Ennoshita chewed, and swallowed, and looked out of the window at the dark shops going past.

"Their libero cheats toward the line," he said. "When Tanaka's in the front row. Tanaka hit 2 down the line in set 3, and after that their libero stood half a meter over before every serve."

"So the cross is open."

"So the cross is open. Tanaka didn't notice. I'll tell him tomorrow. He'll want to try it 40 times." Ennoshita took another bite. "Kageyama looks at Hinata before the quick. Only before the quick. Just his eyes. Their blockers didn't catch it."

"They will."

"Mm. Somebody will." Ennoshita turned the paper wrapper round in his fingers. "And you."

Sho waited.

"When you're nervous about a ball coming to you," Ennoshita said, "your left foot turns out. On your 1st step. Before you've even started running."

Sho stopped chewing.

"Do I?"

"Mm. In set 1, mostly. Less after."

Sho sat with the bread in his hand. Satoshi had watched other people's feet for 9 years, through a screen, in slow motion. Nobody had ever watched his. He looked down at his left shoe on the bus floor as if it might do it now, with nobody to see.

"Thank you, Ennoshita-san."

"Mm." Ennoshita looked out of the window again. The river went past under the bridge, black, with the lights in it. When the bridge was behind them, he said, in the same voice, "Were you scared? On the left. In set 1."

Sho thought about lying, and didn't. "Yes."

"Mm."

"Were you?" said Sho. "Last year. When you played."

Ennoshita was quiet for so long that Sho thought he hadn't heard.

"Last year I wasn't out there much," Ennoshita said. He folded the wrapper in half, and in half again, very small. "Eat your bread."

Sho ate his bread.

Somewhere past the river, Takeda-sensei turned round in his seat at the front with his notebook open.

"Kiyose-kun. Sorry. What do you call it when the ball touches the block and goes out? Whose point is that? I was trying to write it down."

"Block-out," said Sho. "It's the attacker's point. The block touched it last."

"Block-out," Takeda repeated, writing. "Block-out. Thank you."

He turned back round. Sho looked at the back of his head, and then leaned forward over the seat.

"Sensei. I can draw it for you, if you like. With the angles."

Takeda turned round so fast his glasses slid. "Could you?"

Sho drew it in the back of Takeda's notebook, with the bus going over every bump in Miyagi. Ennoshita watched over his shoulder and pointed at 1 of the arrows and said, "That 1's wrong," and it was. By the time they got back to Karasuno, there were 4 diagrams, and Takeda had underlined *block-out* twice, right under *coach*.

---

It was a short walk home from school, and it had never been longer.

His legs had stiffened on the bus. On the hill they went from aching to something heavier, a slow, deep tiredness that started at the knees and went all the way up.

Mio opened the front door before he'd touched it.

"Did you quit yet?"

"We won," said Sho.

Mio looked at his face. Then her eyes dropped, very fast, to his knees, and then to his forearms, and came back up to his face as if she'd only been looking for a sock.

"It was probably luck," she said.

"Definitely luck."

He sat down on the step in the genkan to take his shoes off, and it was a mistake. His thighs had decided that sitting was where they were staying. He got the 1st shoe off. He sat there holding it.

His mother came out of the kitchen, drying her hands, and looked at him sitting on the step with 1 shoe in his hand.

"You won?" she said. "Your 1st match?"

"Practice match."

"We'll have sekihan tomorrow." She looked at his legs, and at the shoe, and held out her hand. He took it, and she pulled, and he came up off the step with a noise he hadn't meant to make. "Dinner 1st. Then the bath. A long 1."

She went back to the kitchen.

---

Dinner was hamburg steak. Sho ate 2 and a half and could have eaten the plate.

His father ate. Halfway through, he put down his chopsticks and said, carefully, "Is it true the libero can't serve?"

Everybody looked at him.

"I read it somewhere," his father said, and picked his chopsticks back up.

His phone was face down beside his plate. Sho knew, without having to check, what was in its search history, because on Friday night it had been read aloud to all of them until his mother said *eat*.

"It depends on the rules," Sho said. "In the official ones, yes. He can't serve."

"Ah," said his father, and went back to eating.

Mio had been quiet all through dinner. When Sho stood up to take his plate to the sink, she said it into her rice.

"Fine. New bet. 2 puddings says you quit by summer."

"The old 1 was Golden Week."

"I'm extending it."

Sho looked at her. She was very interested in her rice.

"Deal," he said.

---

Upstairs, after the bath, he sat on the floor with his back against the bed, because the chair was too far away, and opened the notebook on his knees.

> **Oikawa.** Setter, Aoba Johsai. Captain. Jump spin serve. 1-hand toss, right hand, about 2 m, in front of the hitting shoulder. 4-step approach, starts 4 big steps back. Lands about 1 m inside. 3 bounces before every serve.
>
> Watched our receive all through set 3 from behind the bench. Saw me shank 1 into the table. Picked Tsukishima anyway. **Picks the 1 who'll mind most.**
>
> Smiled at the quick. Shut down for half a beat when I asked to learn his serve. **He hates being asked.** Kageyama went stiff when I asked. Number 4 looked at Kageyama 1st.
>
> Daichi saw the formation fix 2 seconds before I could say it. No tape. Remember that.
>
> I start my approach with my left foot turned out when I'm nervous (Ennoshita). Fix.
>
> Legs: about 1 and a half sets, then they're gone. Last jump of a long rally a hand lower than the 1st. Can't hit over a tall block when they're gone. Tooled 3. Fix. Ask somebody who knows legs.

He looked at the last line. *Ask somebody.* There wasn't anybody to ask yet. The club had a teacher who'd just learned the word *block-out*, and a captain, and no coach.

He turned to a new page.

> **Jump serve.** Order: float 1st, then jump float, then spin. 15 min a day of toss-and-catch, no hitting, until the toss lands in the same spot 20 times in a row. Then 1 step. Shoulder warm-up 1st, every time. Stop if it hurts.

He looked at *Stop if it hurts* with his thumb on the page. Then he went on.

> Guardian Deity. Coming back. Who?

He turned off the light and lay in the dark with his legs aching under the quilt, thinking about a toss that went to the same place in the air every time, as if it were hanging there on a thread, waiting for somebody to come and get it.

Wider. I'll take more court."); 25-23.

## Chapter 10: Guardian deity

At 4:15 his legs were 2 planks of wood.

He found that out at the top of the stairs. He found it out again at the bottom, and again when he crouched in the genkan to tie his shoes and had to put a hand on the wall to stand back up. The fronts of his thighs were the worst. Every step down sent a dull complaint all the way up them, the kind a body makes the morning after it's done something it wasn't ready for.

The porch light was on.

He hadn't turned it on. He stood on the step in the cold, under the yellow light, with the yard lit up in front of him as far as the hedge, and then he went down into it.

Toss and catch. 15 minutes, no hitting. Right hand, ball out in front of the right shoulder, about 2 meters up, and catch it where it comes down without moving his feet. That was the whole exercise. It was the 1st line of the plan, and it was harder than it sounded.

The 1st 1 went up behind his head. The 2nd went up straight and came down a meter to his left, and he had to step for it. The 3rd went where he'd meant it to, and the 4th didn't. Oikawa's toss had gone up the same way every time, as if it were on a thread. Sho's went up like a ball thrown by a 15-year-old in his yard at 4:20 in the morning.

He counted. Best run, 4 in a row in the same patch of grass. Then 6. Then he dropped 1 on his own foot.

By 4:30 the best run was still 6.

He wrote it on the back of his hand with the pen from his pocket, *6*, and went round to the side of the house to do wall passes, very slowly, with his thighs shaking every time he bent his knees.

---

At breakfast, Mio put down her chopsticks and announced, to the whole table, "Something beeps through my wall at 4 in the morning."

"4:15," said Sho.

"Every day."

"Most days. Sunday's my rest day."

"What do you even DO at 4:15?"

"I throw a ball in the air and catch it," said Sho. "In the yard. For 15 minutes."

Mio stared at him.

"That's it?"

"Then wall passes. Then I come in and eat." He picked up his rice bowl. His arms felt fine. It was only his legs. "You can join me."

"I would rather die."

His mother put the plate of tamagoyaki down in front of him. There were 2 more pieces on it than there used to be. Nobody mentioned the porch light, and Sho didn't ask.

Mio was looking at the *6* on the back of his hand.

"What's that?"

"My best."

"Out of what?"

"20."

She thought about it. "That's bad."

"It's very bad," Sho agreed, and ate the tamagoyaki.

---

The stranger showed up in the afternoon.

Kageyama was practicing his aim. He'd put an empty water bottle on the floor on the far side of the net and was trying to hit it with his serve. Hinata kept wandering into the line of fire to watch, and Kageyama kept yelling at him to move.

At the other end of the gym, Tanaka was hitting cross. Ennoshita stood at the net post with his arms folded and counted under his breath, and every time Tanaka's wrist turned over late and the ball went into the far corner, Ennoshita said a number, and Tanaka said "AGAIN," and Narita set him another.

"14," said Ennoshita.

"AGAIN."

On the next serve, Hinata finally got out of the way, and Kageyama's serve went screaming toward the bottle.

Somebody stepped in front of it.

There was a soft sound. *Thup.* The ball came off the stranger's forearms and went up in a high, lazy arc and dropped into the setter's spot, a meter off the net, right where Kageyama would have wanted it.

Sho, stretching against the wall, stopped stretching.

He'd heard that sound before, on tape, from the best liberos in the V.League, and not very often. The sound of a pass with nothing wasted in it, all the pace soaked out of the ball by arms that knew how much to give.

The stranger was short. Shorter than Hinata, with spiky dark hair that had a streak of blond at the front and stood straight up, and a grin that was much too big for the rest of him.

Tanaka saw him and dropped the ball he was holding.

"NOYA!"

"RYU!"

They slammed into each other in the middle of the gym and started yelling, both at once, about nothing anybody else could follow. Ennoshita, at the net post, said "15," to nobody.

Sugawara and Daichi had come in behind the stranger. Daichi looked at him, and smiled, and tossed him a ball, and the stranger caught it and held it against his chest before he tossed it back.

"Nishinoya Yu," Sugawara said, to the 1st years. "2nd year. Libero." He smiled. "Our Guardian Deity."

Hinata had been staring at Nishinoya since the pass. Now he walked right up to him and stopped, and looked down at him, a little.

"You're shorter than me," Hinata said.

There was a terrible silence.

"And you received that serve," Hinata went on, faster. "Kageyama's serve. It went *thup* and it went right there and you didn't even move. That was AMAZING. You're amazing. Nishinoya-senpai!"

Nishinoya's whole face changed. It went still, and then it went red, and he put a hand over his heart and turned away and looked up at the gym ceiling.

"...Say that again."

"Nishinoya-SENPAI!"

"Ryu," Nishinoya whispered. "He called me senpai."

"I heard," said Tanaka, wiping his eyes.

Nishinoya spun round and put both hands on Hinata's shoulders. "I'll buy you a popsicle. 2 popsicles."

---

Tanaka told the 1st years about the suspension in a stage whisper while Nishinoya was in the club room changing.

"A week off school," he said. "And a month off the club. There was a fight. Sort of. With the ace. He's a 3rd year. Noya was trying to stop him leaving, and the vice principal walked into the middle of it, and there was a vase."

"The vice principal," said Hinata. "The..." He touched his own hair.

"Same 1."

Hinata looked worried. "So is he a delinquent? Nishinoya-senpai?"

"No!" Tanaka made a shape with both hands, an explosion going off. "He's just. Very. You know?"

When Nishinoya came back out, he looked round the gym at each of them. Then he looked at Sugawara.

"Where's Asahi-san?"

Sugawara's smile stayed where it was a beat too long.

"He hasn't come back," he said.

Nishinoya's face shut.

"That idiot," he said. It came out short and rough. "That big idiot."

"Noya," said Tanaka.

"He's an idiot, Ryu. You know he is." Nishinoya dropped his bag by the stage, hard. "I'll practice. I'll come in every day. But I'm not playing in matches. Not till he's back."

"Noya," said Daichi.

"Not without him, Daichi-san."

Daichi looked at him. Then he nodded once, and picked up the handle of the ball cart.

Sho's hand was half up before he'd decided to put it there.

"Sorry," he said. "Who's Asahi?"

They all looked at him. Sugawara, for once, looked as if he didn't know what to do with his face.

"3rd year," Daichi said. "Wing spiker." He pushed the cart toward the middle of the court. "Receiving drill. Everybody."

Satoshi had walked into old team wounds before, as the new man with the clipboard. Sho put his hand down and got in line.

---

Daichi got up on a chair on the other side of the net to hit at the receivers, and Nishinoya went to the back court to show them how. Before he went to the line, Sho asked Sugawara for 1 set on the left. He wanted to see something 1st.

Sugawara set him high. Sho's legs were still wood from yesterday, and the tape came up level with his eyes again, so he hit the 1 thing he could see, a hard cross, flat and heavy, with the whole hand on it, the best ball he'd hit since Seijoh's libero had taken 1 off him with a single step.

Nishinoya was already there.

*Thup.*

The ball went up soft and floated down into the setter's spot.

Sho landed on both feet and stood still. That ball should have died. He'd felt it leave his hand. It should have been in the floor.

"Whoa!" Nishinoya shook out his arms, grinning. "That was heavy! My arms are buzzing! Do it again!"

Sho ducked under the net and walked over and stopped in front of him, and bowed, all the way, 90 degrees.

"Nishinoya-senpai," he said, to the floor. "Please teach me to receive."

There was a pause.

"...Is a guy that tall making fun of me?"

Sho straightened up and looked down at him, a long way down, and kept his face perfectly serious.

"No, senpai. I'm 182 centimeters and you just ate my best spike like a snack. I'm begging."

Nishinoya looked at him with his mouth a little open. Then his ears went red.

"Ryu," he said, without taking his eyes off Sho. "He called me senpai too."

Then he pointed up at Sho with his whole arm. "Fine! I'll teach you! Both of you! Line up!"

---

It was a disaster, and Sho would have stayed all night.

"Okay," said Sho, with the notebook open on the bench. "When the ball comes. Do you read the hitter's shoulder 1st, or his approach?"

Nishinoya thought about it very hard. You could watch him doing it.

"You go *shu*," he said, and dropped into a little crouch. "And then *don*." He made his arms into a platform and popped them forward, a short sharp shove.

Sho looked at him.

Then he wrote, in the notebook, neatly: *shu → don.*

"I get it!" Hinata yelled.

"You do not get it," said Sho.

"I totally get it!"

Tanaka leaned over the notebook. "*Shu* means you get down low," he said. "Fast. Like that. And *don* means *don*."

Sho wrote that down too.

They started. Daichi stood on his chair and hit, and Hinata and Sho and Tsukishima took turns. Tsukishima had come to the line without being told, which Sho hadn't expected, the day after the Great King had pointed at him in front of a balcony full of girls. He received with his face perfectly blank, 1 ball in 3 going up, and kept his mouth shut about all of it, and when Nishinoya shouted "GOOD!" at 1 of them, his ears went pink.

Hinata wanted every ball. "Again! Daichi-san, again!" After the 6th shank in a row he stood in the middle of the court with his fists clenched.

"He said my receive was terrible," he said. "The Great King. Yesterday."

"It is terrible," said Tsukishima.

"So's yours! He said so!"

"I know what he said."

"So I'm FIXING it," Hinata said, and got back in line.

For the 1st 20 balls Hinata was a disaster in the ordinary way and Sho was a disaster in a well-informed way.

He knew what he was doing wrong. He knew every time. The ball came off Daichi's hand, and he watched it, and his head did the work, line, speed, spin, where it would land, and by the time the answer arrived the ball was already past the moment, and his feet were late, and the platform came up a beat behind, and the ball came off it sideways.

That was the 1st thing.

The 2nd thing was *shu*. Nishinoya got low and stayed low, knees bent, hips down, for the whole of every rally. Sho got low too. For the 1st 3 balls he could hold it. By the 8th, his thighs were shaking, the fronts of them, the same muscles that had carried him through 3 sets yesterday, and every time he waited for the next ball he came up a little higher, until he was receiving standing almost straight, like a man at a bus stop.

"You're standing UP!" Nishinoya shouted at him. "Get down! *Shu!*"

"I'm trying, senpai."

"Try lower!"

He tried lower. His legs held it for 2 more balls and then let him know what they thought of that.

On the 21st shank, Nishinoya walked over and slapped him on the back hard enough to make him stagger.

"And you're thinking with your eyes!"

Sho looked down at him. "I'm what?"

"Your eyes go like this." Nishinoya made a frantic tracking motion with 2 fingers. "You're watching it, and watching it, and watching it, and THEN your feet go. Your feet are late. You're waiting to be sure!"

"I need to be sure where it's going."

"No, you don't!" Nishinoya looked honestly baffled. "Then you're too late! You just go! If you're wrong, you fix it on the way!"

Sho opened his mouth to explain why that was wrong, and closed it again.

He'd just watched Nishinoya take 30 balls in a row off Daichi's hand. Nishinoya's feet moved before Daichi's hand touched the ball, every time. Before the hit. He was right almost every time, and when he was wrong he changed it in the middle of a step, a little shuffle, and was right by the time the ball got there.

He was reading. He was reading faster than words.

And Sho thought about the 3v3. About the eye. It had never once come while he stood there waiting for it. It had come in exactly the place Nishinoya lived all the time.

*Okay,* he thought. *That's data. Write it down later.*

He went back to the line.

"*Shu,*" he said under his breath. "*Don.*"

Daichi hit the ball, and Sho's feet went before his head had finished. They went a little wrong, and on the way he felt them change, a small step left, and he got as low as his legs would let him and put his arms out and gave the ball a short shove forward.

*Thup.*

It went up, and came down a meter from the setter's spot.

Nishinoya grabbed the next ball out of the cart and slapped it into Sho's chest. "AGAIN!"

He did it again. It went into the net. He did it again, and it went up. Out of the last 10, 4 went up, and by the end his legs were shaking so hard that when Daichi got down off the chair, Sho sat down on the floor where he was.

His left hand went to his left knee. He felt round it, the way you'd check a pocket for keys. It was fine. It was only the thighs. He took the hand away.

Nishinoya looked at him sitting there and laughed and sat down next to him.

"Tomorrow your legs'll hurt," Nishinoya said.

"They hurt now, senpai."

"Tomorrow they'll hurt more. That's how you know it's working." He bounced up off the floor as if he'd never been on it. "*Shu!*"

---

Kageyama came over a little after 6.

He'd been watching from the other end while he worked on his serve. Now he stood at the end line with a ball in both hands and his eyes on Nishinoya's arms.

He bowed, stiffly. "Nishinoya-san. Onegaishimasu."

Nishinoya grinned and got into position. Kageyama served. *Thup.* Kageyama served again, harder. *Thup.* Kageyama served a 3rd time, harder still, with his mouth set, and Nishinoya threw himself sideways and got it and rolled over his shoulder and came up on his feet shouting, and the ball came down where Kageyama would have wanted it.

When Nishinoya went to drink, Sho got up off the floor and stepped into his spot.

"Serve at me," he said to Kageyama.

Kageyama looked at him. "You'll shank it."

"Yes," said Sho. "Serve anyway."

Kageyama looked at him a beat longer. Then he nodded, once, and served.

Sho shanked it. It went off his left forearm and sailed toward the stage.

Kageyama walked to the stage, picked up the ball, walked back, and served again. Sho shanked that 1 too, and Kageyama fetched it.

Around the 20th, the ball came in fast at his chest and he reached for it late, with his hands instead of his arms, and it caught his right ring finger at a bad angle and bent it back. He shook the hand. The finger had already gone fat and hot.

"Again," he said.

Kageyama looked at the hand, and at him, and served again.

They did 25. Sho got 6 of them up. Kageyama fetched every 1 he didn't. On the last, Sho's pass went up and floated down near the net, and Kageyama ducked under the tape, picked it up where it had come down, set it once to himself, caught it, and looked at Sho.

"Better," he said.

Sho went to the bench to shake his finger out, and Kiyoko was there with a roll of white tape.

She took his hand without asking, looked at the finger, and taped it to the 1 next to it, 3 quick neat turns, and tore the tape off clean.

"Thank you, Shimizu-senpai."

Her eyes went to the notebook lying open on the bench, to *shu → don* and *Tanaka: shu = get down low, fast*, and to the *6* on the back of his hand, which hadn't quite washed off.

"You write a lot," she said, and went back to the water bottles.

---

At the very end, before they took the net down, Sho asked Sugawara for a few more sets.

Sugawara looked at his legs. "3," he said. "Your knees are shaking from here."

"5, please."

"3."

He set 3 to the left side, high and outside, the kind of set you give the ace when everything else has gone wrong.

On the 3rd 1, his hands held the ball.

It was tiny. A fraction of a beat at the top, the ball staying in his fingers a hair too long before it left them. The set still came out fine. Sho hit it, a short tired swing off a short tired jump, into the floor, and landed on both feet.

Satoshi had watched tape of setters for 9 years. He'd have stopped the tape there and rewound it 3 times.

"Thank you, Sugawara-san," said Sho, and went to help with the net.

While they were rolling it up, Tanaka and Nishinoya came and stood on either side of him.

"Kiyose," said Tanaka. "A question. For the record."

"Yes, senpai."

"What," said Nishinoya, "do you think of Kiyoko-san?"

On Saturday, when Sho had asked who she was, Tanaka had leaned down and breathed it at him like a warning.

"Like the sun," said Sho. "You don't look directly at it."

Tanaka grabbed his shoulder. "Noya. He LISTENED to me."

Nishinoya squinted up at Sho.

"...He's really tall, though," he said.

"What's that got to do with it?"

"I don't know! It's suspicious!"

They went off toward the stage, arguing about it, and Sho rolled up the rest of the net.

---

Nishinoya was the last to go, except for Sho and Daichi, who was waiting by the door to lock up.

He'd said 3 times that afternoon that he wasn't playing in matches, and he'd stayed until 7:30. Now he was sitting on the step by the door pulling his shoes on, and Sho, coiling the net cable beside him, said, "Nishinoya-senpai."

"Yeah?"

"Asahi-san. What's he like?"

Nishinoya stopped with 1 shoe half on.

When he answered, his voice had gone quiet. Sho stopped coiling.

"He's big," Nishinoya said. "Really big. His spikes are so heavy your arms go numb." He pulled the shoe on. "And he got blocked. A lot. In 1 match. Every ball. And after that..." He stood up. "After that he stopped asking for the ball."

He opened the door. The air that came in was cold and smelled of the wet field.

"You'll see," he said, with his back to the gym. "If he comes back."

The door shut behind him.

Sho stood by the door with the net cable in his hands.

There had been a match at 20, in Osaka, on a Saturday in a gym that smelled of floor wax, 2 months before the knee went. At 22-22, before the serve, the setter had looked across at him, the way he did when he was deciding who got the high ball, the way he'd shouted *Matsumoto!* 100 times that season. And Satoshi had been looking at the floor, with his hand down at his side.

The setter had given it to somebody else. After that, the setter had stopped looking his way.

Daichi turned off the lights.

---

Tanaka, Hinata and Kageyama were waiting for him at the bottom of the hill, outside Sakanoshita. Nishinoya had promised Hinata 2 popsicles and then gone home without buying them, and Hinata wanted it on record.

The blond man at the register came out from behind the counter when he saw the 4 of them through the glass. He took 4 popsicles out of the chest freezer by the door, said "Catch," and threw them 1 after another. Tanaka caught his. Kageyama caught his. Hinata juggled his twice and caught it against his chest. Sho caught his and looked at the price sticker.

"These are 60 yen."

"They're free."

"Why?"

"Because if I sell you 1, you'll spend 10 minutes trying to get it for 50," said the man, "and I want to close the register." He was already going back inside. "You won against Seijoh, right? Shut up and eat."

The door swung shut behind him. Sho bowed to it anyway.

They walked toward the main road, eating. Hinata had finished his in about 9 bites and was still talking about the *thup*.

Tanaka bumped Kageyama with his shoulder. "So that Oikawa guy was your senpai, huh? Kitagawa Daiichi. That's a big school. Didn't Shiratorizawa want you?"

Kageyama walked a few steps.

"I applied," he said. "I didn't get in."

Hinata stopped chewing his stick. "You didn't?"

"The entrance exam was hard." Kageyama looked straight ahead. "I didn't understand it."

"...Any of it?" said Tanaka.

"Most of it."

Nobody laughed. Tanaka opened his mouth, looked at Kageyama's face, and shut it again.

"So I came here," Kageyama went on, stiffly. "Because I heard Coach Ukai was coming back. The old 1. He was famous. He took Karasuno to Nationals." He looked at the stick in his hand. "And then he collapsed. Before I got here. So."

The road was quiet. A car went past with its headlights on, and their 4 shadows swung round on the pavement and swung back.

"...I'm glad you didn't get in," Hinata said.

Kageyama's head turned. "Ha?"

"Then you'd be over there." Hinata pointed with his popsicle stick, roughly toward the hills. "And I'd have to beat you. And I'd have nobody to set me."

Kageyama looked at him for 3 steps. Then he looked at the road.

"Boke," he said, with not much in it.

Sho walked on Kageyama's other side, with his legs aching all the way up the backs now as well as the fronts. Kageyama had come all this way for a man who wasn't there when he arrived. Nishinoya was coming in every day for a man who wasn't there either. Sho's ring finger throbbed inside its tape where his hand was on the strap of his bag.

"Kageyama-kun," he said. "Before the exams. If you want somebody to go through the questions with you. I'm all right at exams."

Kageyama looked at him with his eyebrows drawn down.

"...Maybe," he said.

At the corner the others went their way, Tanaka still bumping Kageyama with his shoulder and Kageyama letting him, and Sho went his.

Under the light of the vending machine by the shrine, he stopped and opened the notebook against the side of it, and wrote 2 lines.

> **Asahi.** Ace. Got blocked until he stopped asking for the ball.
>
> **Receiving.** Move 1st. Correct after.

The vending machine hummed. Somewhere behind the shrine a dog barked twice and stopped.

He looked at the 2 lines together. Then he underlined both, and walked home on his wooden legs, toward the porch light.

**Continuity (ch 10)**

- Revised Nov 22 (revision pass of ch 1 to 34). Old ch 9. One day: the Wednesday after the Seijoh match (ch 11 opens at the end of Thursday practice). No status card (cards retired Nov 22).
- Canon: Nishinoya returns from his suspension and receives Kageyama's serve, aimed at a water bottle, into the setter's spot; "Ryu!" "Noya!"; Hinata: "You're shorter than me" and "Nishinoya-senpai", Noya melts and promises popsicles; the suspension (1 week from school, 1 month from the club, a fight about the ace leaving, a vase broken in front of the vice principal); "Where's Asahi-san?" / Suga: "He hasn't come back."; Noya won't play matches without Asahi but practices and teaches receiving; he can't explain receiving (his sound words are the fic's); Kageyama serves at him; the Tanaka and Noya Kiyoko guard (flavor); the Sakanoshita clerk (Ukai, unnamed) throws free popsicles; on the walk Kageyama says he failed Shiratorizawa's entrance exam and came to Karasuno because old Coach Ukai was coming back, and Ukai collapsed before he got there. Canon order bent: Noya stays for the drill instead of storming out [memory, unverified]. "Rolling Thunder" is cut (it may be later in canon).
- 4:15 a.m.: legs "2 planks of wood" (fronts of the thighs worst), a hand on the wall to stand; **the porch light already on (Mom), nobody mentions it** (arc 2 echoes it). Toss-and-catch day 1 (ch 9's plan): best run **6 in a row** out of a goal of 20 by 4:30; *6* written on the back of his hand; wall passes with shaking thighs.
- Breakfast: Mio: "Something beeps through my wall at 4 in the morning." "4:15." "Every day." "Most days. Sunday's my rest day." "What do you even DO at 4:15?" ... "You can join me." "I would rather die." Mom's tamagoyaki on his plate, 2 more pieces. Mio: "What's that?" "My best." "Out of what?" "20." "That's bad." "It's very bad."
- Afternoon: Tanaka hits cross after cross while Ennoshita counts at the net post ("14." "AGAIN." ... "15," to nobody during the reunion; ch 9's bus tip). Noya's entrance, the reunion; Suga: "Nishinoya Yu. 2nd year. Libero. Our Guardian Deity." Tanaka on the suspension ("He's just. Very. You know?"). Noya: "That idiot. That big idiot." / drops his bag by the stage: "I'll practice. I'll come in every day. But I'm not playing in matches. Not till he's back." / "Not without him, Daichi-san." (ch 12: every day he says he won't play matches and stays till the lights go off). Sho: "Sorry. Who's Asahi?" Daichi: "3rd year. Wing spiker... Receiving drill."
- **Noya digs Sho's best cross** (1 set from Suga before the drill; the tape level with his eyes again) ("That ball should have died."). The 90-degree bow: "Nishinoya-senpai. Please teach me to receive." "...Is a guy that tall making fun of me?" **"No, senpai. I'm 182 centimeters and you just ate my best spike like a snack. I'm begging."** "Say it again." Noya, red-eared: "Ryu. He called me senpai too." (Iwaizumi keeps "There's 2 of them now", ch 9.) "Fine! I'll teach you! Both of you! Line up!"
- The lesson (Sho, Hinata, Tsukishima take turns; Tsukishima came without being told, 1 in 3 up, ears pink at Noya's "GOOD!"; Hinata: "He said my receive was terrible. The Great King. Yesterday." Tsukishima: "It is terrible." "So's yours!" "I know what he said." "So I'm FIXING it."): "You go *shu*. And then *don*." *shu → don*; Hinata "I get it!" "You do not get it."; Tanaka: "*Shu* means you get down low. Fast... And *don* means *don*." **Body (Nov 22): Sho can hold *shu* for 3 balls; by the 8th his thighs shake and he's receiving "standing almost straight"; "You're standing UP!"** On the 21st shank: **"You're thinking with your eyes!... You're waiting to be sure!" "I need to be sure where it's going." "Then you're too late! You just go! If you're wrong, you fix it on the way!"** Sho links it to the eye (it came when a play outran his head and he stopped talking to himself). The 1st *shu, don* pass lands a meter from the setter's spot; 4 of the last 10 up; he sits on the floor; **his left hand checks his left knee, "the way you'd check a pocket for keys"; it's fine; only the thighs**; Noya: "Tomorrow your legs'll hurt... That's how you know it's working."
- Kageyama after 6: "Nishinoya-san. Onegaishimasu."; 3 serves dug. **"Serve at me." "You'll shank it." "Yes. Serve anyway."** 25 serves, **6 up**, Kageyama fetches every shank; **around the 20th the right ring finger jams (hands instead of arms) and Sho says "Again."**; on the last, Kageyama ducks under the tape, picks it up, sets it once to himself: **"Better."**
- **Kiyoko tapes the finger to the next 1, 3 turns, tears it off clean: "You write a lot."** (her eyes on *shu → don* and the *6*).
- **Suga: "3. Your knees are shaking from here." "5, please." "3."; his hands hold the 3rd a fraction too long** (ch 11, 12 pay it off). Sho notices and says only "Thank you, Sugawara-san."
- The guard, while the net comes down: "What do you think of Kiyoko-san?" **"Like the sun. You don't look directly at it."** (Tanaka on Saturday, when Sho asked who she was, ch 4) Tanaka: "Noya. He LISTENED to me." Noya: "...He's really tall, though." "What's that got to do with it?" "I don't know! It's suspicious!"
- **Noya on the step by the door, 7:30 (Daichi waiting to lock up): "He's big. Really big. His spikes are so heavy your arms go numb. And he got blocked. A lot. In 1 match. Every ball. And after that... After that he stopped asking for the ball." "You'll see. If he comes back."**
- **Satoshi, 2 months before the knee:** a Saturday match in Osaka, 22-22, before the serve the setter who shouted *Matsumoto!* looked across at him to decide who got the high ball; Satoshi was looking at the floor with his hand down at his side; the setter gave it to somebody else and after that stopped looking his way (ch 14: "A setter calling his name and him not raising his hand.") (bible Soul sheet: Satoshi stopped asking for the ball once too; never told Asahi).
- Sakanoshita (Tanaka, Hinata, Kageyama waiting; Noya forgot the popsicles): "Catch." "These are 60 yen." "They're free." "Why?" "Because if I sell you 1, you'll spend 10 minutes trying to get it for 50... You won against Seijoh, right? Shut up and eat."
- The walk: **Kageyama: "I applied. I didn't get in." "The entrance exam was hard. I didn't understand it." "...Any of it?" "Most of it." ... "And then he collapsed. Before I got here. So." Hinata: "...I'm glad you didn't get in." "Ha?" "Then you'd be over there. And I'd have to beat you. And I'd have nobody to set me." "Boke."** Sho: Kageyama came for a man who wasn't there; Noya comes in every day for a man who isn't there either. Sho: "Before the exams. If you want somebody to go through the questions with you." "...Maybe." (arc 3 exams).
- Notebook, under the vending machine by the shrine: ***Asahi.* Ace. Got blocked until he stopped asking for the ball. / *Receiving.* Move 1st. Correct after.** Both underlined. (Toss-and-catch best run on day 1: 6 of 20; the count must climb slowly.)
- No eye events.
