- Review fixes (Nov 22): Kageyama can't catch a pass from the end line; Noya never "wasn't rejoining" (he practices, refuses matches); Kiyoko's 1st sight is Tuesday (ch 2), so the sun line points to Saturday's question; the guard bit made messy and moved off Kiyoko's moment; the doubled senpai bit trimmed; the Satoshi memory reworded (before the serve; "the setter stopped looking his way"); the jump stays at the tape; negatives and explanations cut; Tsukishima, Hinata's reason, Tanaka's cross, the knee check and Suga's "3" added. Note for ch 13: "Two weeks ago" is now 5 days; vary its shu-don pass wording.
- Next: Ch 11 "The ace who left": Thursday, Takeda's Nekoma news; Asahi; Suga's story.

## Chapter 11: The ace who left

Nishinoya had been right. On Thursday his legs hurt more.

Last night the ache had reached the backs of his legs. This morning it had reached his calves, and the place where his hips met his legs, and it woke up with him at 4:15 and came down the stairs with him 1 step at a time, with a hand on the banister.

The porch light was on again.

Toss and catch. The ball went up, came down, went up. On the 3rd go round he got 7 in a row in the same patch of grass, and then 9, and then the 10th came down on the edge of the patch and he had to lean for it, and that was the end of that. 9. He wrote it on the back of his hand under the ghost of yesterday's 6.

---

At practice, Nishinoya took him again for receive.

Daichi climbed up on his chair on the other side of the net with the cart beside him. Sho got down into *shu* and stayed there, and counted. On the 3rd ball his thighs started to shake, the same as yesterday, and it hurt more getting there.

"You're sitting on your knees!" Nishinoya came over and walked round him, looking at him from the side like somebody looking at a badly parked bicycle. "Your weight's all in the front. Here." He slapped Sho on the back of the hips, hard. "Back! Put it back here! Like there's a chair!"

"There isn't a chair, senpai."

"There's an INVISIBLE chair! Sit on it!"

Sho pushed his hips back, the way he'd have told any 15-year-old on any tape to do it, and his weight came off the fronts of his thighs and went into the backs of his legs and his hips, where it was supposed to go. The shaking eased.

"Like that?"

"Like THAT!" Nishinoya looked delighted with himself. "See? I'm good at teaching!"

Daichi hit. Sho stayed on the invisible chair. He held it for 5 balls before the backs of his legs started complaining too, and then he came up, and Nishinoya shouted "CHAIR!" at him from the side, and he went back down.

Out of 30 balls off Daichi's hand, 11 went up. Nishinoya shouted "GOOD!" at 2 of them and "THINKING!" at most of the rest.

"Kiyose," said Daichi, from the chair, between balls. "Better than yesterday."

"Yes, Captain."

"Still bad."

"Yes, Captain."

Daichi hit the next 1.

---

Takeda-sensei told them at the end of Thursday practice.

He came in while they were putting the balls away and stood in the middle of the floor holding a sheet of paper in both hands, and waited until they'd all come over. The sheet had a table drawn on it in ruler lines, days across the top.

"Golden Week," he said. "We'll have a training camp. And on the last day, a practice match." He took a breath. "Against Nekoma."

The 2nd years went silent. Then Tanaka shot up off the floor.

"NEKOMA? The Tokyo Nekoma?"

"Who's Nekoma?" said Hinata.

Tanaka turned round to the 1st years and lowered his voice. A long time ago, he said, Karasuno's old coach and Nekoma's old coach had been rivals. The 2 schools played each other all the time, practice matches every year, crows against cats. The whole town used to come and watch. People had a name for it.

"The Battle at the Garbage Dump," said Tanaka. "Because crows and cats both go through the garbage."

"That's a terrible name," said Tsukishima.

"It's a LEGENDARY name."

Sugawara was frowning a little. "We haven't played them in years, sensei. Not since the old coach left. Why now?"

Takeda smiled and pushed his glasses up his nose.

"Let's just say," he said, "I'm hoping it gets someone's attention."

Sho, with a ball in each hand, looked at Takeda's face. It was the face of a man who'd set a trap and was very proud of it, and couldn't hide it.

Daichi had been looking at the sheet in Takeda's hands. Then he looked across the gym at the stage steps, where Nishinoya was sitting with his elbows on his knees.

"If we're playing Nekoma," Daichi said, "we'll want a libero who plays matches."

Nishinoya looked at the far wall.

On the other side of the cart, Hinata had turned to Kageyama and was bouncing on his toes. "Kageyama! Nekoma! Cats! We'll beat them, right? And the ace'll be back by then, right?" He turned round to the whole gym. "Asahi-san?"

Nobody answered him.

Daichi looked at the cart. Sugawara looked at the ball in his hands. Nishinoya kept looking at the wall with his jaw set. Tanaka opened his mouth and shut it.

Hinata looked round at all their faces, and stopped bouncing.

"Okay," he said, quieter. "Then I'll go ask him."

---

Sakanoshita was on the way home for 4 of them. Hinata bought a meat bun and ate it in the doorway. Kageyama bought milk. Tanaka bought 2 buns and a drink and argued with Hinata about Nekoma with his mouth full.

Sho asked for a meat bun and, because he'd just watched Kageyama buy 1 and suddenly wanted 1 too, put a carton of milk on the counter beside his coins.

200 yen. The bun was 120 and the milk was 120.

"Sorry," he said. "I'll put the milk back."

The blond man at the register looked as if he'd had a long day of people. He looked at the coins, and at the milk, and at Sho.

He swept the coins off the counter into his hand, took a bun out of the steamer with the tongs, and pushed it and the milk across.

"Sir, that's 40..."

"Next," said the man, and looked past him at Tanaka.

Sho stood there with the milk in his hand. Then he bowed to the counter and went out.

"I'll bring it tomorrow," he said, from the door.

The man was reading his magazine.

---

They walked down toward the main road in a row, Tanaka in the middle, with the last of the light going purple over the rice fields.

"So who's Asahi-san?" said Hinata. "Really. What's he like?"

Tanaka chewed and swallowed.

"Big," he said. "You'll see. People are scared of him. 1st years see him in the corridor and go the other way. There was a rumor last year he was 20 and he'd been held back twice." He snorted. "He looks like he's done time. And if you yell at him, he cries."

"He cries?" said Hinata.

"He's soft. He's the softest guy in the club." Tanaka looked at the bun in his hand. "And when he hit, the whole gym shook. He's my senpai, you know. When I was a 1st year and I couldn't do anything, he used to stay late and hit at me till I could."

Kageyama was walking a little behind, drinking his milk. "Why did he stop?"

"Ask Suga-san," said Tanaka, and bit his bun so hard that half of it went in at once.

Sho walked on Tanaka's other side, holding the milk he hadn't paid for all of.

He had 1 question. He'd had it since Wednesday night, since Nishinoya at the door. It was the question Satoshi would have asked about any hitter on any tape, before anything else.

"Tanaka-senpai," he said. "What's the set he likes?"

Tanaka looked at him sideways. "Huh?"

"Asahi-san. Which ball."

Tanaka walked a few more steps. The bun was gone, and he folded the paper round his fingers.

"The bad 1," he said. "When everything's gone wrong. Nobody's passed it right and Suga-san's running backward and he just throws it up as high as he can, out to the left, and everybody knows it's going to Asahi-san because there isn't anybody else." He looked at the road ahead. "He used to shout for those. Suga-san's name. From the back of the court. You could hear him over everything."

Nobody spoke after that until the next corner. A truck went past with its lights on.

"Was he cool?" said Hinata. "When he hit those?"

Tanaka laughed, a short real 1, and put him in a headlock. "He was SO cool," he said, into Hinata's hair, and Hinata yelled, and Kageyama stepped round them both and kept walking, and Sho followed with the milk.

*The bad 1. When everything's gone wrong.* Satoshi had hit that ball for years, the 1 you couldn't hide from, because there was nobody else to throw it to. A team had 1 of those. 1 person.

---

At home, Mio was on the sofa with her homework spread over the low table and her feet on Dad's newspaper. She looked up when Sho came in, at his face, then down at his knees, then at his hands.

"What's that?"

He looked at the back of his hand. *9*, in blue, over the gray smudge of yesterday's *6*.

"Toss and catch."

"It went up."

"By 3."

She thought about it with her pencil in her mouth. "Still bad."

"Yes."

"To 20?"

"To 20."

"That's 11 more." She did it on her fingers anyway, to check. "That's ages."

"Yes."

Mio looked at his knees again. Then she went back to her homework.

"Your milk's dripping," she said, writing, and it was, a little, out of the corner of the carton he'd forgotten he was holding.

He took the stairs 1 at a time, with a hand on the wall, and on his bed he opened the notebook and wrote under Wednesday's lines:

> Nekoma. Golden Week, last day.
>
> Asahi-san. Best ball: the bad 1. Used to shout for it.

He looked at the 2nd line, and at the 1st, and turned off the light.

---

Hinata went on Friday, at lunch.

Kageyama was waiting at the top of the 1st-year stairs when Hinata came out of 1-1. Nobody had asked him to. He fell in beside Hinata with his hands in his pockets, and Hinata looked round at him and let him.

Sho met them on the way and went with them as far as the stairs to the 3rd-year floor. Then he stopped on the landing and leaned on the railing.

"You're not coming?" said Hinata.

"I'll wait here."

"Why?"

"It's his floor," said Sho. "3 of us is a lot of 1st years."

Hinata thought about that, and seemed to decide it was a reason, and went up. Kageyama went up after him.

Sho stayed on the landing and ate his meat bun, the 1st of the 2 he'd bought at the 7-Eleven by the station on his way to school, and listened.

The 3rd-year floor was louder than the 1st. He heard Hinata's "Shitsurei shimasu!" at a classroom door, much too loud. He heard somebody in the room call, "Azumane! Some 1st years here for you!" He heard Sugawara's voice, surprised and warm, coming over to make introductions.

Then he heard a voice he didn't know. Low and soft and a little careful, the voice of somebody big who'd spent years trying to take up less room.

He caught pieces.

"...the Little Giant, on TV, at Nationals, and he was small like me, and he..." That was Hinata, much too fast.

Something low from the soft voice, too low to catch.

"...so I want to be the ace! Like him! And everybody says you're the ace, so I wanted to see..."

"Hinata," said Sugawara, somewhere in there, the way you'd put a hand on somebody's arm.

Then a pause, much longer than it needed to be.

Then the soft voice again, and that part Sho heard clearly.

"I'm not the ace."

A chair scraped. Footsteps, going away down the corridor in the other direction, fast for somebody that big.

Sho finished his meat bun. He folded the paper sleeve in half and in half again and put it in his pocket.

Hinata and Kageyama came back down 5 minutes later. Hinata's face had gone very still, the way it never was. Kageyama was frowning.

"He said he's not the ace," said Hinata.

"I heard."

"He just. Left."

"I heard that too."

"He had his hands in his pockets," said Kageyama. "The whole time. Even when Hinata bowed." He frowned harder, at the stairs. "Hitters don't do that."

Hinata looked at the floor. Then his chin came up.

"Then I'll be the ace," he said.

Sho looked at him. All 163 centimeters of him, standing on the landing with his fists closed.

"Okay," said Sho.

Hinata blinked. "You're not going to say I'm too short?"

"You know you're short."

Hinata opened his mouth, and shut it, and looked at Kageyama. Kageyama shrugged.

"Go and eat something," said Sho. "You'll fall over in 5th period."

They went down. Sho stayed on the landing.

He had the 2nd meat bun in his bag. He'd been saving it for before practice. He left it there and stayed where he was, with his arms on the railing, and watched the stairs.

10 minutes before the bell, somebody came down them.

He was big. That was the 1st thing. Taller than Sho by a little, and much wider, with long dark hair tied back at his neck and a shadow of stubble on his chin, so that he looked older than any of them, like somebody's uncle who'd come to pick them up. He came down with his shoulders hunched in and his eyes on his feet, holding a carton of juice he hadn't opened.

He saw Sho on the landing and stopped.

Sho watched him take it in. The color of Sho's indoor shoes, which said 1st year. Sho's face. And then a small flinch, as if Sho might also be about to tell him what he was.

Sho took the meat bun out of his bag and held it out.

"Bought 1 too many," he said.

The big 3rd year looked at the meat bun. Then at Sho.

"...Sorry," he said, and took it.

Sho gave him the small bow you give a senpai on a staircase, and walked down the stairs ahead of him to the 1st-year floor, and didn't look back to see whether he'd eaten it.

---

Sugawara told them the rest that afternoon.

It was before practice, while the others were stretching. Hinata had gone to him the moment he walked into the gym and asked, and Sugawara had looked at him, and then at the door, and then sat down on the edge of the stage with a ball in his lap, and the 1st years had sat down on the floor in front of him without being told to. Even Tsukishima, a little apart, with his back against the wall.

"Asahi's the tallest of us," Sugawara said. "And the strongest. You've seen him. So last year he became the ace without anybody really deciding it. When things got bad, the ball went to him. When we couldn't score, he was the 1 who had to." He turned the ball over in his hands. "And every team we played knew that. So every block we played went straight to him."

"That's what an ace is," said Hinata.

"Mm," said Sugawara. "Then we played Date Tech in the prelims. Date Tech have a block people call the Iron Wall. 3 big guys who all go up together, and they go wherever the ace goes." He stopped. "Every spike he hit that day, they blocked. Every 1. I don't think he got a single ball past them."

The gym had gone quiet around them. Behind them, Tanaka had stopped stretching.

"After that," said Sugawara, "I think he started to hate it a bit. Volleyball. Being the 1 who has to. He and Nishinoya had a fight about it. Nishinoya said he should keep asking for the ball. Asahi said something like, what's the point, if it just comes back down. And then he stopped coming."

"Because of 1 match?" said Hinata.

"...It was a lot of balls, Hinata," said Sugawara.

Kageyama had been sitting with his arms folded the whole time. He frowned.

"If the ace gets blocked every time," he said, "that's the setter's fault too."

Sho was looking at Sugawara's hands.

They'd been moving on the ball the whole time, the thumbs going over the panels, round and round, the way a setter's thumbs go before a set. When Kageyama spoke, they stopped.

"...Yeah," said Sugawara. "It is."

He said it lightly, and smiled at Kageyama, an ordinary smile, and stood up off the stage.

"Warm-up," he said. "Come on. Daichi's looking."

Kageyama went. Hinata went. Tsukishima pushed himself off the wall and went.

Sho went last. He looked back once. Sugawara had put the ball down on the stage and was standing beside it with his hands empty, looking at nothing, and then Daichi called his name and he turned and went.

In the warm-up lines Hinata ended up next to Sho, jogging in place, with his face still screwed up.

"Kiyose," he said. "If you got blocked every time. Every single ball. Would you come back?"

Sho thought about it properly, because Hinata had asked it properly.

"I'd want the next 1," he said. "Mostly so I could see what they did."

"Me too," said Hinata at once, and then, after a few more steps on the spot: "...I think."

Then it was Hinata's turn in the line, and he ran.

At the receive drill, Nishinoya came and stood next to Sho in the line while Daichi climbed back up on his chair.

"You went up there," Nishinoya said. "At lunch. Hinata said."

"I stayed on the landing, senpai."

"Did you see him?"

"On the stairs. After."

Nishinoya was looking at the ball cart. "And?"

"I gave him a meat bun."

Nishinoya turned his head and looked at him. "Did he eat it?"

"I didn't stay to see."

Nishinoya thought about that. Then he nodded.

"He'll have eaten it," he said. "He eats when he's sad. He eats everything." He bounced on his toes, once, twice, and dropped into *shu*. "Get down. Chair. You're up next. And if you stand up on me again I'm telling Daichi-san."

Sho got down onto the invisible chair. His legs were better than Thursday. He held it for 6.

---

On Friday, after practice, Sho went into Sakanoshita on his own with the 40 yen from yesterday in his pocket, and found Takeda-sensei at the register, bowing.

A deep bow, all the way down, with a stack of papers held against his chest.

"Please," Takeda was saying. "Just once. Just come and look at them."

"No," said the blond man behind the counter.

"Ukai-kun, I'm asking you as..."

"Every day this week, sensei." The man turned a page of his magazine. "Go home."

Sho stopped in the doorway.

*Ukai.*

He knew that name. Kageyama had said it on Wednesday night, on the walk home, with a popsicle stick in his hand. The famous old coach. The 1 who'd taken Karasuno to Nationals. The 1 who'd collapsed before Kageyama got here.

The blond man at the register, who gave him the biggest bun every time and let 40 yen go without a word, was named Ukai.

Takeda straightened up too fast, and the papers slid out of his arms and fanned out across the floor between the shelves.

Sho was crouched down and gathering them before he'd thought about it. Practice schedules. A list of gym times. A sheet with *Karasuno High School Boys' Volleyball Club* across the top in Takeda's neat handwriting, and under it a list of names, 3rd years, 2nd years, 1st years. His own name was the last 1. Somebody had written it in carefully and then gone over it again, as if they'd wanted to be sure of the characters.

He squared the edges against his knee and handed the stack back.

"Kiyose-kun." Takeda had gone pink. "Ah. Thank you."

The man behind the counter looked at Sho.

"That 1 tries to get my buns for 100," he said to Takeda.

"He's 1 of ours," said Takeda.

"Tch."

Sho put 120 yen on the counter, exactly, and the man took a bun out of the steamer, the biggest 1 in it, the way he always did, and Sho said "Thank you," and went out after Takeda.

---

On the pavement outside, Takeda stood looking back through the glass at the shop.

"6th time tomorrow," he said, half to himself.

"Sensei," said Sho. "Why him?"

Takeda looked at him. Then he looked back at the shop.

"He played here," he said. "At Karasuno. He was a setter. And his grandfather was our coach, back when we were strong. The 1 they still talk about." He held the papers a little tighter. "Everybody I ask says the same thing. If anybody in this town knows how to coach Karasuno, it's somebody named Ukai."

"Does he want to?"

"I don't know," said Takeda. "He says no very quickly. Sometimes people say no quickly because they've thought about it a lot."

"The Nekoma match," said Sho. "Is that for him?"

Takeda looked at him, and then he laughed.

"Is it that obvious?"

"Your face, sensei. When you told us."

"Ah." Takeda touched his own cheek. "Nekomata-sensei, Nekoma's old coach, has gone back to coaching them. He and Ukai-kun's grandfather were rivals for 30 years." He smiled at the shop window. "If anything brings an Ukai into that gym, I think it'll be that. And if it doesn't, I'll come back tomorrow."

He pushed his glasses up.

"I read about volleyball every night, Kiyose-kun, and I still don't know it. I don't know what a good receive looks like, or why Kageyama-kun's sets are special. I only know they are, because everybody's face changes when he sets." He looked down at the papers. "I don't know volleyball. You all deserve someone who does."

Sho stood on the pavement with his meat bun going cold in his hand.

Satoshi had worked 9 years under coaches who knew volleyball. He tried to think of 1 of them who'd have bowed to a shop counter 5 times in a week, for a sport that wasn't his.

"Thank you, sensei," he said.

Takeda looked surprised. "For what?"

Sho didn't have an answer that wouldn't sound strange, so he bowed. Takeda bowed back, a small 1, with the papers held to his chest, and walked off toward the station.

Sho stayed where he was, with the 40 yen in his hand now, and turned back to the door to pay it.

Through the glass, the man behind the counter had put down his magazine. He'd bent down behind the register, out of sight, and come up again with a sheet of paper in his hand, 1 that had gone under the counter when the stack fell, that Sho had missed. It had a table on it in ruler lines, days across the top. Takeda's Golden Week sheet.

Ukai held it up to the light from the drinks fridge and read it, top to bottom, slowly, with the unlit cigarette in the corner of his mouth. At the bottom, he stopped. Whatever was written in the last box, he read it twice.

Then he folded the sheet in half, and put it in the pocket of his apron, and picked his magazine back up, and stared at the same page, and left it open there.

Sho put the 40 yen back in his pocket. He'd pay it on Monday. He went home the long way, past the school, where the gym lights were off and the shutters were down, and every window was dark.

Asahi about 185 cm. Invented: the exact wording throughout; the Golden Week sheet Ukai keeps; the rumor about Asahi's age.

And if you yell at him, he cries."; "the softest guy in the club"; stayed late to hit at Tanaka when Tanaka was a 1st year). **Sho: "What's the set he likes?" Tanaka: "The bad 1. When everything's gone wrong... he used to shout for those. Suga-san's name. From the back of the court."** (ch 14 delivers the words; arc 3's "the bad ball is the ace's ball"). Hinata: "Was he cool?" "He was SO cool."

Kageyama: "He had his hands in his pockets. The whole time... Hitters don't do that." Hinata: "Then I'll be the ace." "Okay." "You're not going to say I'm too short?" "You know you're short."**

Suga's thumbs stop: "...Yeah. It is."** (Ch 12's confession must go further: the relief when Kageyama came, "one more time"; it must not repeat "my fault".) Suga left with empty hands by the stage.

Sho had squared some papers."; the meat bun was **3** days ago, not 4; Suga's confession must go past "my fault".

## Chapter 12: 40 yen

Sunday was his rest day, and he rested, which was harder than training. He lay on the floor of his room with his legs up the wall and read the notebook from the 1st page to the last, twice, and on Monday morning at 4:15 his legs came down the stairs like legs again.

Toss and catch. 11 in a row, and then the 12th went behind his head.

He wrote *11* on his hand, and put 40 yen in the pocket of his track pants with his house key, and went up the hill.

Tanaka was sitting on the gym step with the key on its red string round his finger, yawning, in the gray light.

"Early," said Sho.

"EARLIER THAN YOU."

They went in. Hinata and Kageyama came at 5:10, arguing about something to do with milk, and the 4 of them had the gym till 7. Tanaka spent most of it hitting cross. Sho spent most of it passing Kageyama's serves, and the coins in his pocket clinked every time he got down into *shu*.

"What's that noise?" said Tanaka, at 6:30, with his hands on his knees.

"40 yen, senpai."

"Why are you carrying 40 yen?"

"I owe it to somebody."

Tanaka looked at him as if he were very strange, and then let it go, because there were more important things.

"Takeda-sensei went to the shop again on Saturday," he said, to the floor. "Narita saw him. 6th time."

"What happened?"

"Narita said he came out smiling." Tanaka straightened up. "Sensei always comes out smiling. That's what's scary about him."

Then he hit 1 more cross, and at 7 the door opened and Daichi came in with the others for morning practice, and looked at the 4 of them, already soaked through, and said, "Mop that before sensei sees it."

---

On Monday, after school, Takeda-sensei walked into the gym with Ukai behind him.

Ukai was wearing a black tracksuit instead of his shop apron, and his hair was held back with the headband, and he had the unlit cigarette behind his ear. He stopped just inside the door and looked round the gym, at the net, the ball cart with its bad wheel, the banner over the stage, the old scoreboard with the numbers that had to be turned by hand, as if he were checking that it was all still where he'd left it.

"THE SHOP GUY?!" said Hinata.

"Everyone," said Takeda, glowing. "This is Ukai Keishin-kun. From today, he'll be coaching us."

At the back, Kageyama had taken a step forward.

"Ukai," he said. "Like... Coach Ukai?"

"My grandfather," said Ukai.

Kageyama stared at him. He'd been buying milk across that man's counter for 2 weeks. Then he bowed, deeper than he'd bowed to anybody since Sho had known him.

"Don't," said Ukai. "I run a shop." He looked away from Kageyama, at the net. "I'm here until the Nekoma match."

"Until the Nekoma match," Takeda agreed, still glowing.

"After that, we'll see." Ukai put his hand in the pocket of his tracksuit, and Sho saw the corner of a folded sheet of paper there, ruled lines showing through the fold, before Ukai's hand covered it. "Nekomata's coming out for this. The old cat himself. I'm not having Nekoma come all the way up here and play a team nobody's coached." He looked at them. "Well?"

"ONEGAISHIMASU!" The whole team bowed.

Kageyama straightened up slowly. He was still looking at Ukai, at the headband and the tracksuit and the cigarette, the way you'd look at a photograph of somebody you'd only heard about.

Ukai walked past Sho toward the bench. He didn't stop.

"You owe me 40 yen," he said, going past.

"Thursday," said Sho. He had his hand in his pocket already. "I have it, Coach."

"Put it away." Ukai dropped his bag on the bench. "It's on your tab."

Sho stood there with the coins in his hand. Tanaka, beside him, looked from Sho to Ukai, and back, and opened his mouth.

"Receive," said Ukai, and clapped once. "Line up. All of you."

---

For the 1st half hour, Ukai hardly spoke.

He stood on a chair on the far side of the net, the way Daichi did, and hit balls at the receive line, hard and flat and not always where you expected, and he watched where every 1 went. He hit at Daichi, and Daichi passed it, and Ukai hit the next. He hit at Tanaka. He hit at Hinata, and Hinata's elbows bent, and the ball went into the wall.

"Elbows," said Ukai.

"Yes!"

He hit at Hinata again. The ball went into the wall again.

"Elbows," said Ukai, in exactly the same voice.

"YES!"

Nishinoya was in the line too, between Tanaka and Yamaguchi, and every ball Ukai hit at him came off his arms and went to the setter's spot as if it had been posted there. After the 3rd, Ukai stopped and looked at him properly, with the ball in his hand, and then hit the next 1 harder, and the next harder than that, and Nishinoya took both of them and grinned up at him.

"Hm," said Ukai.

He hit at Yamaguchi. Yamaguchi's 1st went off the side of his arm and into the bleachers. His 2nd went up, high and wobbly, near enough. Ukai's eyes stayed on him for the ball after that, too, and Yamaguchi passed that 1 as well, and looked at the floor afterward as if he'd been caught at something.

Every so often Ukai got down off the chair and went over to Takeda on the bench and said something short, and Takeda wrote it down with his tongue between his teeth. Sho, waiting in the line, caught some of them.

"Tsukishima. Arms are fine. Doesn't want it."

"The small 1. Hinata. Hopeless. Fast, though. Put down fast."

"Tanaka. Good. Loud."

Then it was Sho's turn.

Ukai hit at him, a hard flat ball at the left hip. Sho's feet went before his head had finished, the way Nishinoya had been shouting at him to do for 4 days, and they went a little wrong and he fixed it on the way, and he sat back on the invisible chair and gave the ball a short shove. It went up a meter short of the setter's spot.

Ukai hit the next 1 to his right. That 1 went into the net.

The 3rd went up again.

Ukai got down off the chair.

"Oi. Tall 1st year."

"Yes, Coach."

"You move before I've hit it."

"Yes, Coach."

"Who taught you to cheat like that?"

"Nishinoya-senpai."

Ukai looked down the line at Nishinoya, who said "CHAIR!" at Sho from 3 places along, helpfully. Then Ukai looked back at Sho.

"Good," he said. "Keep cheating. You're guessing wrong half the time. Guess wrong faster."

He went back up on the chair.

---

After the receive, Ukai had them do approaches and hit, Kageyama setting, to see who they were.

Kageyama set Hinata a quick.

Hinata was already in the air, eyes shut, before Kageyama's hands had touched the ball, and the ball came out flat and fast and arrived at his palm at the top of his swing, and went into the floor on the other side before Tsukishima, blocking, had left the ground.

Ukai's arms came uncrossed.

"What," he said, to nobody. "What the hell was that."

"A quick," said Kageyama.

"I know it's a quick." Ukai had taken a step onto the court. "Do it again."

They did it again. It went in again. Ukai watched it with his mouth a little open and the cigarette hanging off his lip, and for 2 balls he looked about 10 years old.

Sho stood at the back of the hitting line and watched Ukai watch it. That 1 belonged to Hinata and Kageyama. He waited for his turn.

On the bench, Takeda had his notebook open and his pen up. "Ukai-kun. What's special about it? I can see that it is. Everybody's face does that thing. But I don't know why."

Ukai kept his eyes on the court.

"Normal quick, the hitter runs in, and the setter watches him and puts the ball where he's going to be," he said. "That's hard enough. That 1, the kid's in the air before the setter's even touched it. Eyes shut. He swings at a place in the air." He jerked his chin at Kageyama. "And that 1 puts the ball in the place. Exactly. Every time. If he's off by the width of a hand, the kid hits nothing, or gets it in the face." He stopped. "I've never seen anybody do that."

Takeda wrote it all down. Then he looked up.

"So it's trust," he said.

Ukai made a face as if the word tasted bad. "It's a stupid kid and a scary kid," he said. "Write that."

Takeda wrote that down too.

When his turn came, Kageyama set Sho on the left, high, and Sho went. The approach was fine. The jump was what it was, a tall boy's quick shallow jump, better rested than it had been at Seijoh. He hit cross, flat, into the floor inside the far line, and came down on his left foot alone with the knee giving inward, and caught himself.

*Both feet.* He'd done it all through Seijoh, on purpose, every ball. 2 days off, and a coach watching, and his legs had forgotten.

"Oi."

Ukai was looking straight at him.

"Your left foot," Ukai said. "On your 1st step. Why are you babying it?"

The gym kept going around them. Hinata was still talking. Somebody's shoe squeaked on the far court. Sho couldn't hear any of it.

Ennoshita had told him on the bus. *When you're nervous about a ball coming to you, your left foot turns out.* Sho had written it down as nerves.

At 20, in Osaka, the left foot had set down soft on the 1st step, every approach, every morning, for 2 months. He'd thought nobody could see.

"Old injury," Sho said. "From a past life."

Ukai looked at him the way he looked at customers who tried to pay in 1-yen coins.

"Tch. Smart-ass." He jerked his chin at the line. "Fix it. And you land like a bag of rice. On 1 leg. You've got 2. Use both. Every time."

"Yes, Coach."

Sho went back to the end of the line. He took his next approach with his left foot pointed straight, and it felt wrong, like writing with the wrong hand, and he landed on both feet and it cost him half a beat, and Kageyama's next set was already on its way to somebody else. On the 4th approach the foot felt a little less wrong.

---

Before the water break, Ukai sent Takeda to the club room for a roll of masking tape, stood on the chair by the stage door, and ran a long strip of it up the wall from about 2 meters to well past 3. He gave Takeda a marker.

"Everybody," he said. "Standing reach, then jump. Touch as high as you can. Sensei writes it down."

They lined up. Tsukishima touched the tape flat-footed higher than anybody, and jumped without seeming to try, and Takeda wrote 2 numbers next to his name. Daichi jumped. Tanaka jumped and slapped the wall so hard the strip came away at the bottom and Takeda had to press it back.

Hinata stood under it and touched the tape at the very bottom, standing, and somebody laughed. Then he backed up 3 steps and ran at the wall and went up, and kept going up, and slapped the tape higher than Daichi had, higher than Tanaka, and came down grinning.

Ukai looked at the mark with his arms folded and his mouth pulled down at 1 corner.

Sho went next. Standing, he touched higher than Hinata by most of a forearm. He backed up and ran in, left foot straight, and jumped, and touched.

It was a fine mark. Above Tanaka's. A little below Hinata's.

"Again," said Ukai. "5 times. No rest. Same spot."

Sho jumped again, and again. By the 3rd he could feel it. By the 5th he was reaching for the tape and his fingers came down on it a hand's width below his 1st mark, and he landed on both feet and felt it all the way up his thighs.

Takeda wrote both down, the 1st and the 5th, and looked at them, and looked at Ukai.

Ukai looked at the 2 marks on the tape, a hand apart.

"What'd you play before this?" he said.

"Soccer. Swimming. Kendo, for 4 months."

"And since?"

"Nothing, Coach."

"Shows." Ukai looked at Sho's legs, the way he'd look at a delivery that had come short. "Your legs are a kid's who sat in a chair for 3 years. That gets fixed with work. Months of it. You won't have it by Nekoma." He turned away. "Hinata. Again. 5 times."

Hinata jumped 5 times. The 5th was about as high as the 1st. He came down bouncing.

Sho looked at the 2 marks with his name next to them, 1 above the other. On Tuesday night he'd written *Ask somebody who knows legs* at the bottom of a page, because there hadn't been anybody.

He went after Ukai.

"Coach."

Ukai turned round with an expression that said he'd been hoping not to be followed.

"What do I do?" said Sho. "Starting today."

Ukai looked at him. Then he looked at the tape, and at Hinata still bouncing under it, and at the clock.

"Stairs," he said. "You live up a hill?"

"Near the school, Coach."

"Then you live up a hill. Walk it. Every day. Up, down, up. Slow on the way down. That's 1." He held up a 2nd finger. "When you land, I want to hear both feet. Every time. In here, in the street, getting off a train. That's 2. And you sleep." He looked at Sho's face, as if he could see the 4:15 on it. "That's what you get till I've seen you play. Ask me again Wednesday."

"Yes, Coach. Thank you."

"Don't thank me. Go drink something."

At the water break, Sho wrote it all under Ennoshita's line: *Coach saw it too. 1st step. Straight. Land on 2.* And under that, the 2 numbers from the tape, and the gap between them, and *Hill. Slow down. Both feet. Sleep. Wednesday.*

---

Hinata appeared at his elbow before he'd closed the notebook.

"Kiyose! Tanaka-senpai said you wrote ACE on your form!"

"I did."

"I'M going to be the ace!"

"I know. You told me on the stairs."

"So you can't!"

"I'd still like to," said Sho.

Hinata stared at him with his mouth open, trying to work out whether that was allowed.

"Both of you," said Kageyama, from the water cooler, "are idiots."

Nishinoya had been half-listening from the stage. He hopped down. He'd come to practice every day since his return, and every day he'd said he wasn't playing in matches, and every day he'd stayed until the lights went off.

"A 1st year writing ACE?" he said, grinning up at Sho. "I like your guts!"

Then the grin went, and his voice went flat.

"But Karasuno's ace is Asahi-san."

Sho looked down at him. He thought about the stairwell, and the juice carton, and the hands in the pockets.

"Then we'll both have to fight him for it," he said. "Hinata and me. When he's back."

Nishinoya looked up at him with his jaw tight.

"...When he's back," he said, and went back to the stage.

---

Ukai called them in a little after 5.

"I need to see you play a real game," he said. "So tonight, 6:30, you're playing a match."

"Against who?" said Daichi.

"My team. The Neighborhood Association. Karasuno old boys, most of them. Guys who've been at work all day." He looked at his phone. "I've got 4 coming. I need a setter, another spiker, and a libero."

He looked at the team. "You've got 2 setters. They can't be on the same side. 1 of you comes with me."

Kageyama and Sugawara looked at each other.

"I'll go," said Sugawara.

He said it lightly. Ukai nodded, already looking at his phone again, and Sugawara walked round the net post to the other side.

Kageyama stood where he was. Then he went after him.

Sho was at the cart, sorting balls by how flat they were, close enough to the net post to hear, and he didn't move away. The gym had gone quiet in the way it did before a game, people stretching, nobody talking much.

"Is it because of me?" Kageyama said. It came out low and stiff. "That you're going over there."

"No. Well." Sugawara's voice was low too, but it carried. "...After Date Tech. Every set I gave him, they were waiting. And when you came, Kageyama. When I saw you set." He stopped. "Part of me was relieved. That I wouldn't have to be the 1 who sent it up to him any more. Relieved, Kageyama. That's a terrible thing for a setter to feel."

Sho put a ball in the cart, and another.

"But I want to set to him again," said Sugawara. "Just 1 more time. I want to see him hit 1. That's why I'm going over there."

There was a pause.

Kageyama bowed, a short, stiff bow, and walked away from the net post with his face shut, toward the far wall.

Sugawara turned round.

Sho was the closest person to him. He had a ball in each hand. He saw Sugawara see him, and see what he'd heard, and then remember Wednesday.

"You noticed, didn't you," Sugawara said quietly. "Wednesday. The 3rd high 1. When I held it."

"I didn't tell anybody."

"...Why?"

Sho looked at the ball in his left hand.

"It was your set, senpai," he said.

Sugawara laughed. It was small, and a little wet. Then he reached out and patted Sho on the back, too hard, twice.

"Go warm up, Kiyose," he said, and went to the other side of the net, where Ukai's team would be.

---

While Ukai was in the corridor on the phone finding out who was actually coming, Sho found himself at the stage steps next to Yamaguchi, who was stretching his hamstrings very thoroughly and looking at the court.

"Do you think he'll put us on?" Yamaguchi said. "Tonight. Any of us. He's only seen us for 2 hours."

"He watched you in the receive line," said Sho. "After you got the 2nd 1 up. He kept watching for the next 1."

Yamaguchi stopped stretching. "He didn't even talk to me."

"He talks to the people who are bad at something, mostly. He talked to me 3 times."

Yamaguchi went pink round the freckles and looked at the floor, and then he bent down and touched his toes again, harder, and Sho saw him look across at Tsukishima, who was on the other side of the gym with his headphones round his neck, looking at the ceiling.

"Don't tell Tsukki I asked," said Yamaguchi.

"I won't."

---

The Neighborhood Association arrived at 6:20, in their work clothes, and went straight into the club room to change, loud enough to hear through the door.

Nishinoya was standing by the gym door with his bag on his shoulder.

Ukai came back in from the corridor and turned to the door.

"You. The libero. You're with us."

"I'm not playing matches," said Nishinoya.

"It's not a match. It's a game with my friends from the shopping street."

"I'm not..."

"Just tonight."

Nishinoya looked at the court, at the net, at the club-room door with the old boys' voices behind it. His hand was tight on his bag strap.

"We're still 1 short," said Ukai, counting on his fingers. "I need a spiker. Anybody."

Hinata, who'd been staring out of the high window by the door for no reason at all, pointed and yelled.

"ASAHI-SAN!"

Everyone turned.

He was standing outside in the dusk, on the path beside the gym, huge and hunched and caught, holding a school bag in both hands in front of him. He must have been watching through the open door. Sho had no idea for how long.

He took a step back.

All Ukai could see was a very tall boy in a Karasuno uniform, and a team that was 1 short.

"You!" Ukai said. "Tall guy! You play?"

"I... I used to, I..."

"Great. Get changed. You're with us."

"I can't, I'm not..."

"We're short a player. Get in here."

The big 3rd year stood at the door, and nobody in the gym moved.

Sho looked at Nishinoya. Nishinoya was staring at Asahi with his jaw locked and his whole body pointed at him, and his hand on the bag strap had gone white at the knuckles.

Then Asahi came in.

He came in slowly, like somebody walking into cold water. Daichi met him halfway across the floor with a spare practice shirt from the bag by the stage and held it out without a word, and Asahi took it, and looked at it, and went toward the club room with it in both hands. He passed Sho on the way. Sho, who had given him a meat bun on a staircase 3 days ago. Asahi's eyes went to him and away.

When he came back out, the borrowed shirt was too tight across his shoulders. He went to the far side of the net, where Ukai's team was, and stood there, large and still, while Sugawara came over and put a hand on his arm and said something nobody else could hear.

Nishinoya put his bag down.

He walked onto the court and took his place in the back row of the Neighborhood side, right behind Asahi. He kept his eyes on the net in front of Asahi and stood low and ready behind him, the way he stood behind every ball he'd ever received.

On the Karasuno side, Kageyama was flexing his fingers. Hinata was bouncing on his toes, staring through the net at Asahi with his whole face. Daichi had gone to stand beside Ukai at the sideline, and Ukai was writing names on a clipboard with a stub of pencil, and Daichi was nodding.

Sho stood by the Karasuno bench and looked across the net at the ace who'd stopped asking for the ball.

If he was on the court tonight, he was going to block him as hard as he could. Every ball.

Ukai's pencil stopped. His eyes went along the Karasuno side, and stopped, and he tapped the pencil against his teeth.

**Continuity (ch 12)**

- Revised Nov 22 (revision pass of ch 1 to 34). Old ch 10, scenes 5 to 9 (Option B split): Sunday (rest day) and Monday, 4:15 a.m. to the start of the Neighborhood match at 6:30 p.m. No status card (cards retired Nov 22).
- Canon: Ukai Keishin agrees to coach "until the Nekoma match" (Nekomata's return is the lure; canon order kept); Hinata recognizes the shop guy; Ukai is old Coach Ukai's grandson (Kageyama came to Karasuno for the grandfather); Ukai's 1st practice; his awe at the quick ("What the hell was that"); the match that evening against his Neighborhood Association (4 Karasuno alumni after work); Suga goes to Ukai's side to set and tells Kageyama he was relieved not to have to set for Asahi after Date Tech, and wants to set to him again; Ukai recruits Noya as libero ("a game with my friends from the shopping street"); Hinata spots Asahi through the window; Ukai pulls him in as "tall guy". Unverified: 6:30; the exact wording.
- Sunday rest (legs up the wall, the notebook twice). Monday 4:15: toss-and-catch best run **11**. Dawn with Tanaka ("Early." "EARLIER THAN YOU."), Hinata and Kageyama at 5:10; the 40 yen clinks in his track pants ("I owe it to somebody."); Tanaka: Takeda's 6th visit on Saturday, "Sensei always comes out smiling. That's what's scary about him."; Daichi at 7: "Mop that before sensei sees it."
- Ukai arrives (black tracksuit, headband, cigarette behind the ear); Hinata: "THE SHOP GUY?!"; **Kageyama: "Ukai. Like... Coach Ukai?" "My grandfather." The deep bow. "Don't. I run a shop."**; "Until the Nekoma match... After that, we'll see."; the Golden Week sheet in his tracksuit pocket (ch 11); "Nekomata's coming out for this. The old cat himself."
- **The tab: "You owe me 40 yen." "Thursday. I have it, Coach." "Put it away. It's on your tab."** (the 40 yen stays owed; a quiet Ukai-Sho thread).
- Receive (Ukai on the chair): "Elbows." x2 to Hinata; Noya in the line, Ukai hits him harder ("Hm."); **Yamaguchi: 1 into the bleachers, the next up, Ukai watches the 3rd too**; notes to Takeda ("Tsukishima. Arms are fine. Doesn't want it." / "Hinata. Hopeless. Fast, though. Put down fast." / "Tanaka. Good. Loud."); Sho: 1 up short of the setter's spot, 1 into the net, 1 up; **"Who taught you to cheat like that?" "Nishinoya-senpai." "Good. Keep cheating. You're guessing wrong half the time. Guess wrong faster."**
- The quick; **Ukai to Takeda: "...the kid's in the air before the setter's even touched it. Eyes shut... If he's off by the width of a hand, the kid hits nothing, or gets it in the face... I've never seen anybody do that." "So it's trust." "It's a stupid kid and a scary kid. Write that."**
- **The left foot:** Sho lands on 1 foot (2 days off, he'd done both feet all through Seijoh); **"Your left foot. On your 1st step. Why are you babying it?"** (Satoshi at 20: the left foot set down soft on the 1st step for 2 months; never explained) **"Old injury. From a past life." "Tch. Smart-ass. Fix it. And you land like a bag of rice. On 1 leg. You've got 2. Use both. Every time."** Retraining: straight foot "like writing with the wrong hand", better by the 4th.
- **The reach test (masking tape by the stage door, 2 m to past 3 m; Takeda writes numbers, never printed):** Tsukishima highest standing; Tanaka; Hinata lowest standing, jumps above Daichi and Tanaka; Sho's standing reach above Hinata's by most of a forearm, his 1st jump above Tanaka's, a little below Hinata's; **5 in a row: Sho's 5th a hand below his 1st; Hinata's 5th about the same as his 1st.** "What'd you play before this?" "Soccer. Swimming. Kendo, for 4 months." "And since?" "Nothing, Coach." **"Your legs are a kid's who sat in a chair for 3 years. That gets fixed with work. Months of it. You won't have it by Nekoma."**
- **Sho asks (ch 9: "Ask somebody who knows legs"): "What do I do? Starting today." Ukai's program: walk the hill every day, up, down, up, slow on the way down; land on both feet every time, everywhere; sleep. "Ask me again Wednesday."** Later chapters must show this work and the Wednesday follow-up. Notebook: *Coach saw it too. 1st step. Straight. Land on 2.* / the 2 tape numbers and the gap / *Hill. Slow down. Both feet. Sleep. Wednesday.*
- Water break: "Kiyose! Tanaka-senpai said you wrote ACE on your form!" "I did." "I'M going to be the ace!" "I know. You told me on the stairs." "So you can't!" "I'd still like to." Kageyama: "Both of you are idiots." Noya: "A 1st year writing ACE? I like your guts!... But Karasuno's ace is Asahi-san." **Sho: "Then we'll both have to fight him for it. Hinata and me. When he's back." Noya: "...When he's back."** (Replaces the ledger's "Forms are a manifesto" / "Then I'll be the opposite. Literally.": Sho plays the wing, Nov 13; "190 on the form" is gone too.)

I want to see him hit 1."** Kageyama's short stiff bow. **Suga to Sho: "You noticed, didn't you. Wednesday. The 3rd high 1. When I held it." "I didn't tell anybody." "...Why?" "It was your set, senpai."** The wet laugh; 2 pats, too hard.

It's a game with my friends from the shopping street." "Just tonight." Hinata: "ASAHI-SAN!" Ukai: "You! Tall guy! You play?" **Daichi brings Asahi a spare practice shirt; it's too tight across the shoulders (ch 13).** Asahi passes Sho (the meat bun 3 days ago). Suga's hand on his arm. **Noya puts his bag down and stands behind Asahi.**
- Hook: Daichi with Ukai at the clipboard; *If he was on the court tonight, he was going to block him as hard as he could. Every ball.* Ukai's pencil stops on the Karasuno side. (Ch 13: Yamaguchi crossed off, Sho on the LEFT wing; Sho's reason "Because if I go easy, he'll know." stays ch 13's.)
- No eye events.

- Next: Ch 13 "Neighborhood".

## Chapter 13: Neighborhood

Ukai wrote the Karasuno lineup standing at the sideline with the clipboard against his chest, fast, the way you'd write a shopping list.

Then he looked at it, and crossed 1 name out with a line of pencil, and wrote another over it.

"Yamaguchi, you sit," he said. "Kiyose, you're on. Left side."

Yamaguchi was standing right next to Sho when he said it. He'd been stretching his arms across his chest. He stopped, and his face did a small quick thing, and then it was over, and he nodded.

"Okay," he said.

He looked across the gym at Tsukishima, who was already on the court with his back to them. Then he went to the bench and sat down next to Ennoshita, and took his jacket off the back of the bench and spread it over his knees, and smoothed it flat with both hands. Then he looked up at Sho, who hadn't moved.

"Do your best, Kiyose," said Yamaguchi.

Sho stood there with nothing to say back. He bowed to him, and turned to go out onto the court.

On the bench, Ennoshita had his towel on his knees again and his bottle at his feet. He looked up at Sho as Sho went past.

"Left side," he said.

"Yes, senpai."

"Mm." Ennoshita looked at the far side of the net, at Asahi in the too-tight shirt. "Warm your hands up. He hits heavy."

---

The Neighborhood Association had changed in the club room, and they came out looking like what they were: men who'd worked all day.

1 was thin, with glasses and black hair, and still wearing a polo shirt with SHIMADA MART stitched on the chest. 1 had dyed blond hair and an easy grin. 1 was quiet and broad through the shoulders. A 4th was stretching against the wall with a groan.

"Kei-chan!" said the blond 1, to Ukai. "Look at you! You look like a coach!"

Ukai looked at the ceiling.

"I did inventory all day," the 1 in the polo told the gym. "So go easy on me, kids."

"Shimada-san," Tanaka whispered to the 1st years. "And that's Takinoue-san. And Uchizawa-san. They were on the team. Back when."

Sho did the sum. Somewhere around 26, most of them. A couple of years younger than Satoshi had been at his desk at 3:14 in the morning.

*Kids,* he thought, and nearly called the 1 in the polo Shimada-kun out loud.

Sugawara stood on their side of the net in a borrowed bib with his hands loose at his sides, flexing his fingers. Asahi stood in the back row in the borrowed practice shirt, and looked at the floor. Nishinoya stood beside him, low and ready.

"Onegaishimasu!"

---

The 4th alumnus served 1st, a float that came in flat and dropped toward Tanaka. Tanaka passed it. Kageyama came running in from the back row and set Sho on the left, high and wide.

Sho's legs had been through 2 hours of Ukai already, and a reach test. He went up into what they had. It was enough to see over the net. He hit a hard cross, flat, at the deep corner, the best ball he had.

Nishinoya was already there. *Thup.* The ball went up soft and floated down to Sugawara.

"Too honest, Kiyose!" Nishinoya yelled, grinning.

Sugawara got under the ball. His hands were still at the top for half a beat, and then Takinoue came running in for a quick, and Sugawara put the ball right where he needed it, soft and fast, and Takinoue hit it into the floor between Tsukishima and Hinata.

0-1.

Hinata stared across the net. "Suga-san can do THAT?"

"He's a real setter," said Daichi, from the net.

On the other side, Nishinoya slapped Sugawara on the back. "Suga-san! That was cool!"

Sugawara laughed. Sho, walking back to his spot, saw him look at the back of Asahi's head, and away.

The next rally went to them too, and Sho spent it watching Nishinoya.

The 4th alumnus served again. Daichi passed. Kageyama set Daichi on the right this time, and Daichi hit a hard cross, and Nishinoya was moving before Daichi's hand came through, 1 long step to his left, and then a little shuffle back half a step when the ball came off the hand straighter than he'd guessed. The ball came off his forearms up to Sugawara. Sugawara set Takinoue's quick again, and Sho went up beside Tsukishima to block it, and his fingers only got to the top of the tape. The ball went off his fingertips and down into the gap behind Hinata.

0-2.

Sho landed on both feet and looked at his hands. At 4 o'clock that afternoon, those fingertips would have been over the net.

He'd seen it twice now, though. Nishinoya's step, and then the fix.

Nishinoya moved 1st. That was the whole of what he'd taught Sho on Wednesday: you don't wait to be sure, you just go. So when a hitter's arm came back, Nishinoya read it and took 1 big step toward where the ball should go, and corrected on the way.

Which meant that for a fraction of a beat, the short middle he'd just left was empty.

At 0-2, Tanaka passed again, and Kageyama set Sho on the left again, the same high ball. Sho went up and swung his whole arm through as if he were going to hit the same hard cross. He watched Nishinoya's weight go. At the last instant he turned his wrist over and rolled the ball, soft and short, over the block and into the space Nishinoya had just stepped out of.

Nishinoya twisted and lunged and missed it by a hand.

1-2.

Nishinoya lay on the floor. Then he slapped the boards with both palms.

"WHO taught you THAT?"

He sat up, and pointed at Sho, and stopped with his finger in the air.

"...ME?!" he said. "I taught you that! That's MY step!"

"Yes, senpai."

Nishinoya laughed, a huge laugh that went round the roof, and got up, and went back to his spot beside Asahi. The next time he got ready, Sho saw, his 1st step was a little smaller.

---

Tsukishima served the next 1 into the middle, and the Neighborhood side passed it, and Takinoue sided out off the block. 1-3.

At 1-3, with Uchizawa serving, Kageyama set Sho on the left again, and Sho tried it again. The big swing, the late turn of the wrist, the soft roll into the short middle.

Nishinoya's step had got smaller. He took it, and stopped, and was right there under the roll with his arms out, and the ball went up off them, and he was laughing before it reached Sugawara.

"NOT TWICE!"

Sugawara set Takinoue, and Takinoue put it down. 1-4.

Uchizawa served again, and Sho got another ball. Daichi passed, Kageyama set him on the left, and across the net Shimada and Sugawara came up to block him, Shimada on the outside, late, with his hands held stiff like a man who hadn't blocked anybody in a few years. His outside hand was turned in toward the court.

Sho had swung at a lot of hands in 2 lives. He hit at that 1. The ball went off the outside of Shimada's fingers and away past the antenna into the wall.

2-4.

"OW," said Shimada, shaking his hand. "Kids these days."

Kageyama walked past Sho on the way back to his spot. "That set was for the line."

"His outside hand was turned in," said Sho.

Kageyama frowned across the net at Shimada's hands, as if they'd done something rude.

"...Hn," he said.

---

At 2-4, Sho served. It was a plain float that Shimada passed with a grunt, and Sugawara set Takinoue, and Daichi dug Takinoue's hit up off the floor, and Kageyama got under it at the net.

Hinata was already in the air. Eyes shut. The ball came out of Kageyama's hands flat and fast and met his palm, and went into the floor between Takinoue's feet before Takinoue had straightened his knees.

3-4.

The gym went quiet in the good way.

Takinoue looked at the floor. Then at Shimada.

"...Is that allowed?"

"I don't think there's a rule against it," said Shimada slowly.

On the sideline, Ukai had uncrossed his arms again.

In the back row on the Neighborhood side, Asahi was watching Hinata. He watched him land, and whoop, and spin round to Kageyama with both fists up. He watched for so long that Nishinoya had to say his name twice to get him back into position.

Sho served again, and the Neighborhood side sided out on a tip from Takinoue that dropped behind Hinata. 3-5.

Sugawara went back to serve. Asahi came round to the front row on the left.

Sugawara served at Sho on purpose. Sho saw the decision in the toss. A float, nothing special, aimed at the seam between Sho and Daichi. A week ago it would have gone off his arm into the stage.

*Shu.* He was down on the invisible chair before the ball had crossed the net, guessing, a little wrong. A half step left to fix it. *Don.* A short shove, arms still, and the ball went up clean and dropped to Kageyama.

At the edge of his eye, Sho saw Sugawara's right hand come up off his thigh, a couple of centimeters, almost a wave, and go down again.

Kageyama set Hinata. Nishinoya dug the quick, somehow, flat on the floor, and the ball went up, and Sugawara ran in from the end line and got under it and set Asahi.

It was a good set. High and outside on the left, where an ace wants it. Asahi came in on his approach, and he was big, and he was fast, and he was beautiful to watch for 3 steps.

On the 4th, he slowed down.

It was tiny. A hitch on the last step, the speed going out of his legs as if he'd changed his mind halfway. His arm came up short. And at the top, his hand opened before it got to the ball.

Sho had watched a hitch like that on tape 1,000 times. He knew what came out of the end of it before Asahi's hand touched the ball.

"TIP! SHORT!" he called, loud enough for the whole gym.

Asahi tipped. Daichi was already moving. He scooped it up a meter behind the block, and Kageyama ran under it and set Hinata again, and this time Hinata's quick went into the floor before anybody on the other side had moved.

4-5.

On the sideline, Ukai said, "Oi. Kiyose."

"Yes, Coach."

"How'd you know that?"

"His last step slowed down," said Sho. "And his arm was short. And his hand opened early."

Ukai looked at him. Then he looked across the net at Asahi, and kept looking at him, with his arms folded and the cigarette still behind his ear.

---

The ball from Hinata's quick had rolled to the net post on the other side. Sho went to get it.

Asahi got there 1st. He bent down and picked it up, and straightened, and they were standing a meter apart with the net between them, and Asahi was holding the ball.

"...You called it," Asahi said.

"Yes, senpai."

Asahi's eyes went to Sho's face, and down to the ball, and back to Sho's face. He turned the ball over once in his big hands.

"Um," he said. "Sorry. It was a... bad 1."

He held it out under the net. Sho took it. He couldn't think of anything to say that wasn't worse than nothing, so he bowed, a little, with the ball against his chest.

"Asahi," said Sugawara, from the end line. "Your spot."

Asahi went.

---

Daichi was still walking back to serve when Tanaka came back from the net and took Sho by the front of the shirt, hard enough to mean it.

"You," he said, low. "You called his tip. Out loud. In front of everybody."

"Yes, senpai."

"Why'd you do that?"

Sho looked down at him.

"Because if I go easy," he said, "he'll know."

"Know what?"

"That we think he's going to break." Sho stopped. It sounded worse out loud. "That we're all being careful with him. He'd know. If we let it through, he'd know why."

Tanaka held onto his shirt. His jaw was working.

"He's my senpai," he said. "He stayed late for me. Every day. When I was nothing."

"I know."

"You don't know anything. You've been here 2 weeks."

"Yes, senpai."

Tanaka looked at him a beat longer. He let go of the shirt, and smoothed the front of it flat with 1 hand, once, rough.

"...Then block him harder," Tanaka said. "Damn it."

Daichi had stopped at the end line with the ball. He'd heard all of it.

"Kiyose," he said. "Next time you see it, call it."

Tanaka turned his head. "Daichi-san..."

Daichi bounced the ball, once, and looked at the net.

Hinata had drifted over from the net. He looked at Tanaka's back, and at Sho, with his eyebrows pulled together.

"Kiyose," he said. "Are you being mean to Asahi-san?"

"I don't know," said Sho. It was true.

Hinata thought about that. Then he looked across the net at Asahi, the way he'd looked at him on the stairs, as if Asahi were still the ace he'd come to see.

"I want to see him hit 1," said Hinata. "A real 1."

"Me too."

Tanaka's jaw worked. He turned round, toward the net, toward the other side, and filled his lungs.

"ASAHI-SAN! KEEP ASKING FOR THE BALL! YOU'RE THE ACE!"

On the far side, Asahi flinched as if he'd been hit. Daichi, at the end line, let Tanaka shout.

---

Daichi served, and Asahi got another set, and tipped again. This time Tsukishima called it, from the back row, quiet and bored, a half-beat before Sho opened his mouth.

"Tip."

Daichi dug it, and Kageyama set Tanaka, and Tanaka hit it into the floor so hard it bounced up into the balcony rail.

5-5.

As they reset, Tsukishima looked sideways at Sho.

"You didn't have to yell it," he said. "The 1st 1."

"...Yes," said Sho.

Tsukishima looked at him a beat longer than he seemed to mean to. Then he pushed his glasses up and turned back to the net.

Daichi served again, and the Neighborhood side sided out on a hit from Takinoue. 5-6.

Between rallies, while Shimada was retying his shoe, Sho saw Asahi turn round to Nishinoya.

They were too far away to hear. Sho could see Asahi's mouth move, only a little. He could see Nishinoya listening, and Nishinoya's face going from flat to the face he had on before a ball he meant to get.

Nishinoya said something back. Even shorter.

Then he slapped Asahi on the back, hard, and went back to his spot.

Asahi turned round to face the net. He looked straight across it, and for the 1st time all game he kept his eyes up. He looked along Karasuno's front row, and past it, at Sho in the back, and held it.

---

Tanaka sided out with a cross into the corner. 6-6. Kageyama served 1 into the net, 6-7, and the Neighborhood side was serving.

Sho was in the back row for it, on the left, and he got down onto Nishinoya's invisible chair, and his thighs burned holding it. Shimada's float came to him. He passed it. It went up, ugly, toward the net.

Kageyama set Hinata. This time, across the net, somebody jumped with him.

It was Asahi. He was in the front row now, and he'd been watching Hinata all night, and he came across from the right late and big with his arms out, and the ball went off the tips of his fingers and out of bounds before he'd got anywhere near it properly.

7-7.

"NICE RECEIVE, KIYOSE!" came from the bench.

It was Yamaguchi. He was sitting forward with his jacket still over his knees and both hands cupped round his mouth, and when Sho looked over he went pink and put his hands down. Ennoshita, beside him, kept his eyes on the court and was very nearly smiling.

Hinata had come down shouting. "He jumped! Did you see? Asahi-san JUMPED! He touched it!"

"He touched it and it went out," said Tsukishima. "That's our point."

"He JUMPED, though!"

On the far side, Asahi was looking across at Hinata. Hinata pointed at him with both hands, grinning, as if Asahi had done something wonderful. Asahi went red to the ears and turned away.

---

The rotation brought Hinata to the end line, and Sho to the front row on the left, and Tsukishima to the middle, and Tanaka to the right.

Sho's legs had a few good jumps left in them. He could feel about how many, the way you can feel how much is left in a bottle by the weight. 2, he thought. 3, if 1 of them was small.

Tanaka looked along the net at him, past Tsukishima. He put up his hands, both of them, flat, the way you show somebody what you're going to do with them, and turned back.

Hinata served.

It was a bad serve. It clipped the top of the net, wobbled, and dropped over, and Shimada had to lunge for it from the back row. His pass went sideways, way off the net. Sugawara, in the back, couldn't get to it. Uchizawa, the quiet 1, got under it at the net and shoved it up, high and loopy and out toward their left side, a desperate ball with nothing behind it but hope.

It was the kind of set you give the ace when everything else has gone wrong.

Asahi went for it.

He was coming in on Karasuno's right, in front of Tanaka. Sho was on the left, at the far end of the net. The ball was so high that he had time, almost. He was already moving, running along the net toward Tsukishima, watching Asahi's approach the whole way.

Asahi came in hard on all 4 steps, and the last 1 was the fastest. His arm came back big, all the way, and his hand disappeared behind his head.

"BLOCK!" Tanaka roared, and went up on the line.

Tsukishima closed from the middle and went up beside him. Sho's last step came down short and a beat late, his legs a little behind where his head had told them to be, and he went up a beat after Tsukishima, on Tsukishima's left, with the best jump he had left. His hands got there anyway. He'd read it early, and his arms were long, and he drove them up and over the net the way he'd been taught, elbows in, thumbs up, shoulders to his ears, reaching over onto Asahi's side.

6 hands. Asahi swung into them.

The ball hit Sho's hands like a door slammed on them. It drove his fingers back. The sting went up both arms to the elbows. And the ball went straight down, hard, back onto the Neighborhood side, almost as fast as it had come.

Sho came down on both feet with his hands burning, his eyes on Asahi's face a meter away through the net. Asahi's face had gone white.

Sho kept his own face still. His knees were already bending for the next 1, because the ball wasn't dead until it hit the floor, and it hadn't hit the floor. Nishinoya had dived.

Sho saw him at the edge of his eye, a small dark shape flying flat across the boards behind Asahi with 1 arm out and his whole body stretched. He'd started moving before Asahi swung. His fist got under the ball a hand's width above the floor, and the ball went up, high and ugly, and up.

Nishinoya lifted his head off the boards, still flat on his chest, and screamed it, with everything he had, at the big 3rd year standing frozen at the net.

"ASAHI-SAN! CALL FOR IT AGAIN!"

The ball was coming down. Sugawara had run in from the back and was under it, his hands up, and Sho saw them go still at the top. The same pause. The same hold as the 3rd high ball on Wednesday.

Sugawara was about to set somebody else.

**Continuity (ch 13)**

- Canon: the Neighborhood Association after work (Shimada of Shimada Mart, Takinoue, Uchizawa, a 4th alumnus; "Kei-chan"); Suga sets for them; Suga's quick to Takinoue for the 1st point; Daichi: "He's a real setter."; Noya: "Suga-san! That was cool!"; the 1st freak quick stuns the alumni ("...Is that allowed?" "I don't think there's a rule against it."); Asahi watching Hinata; Asahi's hesitant attacks (tips); Tanaka shouting at Asahi to keep asking for the ball; Asahi and Noya talking at the net (unheard; canon: Asahi says he still wants to hit, Noya that he'll keep the ball alive); Hinata's net-cord serve; Shimada's bad receive; Uchizawa's desperate set; Asahi's full swing; the triple block; Noya's save and "CALL FOR IT AGAIN!"; Suga's hands pause (canon: he starts to set someone else). Canon's triple block was Kageyama, Tanaka, Tsukishima; ours is Tanaka, Tsukishima, Sho (ledger). Ages: Shimada and Takinoue 26, Uchizawa 28 (Sho guesses "around 26").
- Divergence: Ukai crosses Yamaguchi off for Sho, "Left side." Yamaguchi: "Okay." / "Do your best, Kiyose." (smooths his jacket; looks at Tsukishima 1st). Ennoshita: "Left side." "Yes, senpai." "Mm. Warm your hands up. He hits heavy." Sho on the LEFT wing (Nov 13). Ukai's thrown-together order puts Tanaka and Sho together in the front row only once round (Hinata's serve).
- Karasuno order (pickup): Kageyama, Hinata, Tanaka, Tsukishima, Sho, Daichi. Neighborhood order: the 4th alumnus, Uchizawa, Suga, Takinoue, Shimada, Asahi (Asahi front row only from Suga's serve on; Shimada back row at the climax; Suga back row at the climax). Neighborhood serves 1st; Karasuno receives with Tanaka at P1.
- Flow: 0-1 Noya digs Sho's cross ("Too honest, Kiyose!"), Suga's quick to Takinoue; 0-2 Daichi's cross dug (Noya's step and fix), Takinoue's quick off Sho's fingertips at the top of the tape (**his legs: "At 4 o'clock that afternoon, those fingertips would have been over the net."**); 1-2 Sho's roll shot into the short middle Noya stepped out of ("WHO taught you THAT?... ME?! I taught you that! That's MY step!"; Noya's step gets smaller); Tsukishima serves, 1-3 Takinoue; 1-4 Sho tries the roll again, Noya digs it: "NOT TWICE!"; 2-4 Sho tools Shimada's turned-in outside hand ("OW. Kids these days."; Kageyama: "That set was for the line." "His outside hand was turned in." "...Hn."); Sho serves, 3-4 Hinata's 1st quick of the night; 3-5 Takinoue tip; Suga serves at Sho on purpose, the *shu/don* pass, Suga's hand lifts 2 cm; Hinata's quick dug by Noya, Suga runs in and sets Asahi; the hitch; **"TIP! SHORT!" (loud)**; Daichi digs; Hinata, 4-5.

"He's my senpai. He stayed late for me. Every day. When I was nothing." "I know." "You don't know anything. You've been here 2 weeks." "...Then block him harder. Damn it." Daichi: "Kiyose. Next time you see it, call it." Tanaka: "Daichi-san..." (no answer). Hinata: "Are you being mean to Asahi-san?" "I don't know." "I want to see him hit 1. A real 1." "Me too." Tanaka: "ASAHI-SAN! KEEP ASKING FOR THE BALL! YOU'RE THE ACE!"
- Daichi serves: Asahi's 2nd tip, Tsukishima calls it from the back row ("Tip."), Tanaka into the floor, 5-5. **Tsukishima: "You didn't have to yell it. The 1st 1." "...Yes."** 5-6 Takinoue. CALL FOR IT AGAIN!"; **ends on Suga's hands holding at the top, about to set somebody else.**

ONE MORE!" KEEP-WORDING).
